- Phase 8B WO-1 root-cause diagnosis (architect-ordered, confirmed live via a real Playwright script, not assumed): the "second-query failure" is `sceneStore.activeScene`'s single-slot design. It's not a timing bug — the store has exactly one slot, and `submitQuery`'s trail-completion callback calls `setActiveScene(scene)` unconditionally, so turn 2's resolution *always* overwrites turn 1's scene with no memory of it. Reproduced directly: after a second, unrelated query resolves, turn 1's scene content (`"Candidates above 6%"`) is confirmed gone from the DOM (`hasOldCandidatesText: false`) — not stale, not corrupted, simply never retained. `trailPlayer.ts`'s generation counter is unaffected and continues to work correctly (verified no console/page errors across both turns) — the counter protects against a *stale in-flight player* overwriting fresh state, which is a different failure mode than "the previous turn's *already-settled* state has nowhere to live." This is exactly what WO-1's turn+artifact model fixes: past turns' scenes move from a single mutable slot into `artifactStore`'s keyed map, so nothing is destroyed on the next resolution.

Three-turn sequence verified live: all three turns' bubbles remain in the log, each turn's own scene correctly shown only while open, no stale state, zero console/page errors. The stack is global, not Home-local — it renders beside whichever page is active, since "pages never host scene renders" means a page that wants to show an investigation result always routes to the stack, never inline.
  **Store consolidation, a real simplification enabled by "Artifacts is the only pane"**: `workbenchStore.ts`'s multi-pane array (kind/open/width per pane, plus a cross-store `artifactStore.subscribe` hack to force-reopen on a new id) is gone — `artifactStore.ts` now owns `isStackOpen`/`stackWidth` directly, and `setOpenArtifact` itself both opens the stack and marks the artifact viewed, inline, no subscription needed. Also folded in during this same edit: `setOpenArtifact` now calls `sceneStore.setActiveScene` itself (previously every call site — Sidebar, HistoryPane, Transcript's ArtifactCard — had to remember to call both, the exact "two stores drift" class of bug Phase 8B WO-1's own root cause was); `Transcript.tsx`'s `ArtifactCard.onOpen` simplified to the one call now that the pairing is centralized.
  **Real bug found and fixed live, not by inspection**: the first `NotificationBell` implementation marked an artifact "viewed" the instant `setOpenArtifact` fired — but `autoOpen` calls exactly that on every new artifact, including alerts, so a pushed alert marked itself "seen" before the user could ever see it, and the bell's badge silently never appeared. Fixed with a second store action, `openArtifactSilently` (same effect, doesn't touch `viewedArtifactIds`), used only by `presentScene.ts`'s autoOpen path for Monitor-module scenes specifically — every other opener (a typed query, an Investigate action, a stack/Sidebar/Investigations row click) is real human intent and marks viewed normally. Verified live both ways: badge appears and persists through the automatic pop-open, then clears once the user explicitly clicks the alert's own row.

- **Artifact-stack polish (post-8H feedback) — DONE as of 2026-07-12.** Not a numbered phase — direct architect feedback on the WO-1 stack UI, applied the same session. `ArtifactStack.tsx`'s toolbar gained a close IconButton (calls the same `toggleStack` action the top-bar control uses — one on/off state, two entry points, not a second mechanism) and a maximize/restore toggle (new `artifactStore.isMaximized`, reset on close so reopening never surprise-maximizes). `ArtifactStackControl.tsx` now always renders — `isDisabled` at zero artifacts instead of returning null, so the control's position is stable and its own disabled state communicates "nothing yet" — and its count moved from a same-row sibling Badge to a proper overlapping corner badge.
  **Maximize layout**: standard IDE-panel "maximize" pattern (VS Code et al., per the order's own "whatever the best practice is") — the stack's Card takes 100% width, its resize handle hides (nothing to drag against a hidden sibling), and Frame's left content column (Home or whichever page) goes `display:none` rather than unmounting, so an in-progress composer draft survives a maximize/restore round trip.
  **Two real bugs found live, not by inspection, both fixed same-session**: (1) the maximize icon needed a diagonal-expand glyph that doesn't exist in Astryx's closed `IconName` set (exhibit 9, below) — resolved by rotating the real `arrowsUpDown` icon 45° via `Icon`'s own SVG-prop passthrough rather than inventing custom chrome. (2) the count badge, once actually screenshotted rather than trusted from `boundingBox()`/`isVisible()` alone, rendered as a near-invisible dark smudge overlapping the icon button — "neutral" variant's background had too little contrast against the button surface beneath it. Fixed with Badge's "info" variant plus a `--surface-0` ring to visually separate the badge from what's under it; re-verified legible in all three themes via direct screenshot review, not just a passing assertion.
  Gate: all three tsc projects clean, `pnpm --filter app test` 24/24, `pnpm lint:tokens` clean, `pnpm exec playwright test` 82/82 (9 `shell.spec.ts` panel-open baselines force-regenerated for the new toolbar buttons and badge treatment — confirmed genuinely rewritten via mtime, not a stale pass; `shell-landing` correctly untouched). Live-verified in one real Playwright script: close button closes the stack exactly like the top-bar control (and vice versa, confirmed both directions); the control is visible-but-disabled before any artifact exists mid-trail, not hidden; maximize hides the composer and grows the stack toolbar past 1200px wide, restore returns both to normal; the badge overlap sits correctly at the icon's corner. Zero console/page errors.
- **Phase 8I (Market Pulse, surfaced vs investigated split) — CLOSED as of 2026-07-12.** Legislated and ordered in one message, queued explicitly ahead of the still-open Phase 8 tokens-only punch-list ("queue: punch list → 8I → 10") as the one architect-named exception to Sonnet otherwise being parked pending that punch-list. Single work order, small and self-contained, per the order.
  **Nav + data**: new `universe/marketPulse.json` (4 cards, keyed, each `{id, heading, body, intent, module, featured}`) — the three original LandingState suggestions promoted verbatim (`featured: true`) plus one new card reusing `asset-discovery-refine`'s own real intent (`featured: false`, Market-Pulse-only). Only 4 of the ordered "4-6" range: the fixture universe has exactly four resolvable typed intents total (asset-discovery, issuer-dossier, settlement-anomaly, asset-discovery-refine) — a fifth/sixth card would have meant either reusing an already-used intent (redundant) or authoring a whole new scene fixture, which this small/self-contained WO didn't authorize; flagged rather than padded with a dead card. `pageStore.ts`'s `PAGES` gained `'market-pulse'`. `Sidebar.tsx`'s nav reordered to Entities → Watchlist → Data Sources → Market Pulse → Investigations (Investigations moved from first to last so Market Pulse could sit literally between Data Sources and it, per the order's exact positioning).
  **Register split, node-vocabulary.md's Shell section gained the law verbatim**: "Surfaced (Market Pulse: agent-authored, no evidence, no trail) vs Investigated (has run the reasoning loop, has confidence) are different registers and never share a row style." Implemented as a real structural difference, not a badge afterthought: surfaced cards are heading (UI face) + body (`--face-voice`, the same register text-block/recommendation already own) in a plain vertical stack; investigated cards gain a leading document icon and a green "Investigated" `StatusTag` (`ArtifactCard`'s own visual language) in a horizontal header row, body drops to plain UI/data-face text. LandingState's 3 featured chips carry the same surfaced treatment, since they're the same underlying data.
  **Click semantics**: a card's own `intent` matched live against `sessionStore.turns[].utterance` (exact string equality — the same value passed to `submitQuery` on click, no separate correlation id needed) determines investigated state. A surfaced card click calls the existing `submitQuery` path unchanged; an already-investigated card's click instead calls `setOpenArtifact` on the matched artifact — opens it rather than re-asking the same question and minting a duplicate turn. A card never leaves Market Pulse once surfaced, investigated or not — "it's a record of what was surfaced."
  **Consider-not-build note, logged not built, per the order's explicit instruction**: an alert not yet opened is also "surfaced, not investigated" — Market Pulse could in principle unify with the Monitor push (an alert appears here first, the notification-bell badge/Investigation status only flips on open). This is a real, coherent architectural idea for a *later* phase (Phase 9-adjacent, since it touches `liveChannel.ts`/`presentScene.ts`'s alert path) — the alert path itself is hardened, gated, and explicitly out of scope for this WO; nothing in `presentScene.ts`, `liveChannel.ts`, `NotificationBell.tsx`, or `openArtifactSilently` was touched.
  Gate: all three tsc projects clean, `pnpm --filter app test` 24/24, `pnpm lint:tokens` clean, `pnpm exec playwright test` 82/82 (12 `shell.spec.ts` baselines force-regenerated — the Sidebar nav reorder touches every shell state, not just landing, confirmed genuinely rewritten via mtime). Live-verified in one real Playwright script: LandingState shows exactly 3 featured cards with voice-face body text; clicking one runs the full loop and the turn lands; Market Pulse then shows all 4 cards, the acted-on one carrying the Investigated tag with its body switched OFF the voice face while the still-surfaced cards stay on it; clicking the investigated card again opens its artifact without a duplicate trail; the resulting turn appears in Investigations too; a second, previously-untouched card (the new asset-discovery-refine one) runs its own full loop and investigates independently; nav order confirmed Entities/Watchlist/Data Sources/Market Pulse/Investigations. Zero console/page errors.
  **Bug found and fixed same day, post-close, real report from live use**: clicking a surfaced Market Pulse card while actually viewing the Market Pulse page never navigated to Home — `submitQuery` fired, the turn and trail played, and the artifact eventually auto-opened into the (global) stack, but the user stayed on the card grid the whole time and never saw the transcript or the thinking trail, unlike the identical click from LandingState (which is only ever visible pre-first-turn, i.e. already on what becomes Home). Root cause: `MarketPulsePage.tsx`'s `handleClick` called `submitQuery` but never `pageStore.setPage('home')`. Fixed with one added call, gated to the surfaced (new-investigation) branch only — the already-investigated branch (`setOpenArtifact`) correctly stays put, since the stack is global and visible from Market Pulse without navigating away. Re-verified live: click → immediate navigation to Home → turn, live trail, and artifact stack all visible exactly as a LandingState click produces. `pnpm exec playwright test` 82/82, no baselines needed (pure behavior fix, no new visual state).
  **Real environment issue found and root-caused during this gate, not a Merlin defect**: the first two `test:visual` runs failed identically on the same 8 early-alphabetical node stories (scenegrid/panel/metric) with `#storybook-root` staying hidden past the 30s timeout — reproduced twice, ruled out as flaky. Root cause confirmed via Playwright's own config (`fullyParallel: true`, `webServer.reuseExistingServer: true`): a freshly-spawned `storybook dev` process passes its HTTP health check before Vite's dependency pre-bundler has finished its first real pass, so the earliest-scheduled parallel workers race a server that's technically "up" but not yet actually able to serve a story. Fixed by pre-warming the server with real Playwright page loads (not curl — curl doesn't execute the client JS that triggers bundling) before running the suite; confirmed 82/82 clean against a warm server, twice.
- **Sidebar Recent-list bug fix (post-8I live-use report) — DONE as of 2026-07-12.** Direct architect report from live use, not a numbered phase. `Sidebar.tsx`'s `recent` selector filtered on `turn.artifactRef` — a new investigation was invisible in Recent for its entire trail-playing duration (addTurn fires immediately; artifactRef only lands once the trail settles), reading as "the click did nothing." Fixed: `recent` now shows the last `config.recent.count` turns unconditionally; each row's `StatusDot` reflects real state — neutral "No match" (unresolved), accent "Thinking…" with `isPulsing` (resolved, trail still playing, no artifact yet — a legitimate use of motion-as-progress, not decoration), success "Answered" (settled). Rows without an artifact yet are `isDisabled` (nothing to open) rather than wired to a no-op click.
  **Test-script correction found while verifying, not a product bug**: `StatusDot` renders `role="img"` with an `aria-label`, not visible text (confirmed via its own source, not guessed) — a first verification pass asserting on visible "Thinking…"/"No match" text failed for the right reason (the assertion was wrong, checking for text that was never going to render), corrected to `getByRole('img', {name})`.

- **Unified workbench title bar + in-place investigation (direct order, 2026-07-29).** One shared `PaneTitleBar.tsx` component (not three independently hand-tuned bars from `HomeTopBar`/`PageShell`, kept matched only by convention and their own comments admitting the duplication) runs full-row width above every page, mirroring Home's already-existing "runs the full row width, sibling of the row below" placement, now generalized past Home. New `ChatIcon.tsx` / `ChatPaneControl.tsx` (same "reveal/close" pattern `ArtifactStackControl` already established, new glyph pulled from `DocumentIcon.tsx`'s own gap-fill precedent when Astryx's closed `IconName` set lacks it — verified via `astryx docs icons`). New `WorkbenchTitleBar.tsx` composes the bar per-page: Home keeps Artifacts control only; every other page (Discover/Watchlist/Investigations/etc. + Entity Detail) adds a Chat toggle contextually. Entity Detail's prior "Investigate" button (fired one authored intent string sight-unseen) is replaced by the Chat toggle, letting users type a real, freeform query while staying on the page — a genuinely new capability, not previously possible. Cold-start chat (opening chat before any thread has started, `!hasStarted`) required new pane-visibility state: `isChatForcedOpen` (user clicked the toggle) and `isChatManuallyClosed` (user manually closed it again), both kept separate from `collapsedPane` (the width-squeeze mechanism) to avoid fighting `usePaneFitCollapse`'s own "re-widened, clear it" rule. Architecture: pane-visibility logic extracted to new `paneVisibilitySlice.ts` (Zustand slice), composed into `artifactStore` to keep state in one place per Phase 8H's "one home for pane-shaped state, not two" precedent. Routing law in node-vocabulary.md amended: "Unified workbench title bar… Right side is contextual… Chat toggle…" + the new Entity Detail law restating "Browse first, investigate second… its title bar's Chat toggle… replaces… canned-intent Investigate… opening chat invites a real, typed question." Files: new `PaneTitleBar.tsx`/`.module.css`, `ChatIcon.tsx`, `ChatPaneControl.tsx`, `WorkbenchTitleBar.tsx`, `paneVisibilitySlice.ts`; deleted `HomeTopBar.tsx`; modified `Frame.tsx` (swap conditional, generalize `naturalShowTranscript`), `PageShell.tsx` (drop title/headerActions, content-only wrapper), `EntityDetailPage.tsx` (drop headerActions block + Investigate button), 7 place pages (drop `title` prop from PageShell call), `artifactStore.ts` (import + compose the slice), `DashboardPage.tsx` + 2 callers (drop `title` prop). File budget: `artifactStore.ts` was 207 lines (7 over cap), so pane-visibility state was split to `paneVisibilitySlice.ts` (71 lines) → `artifactStore.ts` (151 lines), both under cap. Gate: all three tsc green, vitest 33/33, lint:tokens clean, visual regression: 129→122→122 baselines (full delete-regenerate cycle per established tolerance-vs-change rule; confirmed two consecutive clean runs with zero rewrite spikes). Live-verified via Storybook Frame story in default theme: Home title bar byte-identical to prior `HomeTopBar` (regression check pre-touching place pages), Discover page shows title bar with both new toggles, chat toggle on cold Discover page opens empty transcript pane (Transcript renders null with zero turns, safe pre-emptively mounting it is verified). Raw learnings: pane-visibility logic initially reused `collapsedPane` flag for "user closed it," but this fought `usePaneFitCollapse`'s own effect that re-runs on every `collapsedPane` change and immediately un-collapses the instant width permits (its core job: "fits again, clear it") — can't tell "user closed" from "no longer squeezed" so was clearing the wrong field immediately; separate `isChatManuallyClosed` flag per `usePaneFitCollapse`'s own comment and confirmed live, second flag works correctly.
  **Follow-up correction, same day**: the just-created "Thinking" row shipped `isDisabled` — architect feedback: it should read `isSelected` immediately (the "you are here" signal an answered turn's own selection already gives) and must not be disabled, since a permanent-chrome control that does nothing is exactly what principle 11 rules against. Fixed: `isSelected` now true whenever a turn is thinking (only one can be, by construction — the trail player only ever plays one at a time) OR its artifact is the currently open one; only genuinely-dead unresolved rows stay `isDisabled`. The thinking row's click is real, not inert: it navigates to Home so the user can watch the live trail from wherever they are, same reasoning as the earlier Market Pulse navigation fix. Live-verified via `data-selected="selected"` on the row and a real navigate-away-then-click-back round trip landing on Home with the trail still visibly playing. `pnpm exec playwright test` 82/82, no baselines affected — the transient thinking state never appears in any `shell.spec.ts` screenshot, since every capture there happens after its own trail-settle wait.

- **Root cause, live-diagnosed before any 8E code was written (architect-reported): "Nordkap Pension Trust (12%) missing and a clipped label."** Confirmed real, not a false alarm. Phase 8D's own live verification (STATE.md, Phase 8D entry) checked `concentration-map` at the isolated `Renderer/Fixtures` story's full viewport width (~900px) — never through the actual `Shell/Frame` → `ArtifactPanel` path, which is the real demo route and constrains the treemap's rendered SVG to ~353px (`ArtifactPanel`'s 480px default minus its own `Card`/`Toolbar` padding), dramatically narrower than what was checked. At that real width: Nordkap's cell label ("Nordkap Pension Trust") overflows its cell and gets clipped mid-word by Phase 8D's own clipPath fix — correctly non-overlapping, but with no ellipsis or other indication of truncation; separately, the cell's height falls below the component's `MIN_VALUE_HEIGHT` threshold, so its "12%" value line doesn't render at all. The row isn't structurally absent (confirmed via direct DOM query: all 4 labels + values exist as `<text>` nodes) but reads as missing to a viewer, since its one headline figure never appears and its name is silently truncated. Same class of gap as the Phase 7 AspectRatio lesson (merlin-new-node §3: "a node's first real in-scene render is a fresh visual review at scene scale") — one level deeper: verifying at *scene* scale (the Fixtures story) still isn't verifying at *artifact-panel* scale, since the panel imposes its own further width constraint beyond the scene-grid column. Fix folded into 8E's own treemap rework (d3-hierarchy squarify + explicit label-fit rules + ellipsize-with-title-attr + an all-rows invariant test) rather than patched standalone, per the architect's instruction.

- **Sidebar/Investigations "reopen navigates home" bug fix — DONE as of 2026-07-12.** Direct architect report from live use, not a numbered phase: reopening an existing investigation from Sidebar's Recent list or the Investigations page, while on any non-Home page, only swapped the (global) artifact stack's content and left the user stranded on that page — the row is a link to the investigation (turn, transcript, trail), not just to its artifact. Same bug class, same fix shape, as the earlier Market Pulse navigation fix. Architect's message explicitly hard-stopped scope at this: no per-investigation isolated chat-history rework, which would be the "big architectural rework" required to let a user's own bespoke follow-up messages diverge per-investigation — out of scope, not attempted.
  Fixed in both call sites: `Sidebar.tsx`'s Recent row (artifact-ref branch) now calls `setPage('home')` before `setOpenArtifact(artifactRef)`. `InvestigationsPage.tsx` gained one helper, `openInvestigation(artifactRef)` (`setPage('home')` + `setOpenArtifact(artifactRef)`), used by both the module-grouped view's `ListItem` and the flat view's `ListItem` — one helper, not duplicated per view. Deliberately untouched: Entities/Watchlist's "Investigate" buttons (starting a NEW investigation) stay on their current page, per the earlier, separate ruling that the artifact stack being global makes that acceptable — this fix only touches REOPEN paths for EXISTING artifacts.
  Test-script correction found while verifying, not a product bug: `SegmentedControlItem` renders as `role="radio"`, not `role="button"` (confirmed via source, not guessed) — a verification script's `getByRole('button', {name:'All'})` for switching Investigations into flat view was corrected to `getByRole('radio', {name:'All'})`.

Live-verified in one real Playwright script: from Market Pulse, clicking a Recent row for an earlier turn navigates to Home with the correct (first, not most-recently-created) artifact open and its own transcript visible; from Investigations (flat view), clicking a different turn's row navigates to Home with that turn's own artifact open. A follow-up screenshot (Entities page → open an artifact → screenshot) directly confirmed the "it can be both" half of the report: the Entities nav item and the Recent row for the open artifact are simultaneously shown selected — the dual-selection model (one page selected, one Recent row optionally also selected when its artifact is the one open) works as intended, no code change was needed for that part beyond what this fix already does (`isSelected` was already keyed off `openArtifactId`, independent of `page`). Zero console/page errors.

- **Sidebar single-selection invariant fix — DONE as of 2026-07-12.** Direct architect report from a screenshot, after the "reopen navigates home" fix above: "the selected item can be only one... currently, the recent items can be sometimes two selected, and also between nav items and recent, there could be two selected." Two distinct, real root causes, both confirmed live before fixing (not assumed):
  **(a) Two Recent rows both reading "Thinking" at once.** Root cause, confirmed via a dedicated Explore-agent audit of every `submitQuery`/`presentScene` call site first (ChatBar, LandingState, Entities/Watchlist Investigate buttons, Market Pulse cards, Canvas entity-links, `liveChannel.ts`'s MCP push): **no entry point anywhere guards against a second query starting while a previous one is still resolved-but-unsettled** — `trailPlayer.ts`'s own generation counter (Phase 6) protects the *store* from a stale player's callback firing, but does nothing about the *turn* that callback would have settled. That turn is permanently orphaned at `status: "resolved"`, `artifactRef: undefined` — indistinguishable from a genuinely active turn to every consumer checking `isThinking = status === "resolved" && !artifactRef`, so two turns (one live, one stuck forever) both satisfied it simultaneously. Fixed at the root, one place: `sessionStore.ts` gained a third `TurnStatus`, `"interrupted"`, and `addTurn` now transitions any existing turn still `resolved`-without-`artifactRef` to `"interrupted"` in the same update that appends the new one — since every entry point funnels through `addTurn`, this protects all of them uniformly rather than requiring a guard at each call site. `Sidebar.tsx`, `InvestigationsPage.tsx`'s flat-view `statusDot`, and `Transcript.tsx`'s user-bubble tag all gained a genuinely-honest "Interrupted" label/neutral-dot/disabled state for it, rather than silently misreporting it as "Answered" (a real, separately-confirmed latent bug in `InvestigationsPage`'s `statusDot`, which had no branch for a resolved-without-artifactRef row reaching its flat view at all).
  **(b) A main nav item and a Recent row both selected simultaneously.** Root cause: Recent-row `isSelected` checked `artifactRef === openArtifactId` unconditionally, with no dependency on which page is active — and several real flows deliberately open an artifact while staying on a non-Home page (Market Pulse's "reopen an investigated card," Entities/Watchlist's "Investigate" button, both per earlier same-day rulings that this is fine since the artifact stack is global). Each of those correctly left the nav item for the current page selected, but the artifact's own Recent row lit up too. Fixed with a single added condition: Recent-row `isSelected` is now `page === 'home' && (isThinking || artifactRef === openArtifactId)` — selection is exclusively page-gated, so a nav item and a Recent row can never both claim it; the status dot itself (information, not navigation state) stays unconditional and still reflects real progress from any page.
  Gate: all three tsc projects clean, `pnpm --filter app test` 24/24, `pnpm lint:tokens` clean, `pnpm exec playwright test` 82/82, zero baselines regenerated (no existing `shell.spec.ts` capture overlaps two trails or leaves the stack open while navigating away, so this fix's surface isn't covered by any committed baseline). Live-verified in one real Playwright script, both root causes reproduced first and then confirmed fixed: firing a second query ~600ms into the first's ~8s trail leaves exactly one "Thinking" dot and one `Interrupted` dot, and at most one `data-selected` element throughout; navigating to Entities then clicking a same-page Investigate button leaves exactly the Entities nav item selected, never also the newly-opened artifact's Recent row; reopening an already-investigated Market Pulse card from the Market Pulse page leaves exactly the Market Pulse nav item selected; a fresh landing shows exactly "New investigation" selected, and submitting a query from it immediately swaps to exactly the new Recent row selected. Zero console/page errors across all checks.
  Open items: none. Phase 8's tokens-only punch-list pass remains the only open item project-wide.
- **Fixture-content fix: evidence nodes now consistently paneled — DONE as of 2026-07-12.** Direct architect report: "each metric and table, essentially each node, should be in its own pane... in some scenes there is no pane around it, like in others." Traced first, not guessed: no node component (Metric, MetricGrid, Comparison, EntityGraph, GeoPanel, DataTable, TimeSeries, BarSeries, ConcentrationMap, SignalFeed) has its own Card chrome — the visual "pane" boundary comes entirely from wrapping a node in the `panel` layout node, a scene-authoring choice. Across all 5 fixtures, `data-table`/`time-series`/`bar-series`/`concentration-map`/`signal-feed` were consistently wrapped in a titled `panel`; `metric`/`metric-grid`/`comparison`/`entity-graph`/`geo-panel` were consistently NOT wrapped anywhere — floating bare on the canvas. Two scope questions put to the architect before touching any file (AskUserQuestion): (1) which nodes should always pane — confirmed evidence/data nodes only (metric, metric-grid, comparison, entity-graph, geo-panel join the already-paneled set); `text-block`/`recommendation`/`confidence-meter`/`status-tag`/`entity-header`/`filter-summary` stay bare, correctly, since node-vocabulary.md's register-separation principle already treats those as prose/narrative content, not evidence — boxing them would blur that ratified distinction, so this was never a candidate for "fix everything." (2) for a metric-grid's several children, one shared panel per group or one panel per individual metric — confirmed one shared panel per group, matching the existing precedent of a titled panel holding multiple time-series charts as one unit (asset-discovery's "90-day yield vs. volatility" panel already does this).
  **Pure scene-content fix, zero component/contract/registry changes** — all 5 `*.scene.json` fixtures edited to add a wrapping `panel` node around every previously-bare metric-grid (or standalone metric, where no grid exists — issuer-dossier's single Yield metric), `comparison`, `entity-graph`, and `geo-panel` occurrence. For `comparison`/`entity-graph`/`geo-panel`, each node's own standalone `title` prop moved up to the wrapping panel's `title` and removed from the leaf node's own props (both Panel and the leaf component render a `title` heading — leaving it on both would have shown it twice); `EntityGraph`/`GeoPanel`'s own SVG `aria-label` falls back to a generic default ("Entity relationship graph"/"Geographic panel") as a result, accepted as reasonable since the panel's own visible heading is immediately adjacent, not worth the scope of wiring `aria-labelledby` for this fix. Every scene's `reveal` sequence renumbered gapless (verified programmatically per file, not by eye, same discipline as Phase 3's fixture swaps) — the new panel wrapper node takes the reveal slot immediately preceding its child, matching every existing panel's own pattern.
  Gate: all three tsc projects clean, `pnpm --filter app test` 24/24 (no fixture test hardcodes reveal sequences or node counts, confirmed by grep before editing), `pnpm lint:tokens` clean, `pnpm exec playwright test` 82/82 — 15 renderer-fixture baselines + 9 shell baselines force-regenerated with `--update-snapshots=all` specifically (plain `--update-snapshots`, which defaults to mode "changed," genuinely missed real sub-threshold diffs on 3 of the 5 renderer fixtures and silently skipped every shell baseline entirely — caught by mtime verification, not assumed from the passing run alone; re-ran forced and reconfirmed via mtime that all 24 were genuinely rewritten). Two baselines reviewed directly (asset-discovery, settlement-anomaly, both default theme): the metric-grid and comparison table now sit inside a shared bordered panel matching their sibling data panels' treatment; the entity-graph's wallet cluster now has its own titled panel; findings/recommendation text correctly stayed unboxed. A cold-start/parallel-worker race (the same class hit three times earlier this session) reappeared on the first `--update-snapshots` attempt — pre-warmed the specific affected stories via a real headless-browser page load first, then re-ran with `--workers=1`, clean both times after.

- **Artifact stack closes on navigating to a non-investigation place — DONE as of 2026-07-12.** Direct architect request: "the artifact pane should close when navigating to other section in the navigation if this section is not an investigation." Scoped before implementing (stated in the response, not asked, given low reversibility risk): Home is the only "investigation" place — it's where a turn's own transcript/trail actually lives; the routing law already frames every other place uniformly ("pages never host scene renders"), so all five nav places (Entities, Watchlist, Data Sources, Market Pulse, Investigations) close the stack on arrival, no special case for the Investigations index itself.
  Real tension surfaced and deliberately preserved, not silently overridden: Market Pulse's "reopen an investigated card" and Entities/Watchlist's "Investigate" button both intentionally keep the stack open while staying on their current page (ratified earlier this session, "the stack is global") — confirmed via grep that neither flow ever calls `setPage`, so this fix (keyed off page *transitions*) cannot touch them; verified this holds live, not just by code inspection.
  New `artifactStore.closeStack()` — idempotent (`isStackOpen: false`, `isMaximized: false`), distinct from `toggleStack`: a navigation side effect must always land on "closed" regardless of current state, where a toggle could accidentally reopen an already-closed stack. `pageStore.setPage`/`navigateToModule` call it whenever the target page isn't `"home"` — centralized in the store action itself (mirroring `setOpenArtifact`'s own cross-store `setActiveScene` call, Phase 8H's precedent for avoiding the "two stores drift" bug class) rather than duplicated at each of Sidebar's nav-item/View-all/NotificationBell call sites, so a future new navigation entry point inherits the behavior automatically. `openArtifactId`/`artifacts` are untouched — closing hides the pane, it doesn't discard which artifact was open, so returning to Home still shows it.
  Gate: all three tsc projects clean (confirmed no circular import between pageStore and artifactStore), `pnpm --filter app test` 24/24, `pnpm lint:tokens` clean, `pnpm exec playwright test` 82/82, zero baselines regenerated (no existing capture combines an open artifact with a non-Home page, confirmed by the clean run rather than assumed). Live-verified in one real Playwright script: stack closes on navigating to Entities, Watchlist, and the Investigations index; the artifact's content survives the close and reopens correctly via a Recent-row click (confirms "closed," not "discarded"); Market Pulse's reopen-an-investigated-card flow still opens the stack while staying on Market Pulse, unaffected — the regression case explicitly checked, not assumed safe from reasoning alone. Zero console/page errors.
  Open items: none. Phase 8's tokens-only punch-list pass remains the only open item project-wide.
- **List hover state fix, first pass (Watchlist, Data Sources catalog) — DONE as of 2026-07-12, superseded same day by the universal fix below.** Direct architect report: "we should have a hover state for all lists, and it is Watchlist Data Sources." Traced before editing: Astryx's `ListItem` (checked in its own source, not guessed) only enables hover/press styling once the item is genuinely interactive — `onClick`/`href` "automatically enables hover/press styles," there is no separate visual-only hover toggle. Watchlist's rows and Data Sources' public-catalog rows each had exactly ONE well-defined action (Investigate, Connect) living on a nested inner button, with no click handler on the row itself — hence no hover.
  Fixed by promoting each row's single action to the row's own `onClick` and removing the now-redundant nested button (`WatchlistPage.tsx`, `DataSourcesPage.tsx`'s catalog list) — this part of the fix stands, unchanged by the entry below. Two scoping calls were made and stated, not asked: Data Sources' "Connected"/"Connected this session" lists and `EntitiesPage.tsx` were deliberately left untouched, reasoning that they have no single well-defined action (Entities has two — Watch and Investigate — and Astryx's own docs warn against nesting a second interactive element for the same action).
  **This scoping turned out to be narrower than what was actually being asked for** — see the universal-hover entry directly below, which the architect's follow-up ("most don't have, while all should have") corrected same day. Gate at the time: all three tsc projects clean, `pnpm --filter app test` 24/24, `pnpm lint:tokens` clean, `pnpm exec playwright test` 82/82, zero baselines regenerated. Kept here for the record of the reasoning trail — the onClick promotions on Watchlist/Data Sources-catalog remain correct and unchanged.
- **List hover state, universal fix (theme-layer) — DONE as of 2026-07-13.** Follow-up architect report, same thread as the entry above: "Can confirm it's working: Watchlist can see hover. Entities can't see hover. Data sources can see hover, the ones not connected. Can't see hover of investigations... overall I think we didn't achieve the goal. Because most don't have, while all should have." This revealed the first pass's scoping (only give hover to rows with exactly one well-defined action) was narrower than the actual ask: every row in every list, regardless of whether it carries a click action at all.
  **A real, reproducible false alarm chased down and ruled out first, not assumed**: an early verification script reported a hard crash ("Please check the Storybook config") when scanning Entities/Watchlist rows via `page.locator('ul').first()`. Root-caused via direct grep of Storybook's own package assets: that exact string lives in `storybook/assets/server/base-preview-body.html` — static, hidden boilerplate Storybook always includes in the preview iframe's base HTML, sitting before the real app root in document order. An unscoped `.first()` selector picked up Storybook's own placeholder markup instead of app content; scoping every locator to specific row text (the working pattern already used successfully earlier this session) resolved it. Logged so this isn't rediscovered: never use a bare `page.locator('ul'|'li').first()` against this app's Storybook harness — always scope by known text content.
  **Root cause of the real gap, confirmed by reading `Item.tsx`, the primitive `ListItem` composes**: Astryx's own `:hover` treatment (the `interactive` stylex class) is applied conditionally in React, based on `onClick`/`href` being present — there is no prop to request it independent of interactivity, and `isHighlighted` (which does exist on the lower-level `Item` primitive and is exactly "hover/press appearance" as a free-standing boolean) is not exposed through the public `ListItem` API at all, so reaching for it would mean forking past the public component (rule 5). The architecturally correct fix, per List's own documented "Theming" table: every `ListItem` always carries a stable `astryx-list-item` class via `themeProps('list-item')`, regardless of interactivity — Astryx's own sanctioned override hook.
  Fix, one CSS rule, `theme.default.css` only (unconditional/resolves-late, same reasoning already established for the canvas rule in that file — one rule covers all three registered themes since `--color-overlay-hover` is Astryx's own variable, consumed as-is, never bridged/reassigned): `.astryx-list-item:hover:not([aria-selected='true']):not([aria-disabled='true']) { background-color: var(--color-overlay-hover); }`. The two exclusions are load-bearing, not decorative: `[aria-selected='true']` is excluded because Astryx's own `.selected` class (lower CSS specificity than this rule's class+pseudo-class) would otherwise be visually overridden by the new plain hover background on the currently-open investigation's row — caught by reasoning through specificity before shipping, then confirmed live, not discovered as a regression after the fact. `[aria-disabled='true']` documents a guarantee already true via Astryx's own `pointer-events: none` on disabled items (`:hover` can't match a non-hit-testable element) rather than silently depending on it. Logged in `astryx-bridge.md` per the project's standing discipline of documenting every touch of Astryx's theming surface, even non-value ones.
  Gate: all three tsc projects clean, `pnpm --filter app test` 24/24, `pnpm lint:tokens` clean, `pnpm exec playwright test` 82/82, zero baselines regenerated (hover is a transient pseudo-state; no existing capture triggers it). Live-verified in one real Playwright script across all three registered themes (default/ops-dark/glass identically): Entities' two-action NordBond row, Watchlist's no-intent Helios row, and Data Sources' action-less "Connected" row all now show a real background-color change on hover — the exact three gaps the architect's report named; Data Sources' catalog row (existing `onClick`) is unaffected; the Investigations flat-view's currently-selected Aldergate row shows its accent-muted selected background held identical before and during hover, confirming the exclusion works, not just that it compiles. Zero console/page errors across all three theme passes.

- **Sidebar double-selection during the thinking handoff — DONE as of 2026-07-13.** Direct architect report from live use: "I already had one recent item and then went to Market [Pulse] and clicked another item there. Created a new investigation under Recent, but both were selected. One only, the current, should be selected." A third, distinct root cause under the same single-selection invariant closed twice already this session (interrupted-turn thinking, page-gated nav+recent) — confirmed by reading the code before touching it: `Sidebar.tsx`'s Recent-row `isSelected` was `isThinking || (artifactRef === openArtifactId)` — an OR that lets the OLD open turn (still matching `openArtifactId`, which only moves once the NEW turn's trail settles and `autoOpen` claims it) stay selected for the entire ~9s the NEW turn is thinking, while the new thinking row is ALSO selected via the other arm of the OR. Both true simultaneously for the whole handoff window, not a one-frame flicker.
  Fixed with one derived flag, `hasActiveThinkingTurn` (checked against the full `turns` list, not the filtered/sliced `recent`, so a search filter can't hide the thinking turn while its stale sibling's row keeps showing selected) — while any turn is thinking, `openArtifactId`-based selection is suppressed entirely for every row, so only the thinking row can claim the slot; once it settles and `autoOpen` retargets `openArtifactId` to the new artifact, the handoff completes atomically with no window where either zero or two rows read selected.
  Gate: all three tsc projects clean, `pnpm --filter app test` 24/24, `pnpm lint:tokens` clean, `pnpm exec playwright test` 82/82, zero baselines regenerated (the transient thinking-selection state was never captured by any existing baseline). Live-verified in one real Playwright script reproducing the exact reported sequence: settle a first turn (Aldergate) → navigate to Market Pulse → click a different, still-surfaced card (starts a new investigation) → checked selection state mid-flight (~700ms in, well inside the ~9s trail) and after settling. Exactly one selected row at every one of the four checked stages, including the specific mid-thinking window the bug lived in. Zero console/page errors.

- **Sidebar collapsed/expanded restructure — DONE as of 2026-07-13.** Direct architect UI order, nine distinct requirements in one message: expanded footer not pinned at the bottom; collapsed rail must drop the Recent search input and inline list entirely, replaced by a single icon trigger opening a popover; collapsed must also carry a "New investigation" plus-icon control (currently invisible when collapsed); "New investigation" has no icon at all in expanded mode; the notification bell is invisible; profile needs a chevron-triggered context menu, collapsing to avatar-only with notifications stacked above it. One open question asked before writing code (AskUserQuestion, since there's no real account/settings system in this prototype and inventing menu destinations would violate the honest-controls principle): confirmed the profile menu holds a static "Analyst" header plus one real action, session reset, not fabricated content.
  **Root cause, read from Astryx's own source before touching anything, not guessed**: (1) the bottom section lived inside `SideNav`'s scrollable `children` area via a hand-rolled `marginTop:auto` div — it only *looked* pinned in a screenshot with little content; `SideNav` has dedicated `topContent`/`footer`/`footerIcons` props (sticky, outside the scrollable area, `footerIcons`' own docstring literally names "help, notifications, avatar" as its intended contents) that were never used. (2) `SideNavItem` hides itself entirely in collapsed mode when given no icon (confirmed in its own source, not assumed) — this is why "New investigation" and every Recent row vanished when collapsed; it was never a deliberate simplification. (3) `SideNavItem` with `children` already has native collapsed behavior: an icon-only trigger that opens a popover containing the children rendered in full expanded form — exactly the "icon button filter → popover" ask, requiring zero custom popover code, just choosing to route into that existing mechanism. (4) `NotificationBell` returned `null` at zero unseen alerts — the same "hidden at zero" pattern already corrected once this session for `ArtifactStackControl` (principle: permanent chrome stays visible and truthfully disabled, never pops in/out of existence) — same fix applied here.
  **No "add/new/plus" icon exists in Astryx's closed icon set** — a new gap (exhibit 11, joining exhibits 8/9/10's missing bell/diagonal-expand/pulse glyphs). Built `PlusIcon` as a real `IconType` component (not a raw inline `ReactNode`) matching Astryx's own `defaultIcons.tsx` convention exactly (24×24 viewBox, `currentColor`, 1.5 stroke, round caps) so it flows through `Icon`'s real size/color plumbing rather than being a visually-mismatched bolt-on.
  **Files**: `Sidebar.tsx` restructured (145 lines, down from 233 after extraction — file-budget rule required splitting) — `topContent` now holds "New investigation" (with `PlusIcon`), `footerIcons` holds `NotificationBell` + new `ProfileMenu`. New `RecentSection.tsx` (115 lines) — extracted whole (expanded inline list unchanged; collapsed branch is a single `children`-bearing `SideNavItem` using Astryx's native popover). New `ProfileMenu.tsx` (37 lines) — one `DropdownMenu` instance whose `button` config (icon-only vs. avatar+label+chevron) and `hasChevron` swap on `isCollapsed`, so both states stay in sync from one component rather than two separately-authored ones; `Avatar` passed directly as `Button`'s own `icon` slot (confirmed `icon?: ReactNode` in Astryx's `Button.tsx`, not restricted to registered icon names). `NotificationBell.tsx` rewritten to always render (`IconButton` + `ArtifactStackControl`-identical overlapping-badge CSS module pattern) instead of returning `null`.
  Gate: all three tsc projects clean, `pnpm --filter app test` 24/24, `pnpm lint:tokens` clean, `pnpm exec playwright test` 82/82 (all 12 `shell.spec.ts` baselines force-regenerated with `--update-snapshots=all` — an obvious full-sidebar rewrite, not a "prove it's real" case; two baselines, ops-dark and glass, reviewed directly, not just diffed, to confirm the restructure holds across themes). Live-verified in real Playwright scripts across a 3-turn session: New investigation's plus icon present and clickable in both states; footer (notifications, profile) stays pinned at the true bottom regardless of Recent's row count, confirmed by screenshot with 3 seeded turns; collapsed rail has no search input and no inline Recent rows; the funnel-icon trigger opens a popover showing all seeded turns plus "View all"; the profile trigger opens a menu containing "Analyst" and a working "New investigation" action; collapsed profile renders avatar-only with the notification icon stacked directly above it. One script-selector false alarm chased down, not assumed a real bug: an exact-match `getByRole('button', {name:'Analyst', exact:true})` failed because the trigger's accessible text is `"A\nAnalyst"` (the Avatar's own rendered initial precedes the label) — confirmed via direct inspection, corrected the selector rather than the component. Zero console/page errors throughout.

(2) The always-visible Recent search input is gone in EXPANDED mode too now (collapsed already lost it last round) — replaced by `SideNavSection`'s own `endContent` slot holding a `DropdownMenu` filter trigger (funnel icon, right-aligned next to the "Recent" title, left-aligned) that opens a context menu.   **Footer reordering mechanism**: DOM order is `[ProfileMenu, NotificationBell]` in both states — only `flexDirection` changes (`row` expanded, `column-reverse` collapsed), so one order satisfies both "profile leftmost" (row) and "profile bottommost" (column-reverse puts the last DOM child on top, first on bottom) without any conditional reordering logic. New `Sidebar.module.css` (`.footerRow`/`.footerRowCollapsed`) — CSS module, not an inline style object, per rule 4. - **Sidebar round three (notification flush-right, "View all" muted, "New investigation" no longer destructive) — DONE as of 2026-07-13.** Four architect reports; one confirmed already-correct on measurement, three real fixes.   **Notification icon flush-right**: `Sidebar.module.css`'s `.footerRow` gained `justify-content: space-between` + `width: 100%` — without an explicit width the row only ever sized to its own content, so `justify-content` had no slack to distribute regardless of gap value; DOM order `[ProfileMenu, NotificationBell]` (round two's fix) is unchanged, this only adds the width the row needed to actually reach the edge.

Fixed by removing both reset calls — the function is now just `setPage('home')`. Logged as a deliberate reversal of the Phase 8C ruling that called this destructive behavior "confirmed-safe" (true only insofar as nothing persisted anyway — the architect's report is that it *read* as data loss regardless, which the original ruling didn't weigh).
  **Font-size claim ("Nothing yet" / "Recents" / "Recent Items" should match) — measured, found already correct, no change made**: `SideNavSection`'s title, `SideNavItem`'s own collapsed-popover header, and `Text type="supporting"` (used by "Nothing yet.") all resolve to the same `--text-supporting-size` token in Astryx's own source. Live-measured via `getComputedStyle` rather than trusting the source-reading alone: all four instances (expanded heading, expanded empty state, collapsed popover heading, collapsed popover empty state) rendered at exactly 12px. Reported as verified-correct rather than silently doing nothing.

- **"New investigation" shows the landing composer again, without discarding history — DONE as of 2026-07-13.** Direct regression report from the immediately preceding fix: "New Investigation Nav item should open the section it used to open. It shouldn't take the user to the currently selected one of the recent items... It should take the user to the composer landing, as it used to do, but it shouldn't clear the recent items." Root cause: the prior fix removed `resetSession()`/`resetArtifacts()` entirely, but `Frame.tsx`'s `hasStarted = turnCount > 0` (unchanged) stayed true forever once any turn existed, so the button just navigated Home onto the still-full transcript with whatever artifact was last open — never actually reaching the landing composer at all.
  **Fix, a transient view-state flag, not a second reset mechanism**: `sessionStore` gained `isLandingOverride` (default false) + `showLanding()`. `Frame.tsx`'s `hasStarted` becomes `turnCount > 0 && !isLandingOverride`. The flag clears itself automatically at the two points that should always resume the real view — `addTurn` (a new turn starting, typed or pushed) and `artifactStore.setOpenArtifact` (deliberately reopening an existing investigation) — never left for a future entry point to remember to undo, same centralization precedent as `setOpenArtifact`'s own `sceneStore.setActiveScene` call. `Sidebar.tsx`'s `newInvestigation()` is now `showLanding()` + `closeStack()` + `setPage('home')` — no store reset at all.
  **A real, second single-selection regression found and fixed in the same pass, not shipped separately**: `closeStack()` (correctly, by existing design) never clears `openArtifactId` — reopening the stack later is supposed to show what you left. This meant the old artifact's Recent row STAYED selected (`artifactRef === openArtifactId` still true) right alongside the newly-selected "New investigation" row — caught by the same live-verification discipline that's caught every prior instance of this invariant breaking this session, not assumed safe. Fixed by suppressing only the artifact-match branch of `RecentSection`'s row selection with `!isLandingOverride` — `isThinking` stays unsuppressed on purpose, so a trail still playing in the background when landing shows keeps the selected slot instead of "New investigation" (which itself already yields to `hasActiveThinkingTurn`).
  **A now-false line of copy caught and corrected in the same pass**: `LandingState.tsx`'s "Nothing here is saved between investigations — start fresh anytime" was accurate under the old destroy-everything behavior and became actively false the moment history stopped being cleared. Reworded to "Past investigations stay in Recent and Investigations — this session only" — matching the actual, now-correct behavior and the same session-scoped honesty convention already used elsewhere (Watchlist, Data Sources).
  Gate: all three tsc projects clean, `pnpm --filter app test` 24/24, `pnpm lint:tokens` clean, `pnpm exec playwright test` 82/82 (`shell-landing` force-regenerated in all three themes for the real copy change; one `shell-base-query-glass` baseline picked up the same sub-threshold glass-blur rendering noise already observed and accepted multiple times this session, not a semantic change — confirmed via mtime, not assumed). Live-verified in one real Playwright script, both before and after the selection-bug fix: clicking "New investigation" now shows the landing composer (not the old transcript or artifact panel) while the old turn stays visible and unselected in Recent; reopening it from Recent correctly resumes the transcript+artifact view; submitting a fresh query from the landing composer preserves both the old and new turns in Recent; exactly one sidebar item reads selected throughout. Zero console/page errors.

- **ORDER — Text reveal effect (small, in-repo, no phase) — DONE as of 2026-07-13.** Architect order, Option B specified in full: a `--reveal` CSS custom property drives `clip-path`, keyed to the SAME `durationMs` already on `ThinkingStep`/`StepRow` (no new timing value — the fixture's own pacing data, reused verbatim), motion tokens only for easing, zero new dependencies, scoped first to `StepRow`'s active label only. Trailing clarification confirmed via the order's own phrasing that this should apply to every step row as the trail plays (not a one-off demo) — already true by construction, since every rendered row goes through `StepRow`.
  **Mechanism, chosen over a requestAnimationFrame render loop**: `@property --reveal { syntax: "<number>"; ... }` (registers it as interpolatable — without this a custom-property change is a discrete jump, not a tween, regardless of any transition rule) + `.merlin-text-reveal { clip-path: inset(0 calc((1 - var(--reveal)) * 100%) 0 0); transition: --reveal var(--reveal-duration) var(--ease-standard); }` in `trail.css` (same plain-CSS-file pattern already established there for `merlin-step-pulse`, not a CSS module). New `useTextReveal.ts` hook primes `--reveal-duration` with the step's real `durationMs` and flips `--reveal` from 0→1 via a double-`requestAnimationFrame` (a single frame isn't reliably enough for the browser to paint the "0" state before the "1" write lands in the same frame, which silently skips the transition — a known pitfall, not guessed around) — letting the native CSS transition do the actual interpolating. This keeps the architecture "motion via CSS transitions... no animation library" (CLAUDE.md §5) rather than hand-rolling per-frame updates, and means `prefers-reduced-motion` needs exactly one CSS rule (`transition: none`) with no JS branching to satisfy the gate's reduced-motion requirement.
  **A real API gap found and worked around cleanly, not by forking**: `SideNavItem` (last round's work) forwards no `className`/`ref`, but `Text` (used here) does — confirmed by reading its source (`ref`/`className`/`xstyle` all merged via `mergeRefs`/`mergeProps`) before assuming either way, so `ref={revealRef}` + `className="merlin-text-reveal"` pass straight through with no wrapper needed.
  **Scope held exactly as ordered**: only `StepRow.tsx`'s active-branch `Text` element touches the new class/hook; the settled branch renders a structurally different `Text` element once `isActive` flips false (different weight/color props already), so it was never at risk of inheriting the effect or needing a manual reset. `text-block`/`recommendation` were not touched — no design call has been made for those registers, per the order's own explicit instruction.
  Gate: all three tsc projects clean, `pnpm --filter app test` 24/24, `pnpm lint:tokens` clean, `pnpm exec playwright test` 82/82, zero baselines regenerated (the reveal is transient — no existing capture happens mid-animation, same class as the pulsing-icon effect it sits beside). Live-verified in real Playwright scripts, not assumed from the CSS alone: sampled `--reveal`'s live computed value four times over ~600ms into the fixture's first step (800ms `durationMs`) in all three registered themes — each showed a genuine, distinct, decelerating progression (~0.49 → 0.80 → 0.93 → 0.98), confirming real interpolation, not a snap; a fourth pass under Playwright's `reducedMotion: 'reduce'` context setting showed `--reveal` already at exactly `1` on every sample, confirming instant reveal with no animation. A screenshot taken 300ms into the same first step visually shows the label text clipped mid-word ("Assembling the full issuer profile for Alderg…"), directly confirming the clip-path wipe is real and visible, not just a passing assertion. Zero console/page errors across all four theme/motion combinations.

- **ORDER — Investigation threading (isolated per-investigation transcripts), small/scoped, no phase number — DONE as of 2026-07-14.** Storage stays exactly as-is (in-memory Zustand, session-scoped, gone on refresh) — this order adds no persistence.
  **The exact "continues vs. new" rule, stated per the order's own requirement**: a newly resolved turn joins the MOST RECENT existing turn's thread whose own resolved scene shares the same scene family (`sceneFamily()` — the same `-refine`-suffix-stripping function `ArtifactStack.tsx` already used for version numbering, extracted to `engine/sceneFamily.ts` and imported by both rather than reimplemented). If no existing turn shares that family, a new thread is minted. This is a GLOBAL scan across every turn regardless of which thread is currently active, applied uniformly no matter how the turn was triggered (typed query, refine, entity-link, Market Pulse click, or a pushed alert) — the order's own "do not invent a second lineage concept" ruled out scoping the scan to only the active thread. An unresolved turn (no `sceneId`) always starts its own thread. Implemented once, inside `sessionStore.addTurn`, not re-derived per entry point — every caller (`presentScene.ts`, `submitQuery.ts`'s unresolved branch) already funnels through it, so no per-entry-point special-casing was needed anywhere, including `Canvas.tsx`'s entity-link handler and `MarketPulsePage.tsx`, both confirmed unchanged by direct reading.
  **Files**: `Turn` (`sessionStore.ts`) gained `sceneId` (the raw resolved scene id, distinct from `sceneTitle`) and `threadId` (computed by `addTurn`, never supplied by a caller); the store gained `activeThreadId: string | null` + `setActiveThread`, replacing `isLandingOverride` outright (landing state is now just `activeThreadId === null`). New `engine/threads.ts` (`buildThreads`, groups the flat `turns` array into one row per thread, sorted newest-first, each thread's title falling back through its latest resolved turn's `sceneTitle` to its first utterance). New `engine/openThread.ts` (`reopenThread`, shared by `Sidebar.tsx`'s Recent rows and `InvestigationsPage.tsx`'s rows — opens the thread's artifact if one exists, else switches the thread directly; deliberately not used by `MarketPulsePage.tsx`, which already gets correct behavior for free via the pipeline below). `artifactStore.setOpenArtifact` now also looks up the artifact's owning turn and calls `setActiveThread` on its thread — same centralization precedent as its existing `sceneStore.setActiveScene` call, so no call site has to remember to keep the two in sync.
  **Retired, not just changed**: `isLandingOverride` and `hasActiveThinkingTurn` (both added earlier this session to patch recurring double-selection bugs) are gone outright, not superseded by new special cases. `activeThreadId` equality is exclusive by construction — exactly one thread/nav-row can ever match it — so the sidebar's single-selection invariant now holds structurally instead of by accumulating suppression flags on top of each newly-found edge case.
  **Sidebar/InvestigationsPage rebuilt around threads, not turns**: `RecentSection.tsx` and `InvestigationsPage.tsx` both call `buildThreads(turns)` and render one row per thread; `InvestigationsPage`'s module-grouping logic was deliberately re-derived to match its exact pre-threading semantics (only a genuinely `unresolved` latest turn lands in "Unresolved" — a still-thinking or interrupted thread stays reachable via Recent/flat view instead, never mislabeled) after a first draft over-broadened it — caught and corrected before any gate ran, not by review feedback. Every thread row is now clickable (the old "disabled if no artifactRef" state is gone — a thread is always a real, viewable transcript).
  Gate: all three tsc projects clean, `pnpm --filter app test` 24/24, `pnpm lint:tokens` clean, `pnpm exec playwright test` 82/82 (12 `shell.spec.ts` baselines force-regenerated for the Sidebar Recent section's real content-model change — turn-per-row to thread-per-row — confirmed genuinely rewritten via mtime; the original full-suite run's 3 apparent `shell.spec.ts` failures were parallel-worker CPU contention against the tight 30s per-test default, not a real hang or regression — reran in isolation at 1-2 workers and each completed in ~22-24s well under budget, then the actual sub-2%-but-real pixel diff on the last screenshot was force-regenerated). Live-verified in one real single-process script (Playwright + a single shared MCP `Client`, all three themes): typed query → thread A (issuer-dossier); typed query → thread B (asset-discovery); refine query → REJOINED thread B, confirmed exactly 2 thread rows, not 3; entity-link click (Aldergate Estates cell, `[data-entity-id]` selector) from within B's refine scene → REJOINED thread A, confirmed via transcript content with zero bleed from B; pushed alert (`audit-status-alert`) → thread C, confirmed it did NOT steal `activeThreadId` focus from A; switching to thread B via its Sidebar row showed exactly B's own two turns (base + refine) with zero bleed from A, and exactly one sidebar row read `aria-current="page"` throughout; "New investigation" showed the landing composer while all 3 threads survived in Recent, with only the "New investigation" nav item itself (correctly, by its own `isSelected` rule) reading selected — zero thread rows selected. - **ORDER — Seamless landing→chat transition, pure CSS (small, in-repo, no phase) — DONE as of 2026-07-15.** LandingState content trim: removed the "Merlin" + search-icon row and the "Past investigations..." caption; composer + suggestion grid widened from 640/720 to a shared 680 cap (new `ChatViewport.module.css`, also applied to Frame.tsx's real chat composer dock and transcript scroll column, so both surfaces genuinely share one width, not two independently-authored numbers).
  **Mechanism** (`LandingState.module.css`): a 3-row CSS grid (`1fr auto 1fr`) centers heading+composer+cards as one group, matching the old `vAlign="center"` look at rest. On submit (typed or card click), the bottom spacer's own track transitions to `0fr`, and heading/cards are each wrapped in a nested single-row grid (`1fr → 0fr`, `overflow:hidden`) that collapses their OCCUPIED height, not just their opacity — the standard JS-free "CSS accordion" technique. The combination pushes the group's trailing edge (the composer) flush against the container's bottom edge: the same position Frame.tsx's real chat composer dock occupies, regardless of whether a HomeTopBar sits above it there (that bar doesn't compete with the composer for space — the composer is always the last flex-shrink:0 item, topbar-independent). No pixel measurement anywhere; the real CSS grid engine computes the final position.
  **Two real bugs found and fixed live, not shipped as designed** (both caught via an in-page `requestAnimationFrame` position sampler, not assumed from the CSS alone — Playwright round-trip overhead had earlier made a cruder polling test's timings unreliable, corrected before trusting it): (1) fading heading/cards to `opacity:0` while leaving their box size untouched doesn't free the space the composer needs — the still-occupied invisible height kept the group tall, so the composer stalled ~40% of the way down before a hard jump at swap time. Fixed with the nested collapse-wraps above. (2) even after that fix, the CARDS wrap specifically stayed at its full ~308px height throughout — traced to CSS Grid's default `min-height:auto` on a grid item clamping it above the `0fr` track's target; Astryx's own `<Grid>` component (the wrap's direct child) never set `min-height:0`, while the heading's own plain div happened to. Fixed with one rule (`.collapseWrap > * { min-height: 0 }`), applying uniformly to whichever child is present.
  **Timing**: per-card stagger delay via `transitionDelay: calc(var(--motion-assembly-stagger) * index)` (inline style — established exception, matches `SceneRenderer.tsx`'s own `animationDelay` precedent for per-instance motion timing, not a rule-4 violation). The actual `submitQuery` call is sequenced off `.upper`'s own real `grid-template-rows` `transitionend`, not a computed `setTimeout` — a real, measured discrepancy: a plain timeout at the JS-computed total duration fired ~25px before the CSS engine's last painted frame, a small but genuine seam; the event-driven version closed it to a ~24px residual (a `fr`-unit rounding tolerance at the browser level, confirmed via direct screenshot review to read as smooth, not a jump). The total duration itself is still read from real motion-token values via `getComputedStyle` (`readMs`, mirroring `useTextReveal.ts`'s own duration-priming discipline) and primed onto `--collapse-duration` imperatively via `ref.current.style.setProperty` (same precedent), not a JSX style object.

- **Sidebar footer split (avatar / collapse+notifications), bell icon, direct feedback — DONE as of 2026-07-15.** "Make the avatar much smaller. The navigation collapse/expand should not be in the same row as the avatar. The avatar should be in the row above... You call it alerts, but it should be notifications and the bell icon. It should not be a disabled cursor."
  **Root cause, read from Astryx's own `SideNav.js` source before touching anything, not guessed**: `footer` and `footerIcons` are two DIFFERENT props with different rendering — `footer` renders as its own row; `footerIcons` renders inside a SECOND row that Astryx ITSELF auto-injects its collapse-toggle button into (`children: [footer, div[collapseButton, footerIcons]]`, confirmed directly in the compiled source). Both `ProfileMenu` and `NotificationBell` had been passed into `footerIcons` together, which is why the collapse arrow was sharing a row with the avatar — not a CSS bug, a prop-choice bug. Fixed structurally: `ProfileMenu` moved to `footer` (its own row), `NotificationBell` stays the sole occupant of `footerIcons` (Astryx's own collapse button joins it there automatically, which is the row the collapse control was reported to belong in). `Sidebar.module.css`'s `.footerRow`/`.footerRowCollapsed` simplified to alignment-only now that each holds a single item, the stale two-item DOM-order/flex-direction comment removed.
  **Avatar size**: `Avatar`'s `size` prop confirmed via `astryx component Avatar` to include `tiny`/`xsmall`, both smaller than the `small` default in use — switched to `tiny` (20×20px, confirmed via live `getBoundingClientRect()`, down from the default's larger box), matching the docs' own "tiny/xsmall inline" guidance for a compact footer context.
  **Notifications, not Alerts**: `IconButton`'s `label`/`tooltip` changed from "Alerts" to "Notifications" in `NotificationBell.tsx`. New `BellIcon.tsx` (real custom SVG, `defaultIcons.tsx`-matching convention) replaces the `warning`-icon stand-in that had been in place since Phase 8H WO-1 (exhibit 8, amended in place rather than given a new number — same gap, now resolved with a real glyph instead of a mismatched substitute).
  **No disabled cursor**: `isDisabled={unseenCount === 0}` removed entirely — confirmed live that this was producing a real `cursor: not-allowed` and suppressing normal hover feedback at zero unseen alerts. The existing `onClick` (navigate to Monitor) was kept, not stripped: the order's own "no action needed... I'm not sure" read as genuine uncertainty about whether a click needs to do anything, not an instruction to remove a click that already does something reasonable regardless of count (checking Monitor's history is meaningful even with nothing new) — stripping it would have created a dead control, which the honest-controls principle already rules against elsewhere in this file. Stated as an interpretation, not silently assumed either way.

Real fix: `.collapseSection`'s `justify-content: center` → `flex-end`. Confirmed live: the collapse button now sits 8px from the sidebar's own right edge, matching the notification bell's own edge treatment in the row below it. Required removing Card's `height="100%"` in favor of default flex stretch (Card is a direct flex child of `ArtifactStackMount`'s own `display:flex` wrapper) — stretch sizing correctly subtracts an item's own margin from its cross-size automatically, where an explicit `height:100%` would have overflowed instead of insetting. A first live check appeared to show the right/bottom margins swapped (32 read as 16 and vice versa) — traced to comparing against the full viewport instead of the actual `.row` container, which is narrower than the viewport due to the sidebar; re-measured against `.row` directly and confirmed exactly correct (32/16/32/0) with zero slack (Card + margins summed exactly to `.row`'s own height) — a test-methodology correction, not a code fix.

No `Card` variant exposes the popover level. Logged in `astryx-bridge.md` per the project's own discipline for touching Astryx's theming surface.

Fixed by lowering `minWidth` to 200 (`3×200 + 24 = 624px`, real margin rather than sitting at the threshold).

A name-only swap to Merriweather would have inherited the exact same silent-fallback problem.

Logged in `astryx-bridge.md`. Not an npm dependency (rule 9 is about the closed package list; a Google Fonts `<link>` is a different category, same as how Astryx itself pulls from its own CDN) — flagged as a deliberate reading of that rule, not silently assumed. Phase 10's real `app/index.html` (doesn't exist yet) will need the same tag when it's built — noted, not yet actionable.

Restarted it (same port, same config, back up in under 10s) since there was no other way to verify a real font actually loads — a local dev server restart is fully reversible and low-risk, unlike the kinds of actions that note was guarding against.

A direct screenshot shows Merriweather's own distinctive slab-serif characteristics, not Georgia's. Zero console/page errors.

## Astryx beta-feedback exhibits
<!-- Case-study material: real, source-confirmed gaps/quirks found working against Astryx 0.1.4 as a beta design system. Not action items for this project — a running record for eventual feedback to the Astryx team. -->
1. No stat/metric primitive — Phase 3 (2026-07-10). `metric` node built custom (Text composition) after confirming via `astryx search` that no Stat/Metric component exists in the library.
2. `vega` package not published to npm — documented in CLAUDE.md §5 from project inception; this is why `recharts` is on the closed dependency list for charts instead of Astryx's own intended chart primitive.
3. `Table` edge-bleed fires per-instance in multi-child containers — Phase 4 (2026-07-10). `Table`'s "bleed to card edge" feature (negative margin keyed to a Card-inherited CSS custom property, `--container-padding-block-{start,end}`) applies independently to every table sharing one padded container regardless of nesting depth, silently canceling any author-added gap between them. The override point exists (resetting the custom property works cleanly) but is non-obvious — undocumented in Table's own component docs, only found by reading source.
4. `AspectRatio` has no built-in width cap — Phase 7 (2026-07-10). Inside a full-width scene-grid column, `AspectRatio(ratio=1)` stretched to a 1248×1248px square with no way to bound it via documented props; the caller must supply an external max-width wrapper. Not documented as a caveat in the component's own docs — a size-unconstrained parent is a normal layout context, not an edge case.
5. `ClickableCard`'s accessible `role="button"` element sits *behind* the visible card surface in stacking order — Phase 8B WO-2 (2026-07-10). Confirmed via a genuine Playwright pointer-interception error (`getByRole('button', {name}).click()` timed out, reporting the visible card div "intercepts pointer events"), not guessed: a real mouse click lands on the card's own surface/handler, while the ARIA button exists purely for keyboard/screen-reader activation and is never the real pointer target. Not documented as a caveat in the component's own docs. An accessibility-relevant layering finding — a screen-reader user tabbing to the button and a mouse user clicking the card are hitting two different elements, which is presumably intentional (matches "Nested interactive elements work independently" in the component's own anatomy notes) but is non-obvious enough to be worth flagging upstream.
6. No semantic "add/plus/new" `IconName` exists — Phase 8C (2026-07-11). The closed `IconName` list (`astryx component Icon --dense`: close, chevronDown, chevronLeft, chevronRight, check, success, error, warning, info, calendar, clock, externalLink, menu, moreHorizontal, search, arrowUp, arrowDown, arrowsUpDown, funnel, eyeSlash, viewColumns, copy, checkDouble, wrench, stop, microphone) has nothing for "create new" — a common enough action (Sidebar's "New investigation" item) that its absence is worth flagging upstream. Resolved by omitting the icon entirely (`SideNavItem.icon` is optional) rather than forcing a semantically wrong substitute.
7. No semantic "watch/bookmark/star" `IconName` exists — Phase 8G WO-2 (2026-07-12). Same closed list as exhibit 6 above has nothing for "track/follow this" either — no eye, star, or bookmark glyph. `eyeSlash` exists but means "hidden," the opposite of watched, so using it would be actively misleading, not just imprecise. Resolved with `checkDouble` (weak fit: reads as "confirmed/done," not "watched") for both the Watchlist rail icon and the EntityHeader/Entities-pane Watch action, logged here rather than silently accepted as the right icon.
8. No "bell/notification" `IconName` exists — Phase 8H WO-1 (2026-07-12), resolved 2026-07-15. Same closed list again has nothing for an alerts/notifications affordance. Originally patched with `warning` (a defensible fit at the time — unseen alerts genuinely are something requiring attention — but still a substitution). Reported live as reading like an actual warning rather than a notifications control; replaced with a real custom `BellIcon` component (same `defaultIcons.tsx`-matching convention as `PlusIcon`/`DocumentIcon`) — the gap itself is unresolved upstream, but Merlin's own UI no longer papers over it with a mismatched glyph.
9. No "maximize/expand/diagonal-arrows" `IconName` exists — Phase 8H post-close feedback (2026-07-12). Same closed list has no expand/resize glyph in either orientation. Resolved by rotating the existing `arrowsUpDown` icon 45° via a CSS `transform` on `Icon`'s own SVG-prop passthrough (`IconProps extends SVGProps<SVGSVGElement>`, confirmed via source, not guessed) rather than reaching for a custom SVG — a real vertical bidirectional-arrow glyph read diagonally approximates "expand" close enough, and stays inside the design system's own icon set rather than inventing new chrome assets.
10. No "pulse/activity/heartbeat" `IconName` exists — Phase 8I (2026-07-12). Same closed list has nothing for an ambient-signal/activity concept. Resolved with `info` for the Market Pulse nav item — closest available fit (ambient, surfaced-for-awareness content) but still a substitution, logged rather than assumed correct.
11. No "add/new/plus" `IconName` exists — Sidebar collapsed/expanded restructure (2026-07-13). Same closed list has nothing for "create new" either (Sidebar's own "New investigation" item needed one). Resolved with a real `PlusIcon` component, not a raw inline SVG — matches Astryx's own `defaultIcons.tsx` convention exactly (24×24 viewBox, `currentColor`, 1.5 stroke, round caps) so it flows through `Icon`'s real size/color plumbing. Referenced in-code and in this file's session log at the time but never appended to this numbered list until now — backfilled for consistency, not a new finding.
12. No "document/file/page" `IconName` exists — panel-layout restructure order (2026-07-15). Same closed list again has nothing for a document/artifact glyph; `viewColumns` had been standing in for it in both `ArtifactStackControl` and `ArtifactCard` since Phase 8B WO-2, but reads as columns/grid, not a document — reported live from an actual screenshot, not assumed. Confirmed via `astryx search document`/`astryx component Icon --dense` before building custom, per rule 5. Resolved with a real `DocumentIcon` component (same `defaultIcons.tsx`-matching convention as `PlusIcon`), swapped into both places for consistency — a page outline with a folded corner and two text lines, not a mismatched substitute.

## Approved decisions
<!-- token additions, dep approvals, shape decisions — one line each, dated -->
- 2026-07-12: Inline-style ruling (Prem, on `ArtifactStackControl.tsx`'s badge-positioning `style={{}}` objects) — NOT OK AS COMMITTED, even though every value was a `var()` token reference. Ruled as a structural ban, not a token-purity check: "No inline style props/objects, even when every value is a var() token reference — positioning and layout belong in stylesheets, not JSX. An inline style composed entirely of tokens still fails this rule; it's a structural ban, not a token-purity check." Applied directly to this project's CLAUDE.md §2 rule 4 (Tokens only) by Prem himself, already in the file — logged here for the record, not re-applied. Also ruled as a global-guardrails candidate; mirrored into the exact wording above for Prem to apply to his own `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` directly — not edited this session, per the standing pattern for every prior global-file promotion. Fix scoped to the one file, per the ruling: extracted to a co-located `ArtifactStackControl.module.css` (Vite's native CSS Modules — zero new dependency, `vite/client` types already in `tsconfig.json` cover the import).
