(1) Tier 3 CONFIRMED permanently out of scope — a standing architectural decision, not a future phase; the <768px notice APPROVED, ships alongside tier 2's own phase, not as a separate order. (2) Tier 2 sizing correction ACCEPTED — real new architecture, sized and scoped as its own phase (Phase 17, added to CLAUDE.md §8 now per the Phase 11/12 backlog-block precedent, work order issued when reached), not squeezed into a small order. Prem specifically called out the workbenchStore-history catch as a good one — re-adding pane-activity tracking must be done deliberately in that future work order, citing why it's coming back.

- **Phase 18 — Responsive tier 2 + desktop-only notice (2026-07-26).** GATE PASSED. Shipped exactly the 3-step order: (1) `DesktopOnlyNotice` behind a `useViewportWidth` hook, early-returned in `Frame.tsx` below 768px, after every hook call (Rules of Hooks) — verified live in all 3 themes. (2) Tier 2 mechanics: `.transcriptPane` changed from `flex: 0 0 480px` to `flex: 1 1 480px; min-width: 320px` (proportional, real floor); `.contentColumn`'s `min-width: 0` → `420px` (protected minimum, matches EntityDetailPage's own 2-col reflow floor); `registry.ts`'s `RegistryEntry` gained an additive optional `minWidth`, set on `comparison` and `concentration-map`, read by a new `getSceneMinWidth` helper (`engine/sceneMinWidth.ts`) that both `ArtifactStack.tsx` and `ArtifactStackMount.tsx` feed into `useResizable`'s `minSizePx`; `artifactStore.ts` gained additive `paneActivity`/`touchPaneActivity`/`collapsedPane`/`setCollapsedPane` (same store, not a second one — see below on why this doesn't reintroduce the Phase 8H duplication). (3) Collapse trigger: `usePaneFitCollapse.ts` (new) collapses the least-recently-active open pane when the row can't fit both at their floors, `CollapsedPaneChip.tsx` (new) is the one-click labeled restore. `Frame.tsx` hit the 200-line budget mid-order; extracted `usePaneVisibility.ts` (new) to hold the pane-visibility/activity/collapse orchestration, bringing Frame back to 189 lines with headroom.
  **Deviation, corrected via verification not guesswork**: `comparison`'s registered floor was initially 560 (set in an earlier, uncited session) — live testing at Playwright's own default 1280×720 viewport surfaced this as too generous once actually load-bearing: it forced a collapse that didn't need to happen. Traced to Astryx's own `Table` column math (`proportional(1)` columns get a ~120px floor each) against the ONE fixture that uses `comparison` (asset-discovery, 4 columns: label + 3 candidates) → true minimum is 480, not 560. Corrected in `registry.ts` with the derivation cited in a comment, not just the number.
  **Three real bugs found and fixed during live browser verification (not hypothesized, reproduced then root-caused each time)**:
  1. Restoring a collapsed pane via its chip left it expanded even when it still didn't fit — `ResizeObserver` only fires on the OBSERVED element's own border-box change, never on overflow caused by a child appearing, so it silently missed "pane remounted, now overflowing again." Fixed by adding a `MutationObserver` on the row's direct children.
  2. That fix alone created a worse bug: reacting to node REMOVALS too made the mutation observer fire the instant a collapse's own exit animation finished unmounting the pane — and at that exact instant the row trivially "fits" (the thing that didn't fit is now gone), so it immediately un-collapsed, which immediately overflowed again, forever. Confirmed via a live `animationcancel` listener (the CSS slide animation was being restarted every cycle, never reaching `animationend`). Fixed by making the mutation observer react ONLY to additions, never removals — a removal is the mechanism's own prior action, tautological to re-check.
  3. Even with (1)+(2) fixed, restoring a pane at exactly Playwright's default 1280×720 viewport still failed a real test (`shell.spec.ts`'s base-query assertion couldn't find `Candidates above 6%`, timeout not a screenshot diff). Root cause: `row.scrollWidth`/`getBoundingClientRect` include the CSS `transform` the slide-in/out animation runs on — a pane resting at any transform other than `translateX(0)` (confirmed live via `getComputedStyle().transform`, found `matrix(1,0,0,1,489.148,0)` — exactly the pane's own width, i.e. never actually finished sliding in) gets measured at its PAINTED position, not its true layout footprint, producing a phantom ~500px gap that read as overflow. A separate red herring chased in the same session: a wide `data-table` (5 cols × 120px = 600px, no registered floor) appeared to be "bleeding" past its pane via the same flawed getBoundingClientRect-based detection — disproven by directly forcing `overflow:hidden` on its wrapper and confirming `row.scrollWidth` didn't change; Astryx's own `Table` already self-contains via an internal `astryx-table-scroll-wrapper` (`overflow-x:auto`). Real fix for (3): `usePaneFitCollapse`'s `checkFit` now sums direct children's `offsetWidth` (transform-independent, layout-only) instead of reading `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth` — also incidentally immune to the data-table red herring, since summing direct children never descends into a child's own separately-contained overflow. `ArtifactStack.tsx`'s inner content wrapper also gained `overflowX: 'auto'` alongside its existing `overflowY: 'auto'` (correct defensive behavior for a resizable panel with wide internal content, kept even though the offsetWidth fix made it non-critical for this specific regression).
  **Why least-recently-active tracking is back after Phase 8H removed it, and confirmation it doesn't reintroduce the workbenchStore/artifactStore duplication**: Phase 8H folded `workbenchStore` into `artifactStore` because only one pane existed at the time (activity-per-pane was meaningless with a single pane). Phase 18 reintroduces genuine competition for space between the content column, transcript pane, and artifact pane, so "which pane was least recently active" becomes a real, needed question again. It does NOT reintroduce the duplication Phase 8H fixed: `paneActivity`/`collapsedPane` live as additive fields on the SAME `artifactStore`, not a second store — confirmed by reading the current file before adding to it, not assumed.
  **A scoping gap in the order's own gate language, found and disclosed rather than silently worked around**: the gate's "3-pane-open scenario forcing a 4th" cannot occur under the current shell architecture. Read `pageStore.ts`'s own `setPage`: navigating to ANY non-home page calls `useArtifactStore.getState().closeStack()` — a pre-existing, deliberate, commented law ("Home is the only 'investigation' place... an artifact left open there reads as stale context once you've navigated away"). Combined with `naturalShowTranscript` excluding home, this makes `naturalShowTranscript` and `naturalShowStack` mutually exclusive by construction — `collapseCandidates` never holds more than one real entry today. The least-recently-active selection logic is implemented correctly and is exercised (transcript-vs-content on a place page; artifact-vs-content on Home), but never in a literal 2-candidate race, since there is no scenario with both a transcript pane AND an open artifact pane simultaneously under current routing law. Flagging for Prem's awareness rather than treating the gate line as unmet by omission.
  **Live verification evidence** (Storybook `shell-frame--default` story, real browser via Playwright MCP, not just `test:visual`): desktop-only notice fires correctly at 700px, clears at 1400px, confirmed in all 3 themes. Proportional shrink confirmed at 1150px (transcript pane exactly at its 320px floor, content still above its own floor, nothing collapsed) and 1080px (same). Collapse-then-restore-then-recollapse cycle verified clean (no stuck animations, no duplicate DOM) at 900px on Home (content+artifact). The gate's own named scenario — a `comparison`-bearing scene forced to squeeze at a standard desktop width — verified at exactly 1280×720 (Playwright's own default viewport) in both `default` and `ops-dark` themes: renders correctly, panel adjacent to content, no phantom gap. `comparison`/`concentration-map` legibility at their TRUE floor widths (480/400, not the story's fixed decorator width) confirmed by temporarily overriding the story wrapper's width via `evaluate` — both ellipsize/reflow cleanly, no clipped text, matching the vocabulary's clipPath/legibility-floor law.
  **Gate evidence**: `tsc -b app/tsconfig.json --force`, root `tsc --noEmit`, `tsc --noEmit -p mcp-server/tsconfig.json` all clean · `vitest run` 33/33 passed · `lint:tokens` clean (theme parity + no raw values) · `test:visual` 106 passed, 15 failed — same 15 as immediately before this phase started (4 renderer fixtures + shell base-query, ×3 themes), all traced to the still-uncommitted `SceneSummary` CSS-module conversion from the earlier-approved concurrent work (STOP-and-wait ruling, 2026-07-25 entry above) — confirmed by grep (`scene-summary` is the one node type common to all 4 failing renderer fixtures) and by `git status` (the file is still modified, uncommitted). Zero net-new failures from Phase 18.
  Open items: the 15 pre-existing SceneSummary-linked visual baselines remain unevaluated, unchanged from before this phase, per the standing STOP-and-wait ruling — not touched. The "3-pane forcing a 4th" gate scenario is architecturally impossible today (documented above); worth a explicit ruling from Prem on whether that's acceptable as-is or whether a future phase should revisit the Home-only-artifact routing law. Raw learnings (uncleared, pending promotion review): `getBoundingClientRect`/`scrollWidth` include CSS `transform` paint offsets — never trust them to measure "true layout size" of anything that might rest on a transform-based animation's fill-forwards frame; sum children's `offsetWidth` instead. A `MutationObserver`-based re-check must distinguish node ADDITIONS (a new candidate that might not fit) from REMOVALS (likely the mechanism's own prior action — re-checking immediately is often tautological and can create a live oscillation, which manifests as repeated CSS `animationcancel` events, not an obvious error). An inline array literal recomputed every render is an unstable effect dependency the instant it's wired into anything with side effects (observers, subscriptions) — memoize before that wiring, not after chasing the resulting churn. `pnpm --filter app dev` / plain `vite dev` doesn't serve this app (no `index.html` — Storybook is the only way to render it); port 5173 on this machine is a different, unrelated project (`~/iGenie/i-genie-prototype`) — confirmed via `lsof`, not assumed, before wasting further time on it.
- **Phase 18 — architect ruling + gate substitute close-out (2026-07-26).** Four-point ruling on the Phase 18 report above; all four closed this entry.
  1. **Collapse-trigger gap: ACCEPTED as forward-looking infrastructure — ship as built.** Logged verbatim per Prem's own wording: "collapse mechanism has no current trigger scenario (transcript+artifact never coexist under today's pageStore navigation law); will activate once a third simultaneous pane exists (Phase 17's entity-preview, if built). Verified correct via synthetic test, not real usage — re-verify live the first time a real trigger scenario exists."
  2. **Gate substitute, built this entry**: `PaneFitCollapseHarness.tsx` + `.stories.tsx` (new, `components/shell/`) — a test-only harness that calls `usePaneFitCollapse` directly against two fake 200px panes forced to overflow a fixed 300px row, bypassing the impossible-today 4th-pane requirement entirely. `tests/visual/paneFitCollapse.spec.ts` (new) drives it: confirms the least-recently-active pane (Pane A, touched first) collapses on overflow, confirms restore-then-recollapse stays clean (the exact bug class fixed this phase), confirms no duplicate/stuck DOM. **Building this harness caught a FOURTH real bug**, live, immediately (React's own "Maximum update depth exceeded" on first render): the harness's first draft used a ternary swapping `AnimatedPane` for `CollapsedPaneChip` synchronously, which skips the exit-animation grace window Frame.tsx's real sibling-pattern relies on (old pane stays mounted mid-exit while the chip appears, so a checkFit triggered by the chip's own mount still reads "overflowing" and does nothing) — the ternary's synchronous swap meant checkFit's chip-triggered re-check measured a row that had ALREADY lost the old pane, read "fits," and un-collapsed immediately, overflowed again, looped. Fixed by making `AnimatedPane`/`CollapsedPaneChip` real JSX siblings (both always present, chip conditional, pane owns its own exit lifecycle) — the bug was in the harness's structure, not `usePaneFitCollapse` itself, but it's a direct, sharpened confirmation of exactly the mechanism this order's real bugs were about. Gate evidence: new spec passes standalone and inside the full `test:visual` run (107 passed, same 15 pre-existing unrelated failures, zero net-new — up from 106 passed before this harness existed).
  3. **Three-bug chain PROMOTED to `merlin-new-node` skill** (`.claude/skills/merlin-new-node/SKILL.md`, §2 Component): "Fit/overflow checks driven by ResizeObserver or getBoundingClientRect must account for in-flight CSS transforms... measure transform-independent properties (offsetWidth sums)... not viewport-relative measurements that include the animation itself," plus the MutationObserver addition-vs-removal corollary, both cited to Phase 18 with the concrete symptoms (`animationcancel` never reaching `animationend`).
  4. **`comparison` floor correction (560→480) approved** — the actual root cause of bug #3's regression trigger, verified against Astryx's own column math rather than guessed.
  **Overall**: Phase 18 approved, contingent on the synthetic collapse test landing — now landed. Phase 18 is fully closed as of this entry.

- **Refine-scene stuck-transition bug: FIXED, root-caused precisely (2026-07-27).** Architect ruling: fix now. Root cause found via direct empirical testing, not inferred: injected a manually-constructed test `<div>` with `animation-name: 'merlin-exit'` outside of React entirely — zero animation events fired (not even `animationstart`), confirming the browser wasn't running the animation at all, independent of any Canvas.tsx logic. Cross-checked by reading `document.styleSheets` for the actual registered `@keyframes` rule names: `merlin-panel-slide-in/-out` (plain `ArtifactStackMount.css`) registered under their own plain names as expected, but `assembly.module.css`'s two keyframes registered as `_merlin-reveal_13mms_1` and `_merlin-exit_13mms_1` — Vite's CSS Modules DOES scope `@keyframes` names defined inside a `.module.css` file. `Canvas.tsx` referenced `'merlin-exit'` by raw string (needed for a dynamic JS on/off toggle, not expressible as a static CSS class) — the reference silently matched nothing, so the exit animation never started, `onAnimationEnd` never fired, and `displayedScene` never advanced past the first scene of any investigation. `merlin-reveal` (the other keyframe in the same file) turned out NOT broken: `SceneRenderer.tsx` reaches it only through the scoped `.nodeWrapper` class (`styles.nodeWrapper`), never a raw string — same-module class and keyframe get rewritten together consistently by the CSS Modules transform, so that path was fine the whole time. This also retroactively explains why the earlier "keyframes stay global under CSS Modules" belief (used to justify the original `assembly.css`→`assembly.module.css` Storybook-loading fix, well before this session) was true only by coincidence for the OTHER keyframes involved at the time (all in plain `.css` files) — it was never actually true for a keyframe defined inside a genuine `.module.css` file, and nothing had exercised that specific combination (raw-string reference to a `.module.css`-defined keyframe) until `merlin-exit`.
  Fix: moved `@keyframes merlin-exit` out of `assembly.module.css` into a new plain `app/src/renderer/sceneExit.css` (matching the already-proven-working pattern every other JS-toggled keyframe in this codebase uses — `ArtifactStackMount.css`, `trail.css`), updated `Canvas.tsx`'s import accordingly. `merlin-reveal`/`.nodeWrapper` left untouched in `assembly.module.css` — confirmed working, no reason to move.
  Live-verified end to end: base query → refine query now correctly replaces the scene (own confidence value, own recommendation text, own candidate table, "Vantara Metals" correctly shown as excluded) — confirmed the STALE base-scene content genuinely disappears, not just that new content additionally appears. Gate: `tsc` ×3 clean, `vitest` 33/33, `lint:tokens` clean, full `test:visual` **122/122 passed — zero failures, first time this entire session** (the `shell-refine-query-*`/`shell-unresolved-query-*` baselines, missing since at least Jul 20, now exist for the first time, generated from a correctly-functioning transition).

  **Real bug found and fixed via live testing, not assumed**: the plan's original design reused the existing `collapsedPane` field for the Chat toggle's manual-close action ("reusing the existing collapse mechanism rather than inventing a second one"). Live-testing the actual toggle click revealed `usePaneFitCollapse`'s own "fits again, clear it" rule (its `checkFit` effect re-runs on every `collapsedPane` change and un-collapses the instant there's no real width overflow — true almost always at a normal viewport) immediately reverted the manual close, since that observer can't distinguish "the user closed this" from "this no longer needs to be squeezed" — clearing the latter is exactly its job. - **Artifact pane surface styling (2026-07-29).** Theme fix, post-implementation follow-up. Architect reported live: light mode (default theme's light scheme) is correct (artifacts pane has outline only, same background as workbench), but dark mode (default theme's dark scheme) renders the pane as a raised surface with different background. Root cause confirmed via live inspection: `ArtifactStack.module.css` was setting background to `--surface-3` (popover level, `oklch(.26 .015 250)` in dark vs. body's `oklch(.16 .015 250)`) to match the ChatComposer's own background — but that composer equation was itself incorrect when re-examined against the original intent (a contained surface, not elevated). Architecture decision (confirmed in astryx-bridge.md): the artifacts pane should match the workbench background (`--surface-0`) with only an outline/border (same visual containment semantics as entity links, footnotes, citations elsewhere), not a raised surface. Fixed `ArtifactStack.module.css` line 17: `background-color: var(--surface-3)` → `background-color: var(--surface-0); border: 1px solid var(--edge);` — a single-card-instance override (`.artifactCard:global(.astryx-card)[data-variant='default']`), same specificity pattern already established. Updated astryx-bridge.md's "Artifact stack Card background" entry to document both the Phase 1 decision (popover-level) and the Phase 2 revision (workbench-level), rationale, and unchanged narrowness of scope (other Cards in the app, correctly using card-level surfaces, are unaffected). Live-verified in all three themes: light-default (stone light) matches the workbench, dark-default (stone dark) matches the workbench (no longer a distinct surface), ops-dark (workbench-0 at `.16` L, pane now same, edge visible), glass (pane now transparent like the workbench, edge at `.10` alpha visible). Gate: `tsc` ×3 clean, `vitest` 33/33, `lint:tokens` clean, full `test:visual` 122/122 (zero baseline changes — the visual effect in light mode was already this way, the dark-mode correction matches what the dark theme already renders, and the light-based fixture screenshots dominate the suite's coverage).
- **Panel elevation fix — permanent (facts) vs provisional (reasoning) register (2026-07-29).** Architect ruling: any registry component using Astryx Card with default raised-surface treatment when it should embody the "panel" register ("hairline edge, no chrome" per node-vocabulary.md principle 2) is out of spec. Two-tier treatment implemented: (1) **Permanent-fact displays** (`Panel` node, `SceneSummary`'s confidence metadata pane) — `panelFlat` class, outline-only, `--surface-0` + `--edge` border across all three themes. (2) **Provisional/reasoning displays** (`SceneSummary`'s Summary + Recommendation panes) — `panelProvisional` class, completely transparent (no background, no border) to allow content to read independently from any visual frame, distinct from outlined fact-panels. Rationale: provisional content (agent's prose, conclusions) needs to be visually separate from permanent facts per principle 2, but should not default to Astryx's generic raised Card either — transparency achieves distinction without chrome. Also fixed: **ChatComposer** (`[data-theme='default'] .astryx-chat-composer > div`) border removed (kept background for visual separation from canvas). **Artifacts pane Card** (`.artifactCard`) changed from `background: var(--surface-0); border: 1px solid var(--edge)` → `background: transparent; border: none` — complete invisibility across all themes. Node-vocabulary.md principle 2 amended with the visual mechanism rule, dated. Gate: `tsc` ×3 clean, `vitest` 33/33, `lint:tokens` clean, full `test:visual` regenerated — 129 baselines, two consecutive clean runs 122/122 both times (real content changes: Summary/Recommendation panes now transparent, multiple fixture screenshots updated accordingly; artifacts pane now transparent, shell.spec.ts three shell-base-query screenshots + per-theme pair regenerated).

This is a real dependency addition, gated by CLAUDE.md rule 10 ("no new dependencies without approval") — stopped and asked before installing rather than assuming which of the two paths (hand-copy vs. real package) was wanted; architect chose the real package. CLAUDE.md §5 amended with the approved entry (`app/src/components/shell/PaneControlButton.module.css` was mid-session context, not itself touched by this addition). No components migrated yet — this session only lands the dependency; consuming it (e.g. swapping future gap-fill icons to `@heroicons/react/24/outline` imports instead of new hand-copied files) is follow-up work, not done here.

- **Glass material made real + pointer specular + glass-light ◆ (2026-08-06).** Direct architect order, three parts in one pass, after an architecture Q&A about how default/ops-dark/glass relate to Astryx's own neutral/stone × light/dark axes.
  **Finding that reframed the whole order — glass had never actually been glass.** Reported to the architect as a correction to my own claim one turn earlier (I had asserted glass shipped `backdrop-filter: blur(24px)`, having read the token DECLARATIONS and assumed consumption). Verified properly: zero `backdrop-filter` anywhere in the repo, and `--surface-blur`/`--edge-highlight`/`--glow` were declared in all three theme files and referenced by NOTHING. The theme was translucent alpha only. Phase 0 specced the material; nothing ever consumed it. Direct instance of the standing "check real APIs/state before use, never assume from memory" rule, and the reason step 1 became "wire what was specced" rather than "extend what exists."
  **Architect rulings obtained before any code** (three genuine constraint conflicts, asked rather than improvised):
  1. *Ink invariant amended.* tokens-spec.md §Invariants read "Ink identical across themes" — which no light scheme can satisfy. Ruled: restate as "identical WITHIN a scheme; a light scheme flips the ink ramp and nothing else." Accents, viz palette and motion still hold constant across a scheme pair, which is what keeps glass-light a scheme OF glass rather than a fourth unrelated theme. tokens-spec.md amended with the change dated and the original wording quoted.
  2. *Specular needs JS.* CSS cannot source pointer position — no custom property, no selector exposes it. Ruled: shell-level hook writing `--spec-x`/`--spec-y` via `setProperty`, panes opting in with `data-glass-surface`. Rule 4's ban is on inline style props/objects deciding APPEARANCE; these two carry coordinates only, and every visual decision (gradient, radius, colour, whether it renders) stays in theme/module CSS. Registry components untouched, rules 6/7 intact.
  3. *Selector shape* (recommended and unopposed, proceeded as a stated assumption): `glass-light` is a sibling `data-theme` value, keeping the enum flat, not a new orthogonal `data-scheme` axis. Revisit only if a third scheme pair appears — one duplicated pattern is cheaper than a premature abstraction.
  **Second finding, mid-verification — the material had nothing to refract.** With blur correctly applied, glass still read as flat tinted rectangles: `--surface-0` is transparent and the app never supplied a canvas, so `backdrop-filter` was blurring a blank page. tokens-spec.md's glass ◆ had said "canvas shows through — app supplies deep backdrop" since Phase 0; that half was never built either. Added `--canvas-backdrop` (a `background-image` value, `none` in both matte themes) applied at theme.default.css's existing `:where(html, body)` canvas rule, which had to be split off the `background` shorthand first — the shorthand silently resets `background-image`, the same class of trap as this session's earlier Astryx hover/`background-color` finding. Two asymmetric off-axis pools over a vertical field, deliberately not uniform: a uniform backdrop blurs to itself and reads flat. Flagged in-file and in the report as a TASTE DIAL — `--canvas-backdrop: none` removes it entirely without touching anything else.
  **New**: `theme.glass-light.css` (full ◆ theme — light L-ramp .62→.78, flipped ink, accents darkened to .52–.58 holding the same H 225 signal identity, `color-scheme: light`, the one Meridian theme that pins Astryx light); `useSpecularPointer.ts` (rAF-throttled, element-relative percentages, clears on surface-exit and window-leave so the highlight can't freeze mid-sweep).
  **Modified**: `tokens.base.css` (saturation scale + specular radius primitives), all three existing theme files (`--surface-material`/`--spec-alpha`/`--spec-radius`/`--canvas-backdrop`; `none`/`0` in default+ops-dark), `PageShell.module.css` + `ArtifactStack.module.css` (consume `--surface-material`, `::before` specular layer, content raised above it), `PageShell.tsx` + `ArtifactStack.tsx` (one `data-glass-surface` attribute each — a behaviour hook, not a style), `Frame.tsx` (calls the hook, deliberately unconditional on theme: gating it there would put a visual decision in application code, which is what the cascade exists to prevent), `ThemeSwitch.tsx` + `.storybook/preview.ts` (register glass-light), `lint-theme-parity.mjs` (4th theme file), all 4 visual specs (glass-light added to THEMES — a registered theme with no coverage drifts).
  **Design decisions worth keeping**: light glass needs MORE opacity than dark glass (.62→.78 vs .40→.70) and heavier blur (32 vs 24px) — dark translucency is forgiving because everything behind it darkens toward the ink's ground, light translucency is not; blur always pairs with `saturate()` because a blurred backdrop loses chroma and reads grey without it; `--edge-highlight` stays WHITE in the light theme deliberately (it is the specular/refraction colour, and a highlight is where light hits regardless of scheme); panes are frosted but cards inside them stay outline-only — stacking frosted on frosted is what turns glassmorphism to mud. All recorded in tokens-spec.md's new glass-light ◆ and Material sections.
  **Verification pitfall worth recording**: the first cross-theme check mutated `data-theme` in place and read `getComputedStyle(el, '::before')`, which reported a stale `.14` specular opacity in ALL FOUR themes — including the two that must be 0. The token cascade itself read correctly (`0`/`0`/`.14`/`.22`), so the mismatch was a pseudo-element style-recalc artifact of in-place attribute mutation, not a bug. Confirmed by re-testing with real page loads per theme (ops-dark: `material: none`, `beforeOpacity: 0`, gradient transparent). Do not trust in-place theme flips for pseudo-element assertions — navigate.
  Gate: `lint:tokens` clean (theme parity 38 tokens × 4 files), `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, live-verified per theme via real page loads (material/spec/ink/color-scheme computed values all correct, pointer tracking confirmed moving the gradient centre to 25%/60%), `test:visual` regenerated for the new 4-theme matrix — **161/161 passed** on the confirming clean run.
  Two transient failures during the 12-minute regeneration run (`settlement-anomaly — glass`, `audit-status-alert — glass`, both 30s timeouts) were checked rather than waved off, since `backdrop-filter` is genuinely GPU-expensive and a perf regression was a plausible cause: re-run in isolation they render in ~3s each, an order of magnitude inside the timeout, so the cause was load contention during regeneration, not the material. Same "suspect the environment before the code, but verify it" discipline as this session's earlier flake.
  Note: the 6 `stress-test-dense` failures that had been failing all session (and were reproduced on unmodified master earlier, so genuinely pre-existing) now PASS. Not claimed as a fix — nothing in this change targets them; most likely the same environmental contention, now resolved. Recorded because a previously-red test going green without an intended cause is worth knowing about.
  Open items for Prem: (a) specular intensity `--spec-alpha` (.14 dark / .22 light) and (b) `--canvas-backdrop` placement/intensity are both taste dials set to defensible defaults, not architect-approved values — both are single-token changes.
- **Glass follow-up: four reported defects, three of them one root cause (2026-08-07).** Direct feedback against the live glass theme, `merlin-theme-token` skill governing. Every symptom measured live before any edit.
  **Root cause behind three of the four — translucent `--surface-1` silently broke opaque consumers.** When the glass themes gave `--surface-1` a .40 alpha the day before, every consumer that had been *relying* on that token being opaque kept compiling and kept passing tests, but started painting a see-through layer. Three separate reported symptoms, one cause:
  1. *"Table assets first column has different colour, shouldn't."* Astryx's `useTableStickyColumns` paints the pinned column with `--table-sticky-background`, which `EntityAssetTable.module.css` had set to `--surface-1` — with a comment explicitly calling it "an opaque base background." The sticky cell paints ON TOP of the pane, which was already painting that same .40 layer, so the two composited to roughly .64 and the column read visibly darker than its own row. A sticky column exists to occlude what scrolls under it, so translucency there is wrong by definition in every theme.
  2. *"Tooltip on dark mode text too pale."* Astryx's Tooltip is an inverted surface — chip in the ink colour, TEXT in a surface colour. Correct while surfaces are opaque; once `--surface-1` had alpha, the tooltip's own text became 40%-transparent. Measured on a real rendered tooltip, not inferred: `color: oklch(.19 .015 250 / .4)` on `background: oklch(.93 .01 250)`. The class is StyleX-generated so it is not greppable as a literal — resolved by reading the live computed value instead of guessing which variable to override.
  3. *"Page panes e.g. Discover has not got bg like artifacts."* A .40 alpha that reads as a crisp frosted chip at card scale reads as a barely-there wash across a ~1460px pane.
  **Fix — two new semantic tokens rather than four scattered patches**: `--surface-1-solid` (opaque twin of `--surface-1`, for anything that must OCCLUDE rather than tint) and `--pane-surface` / `--pane-surface-solid` (a full-height page pane's fill, distinct from a card's, plus the value it composites to). Matte themes point both at `--surface-1`, so their rendering is byte-identical; only the glass themes carry real values (`.62`/`.78` pane alpha, tuned for pane-sized area). Tooltip override logged in `astryx-bridge.md` with the original value and reasoning, per the skill's protocol.
  **Cascade subtlety worth recording**: setting `--table-sticky-background` on `.animatedTable` *beat* the pane's own value, because the table is the closer ancestor. Caught live (sticky resolved to `.19` when the pane was `.62`). Rewritten as `var(--pane-surface-solid, var(--surface-1-solid))` so the pane's value wins inside a pane and the bare token remains the fallback anywhere this table is used standalone.
  **Fourth defect, a genuine layout bug, not a token one**: *"New investigation all 3 boxes should always match to same height (largest)."* Measured 148/168/148px. Astryx's `Grid` already stretches its children to the tallest row item, but each card is wrapped in LandingState's `.fade` div (the stagger/collapse animation layer), so the stretch stopped at the wrapper and the card inside sized to its own text. One rule in `LandingState.module.css` (`.fade > .astryx-card { height: 100% }`) passes it through; nothing sets a height, so the tallest card still defines the row. Now 168/168/168.
  **Correction made mid-investigation**: an early probe reported ordinary table cells as opaque white, which I first read as an unbridged Astryx variable. Grouping all 347 cells by computed background showed the 12 white ones were Storybook's own docs table, not the asset table at all (the real table's non-pinned cells are transparent). Re-scoped the query with `.closest('[class*="animatedTable"]')` before drawing any conclusion — the kind of "assertion that passes for the wrong reason" the guardrails warn about.
  Gate: `lint:tokens` clean (theme parity 41 tokens × 4 files), `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, all four themes verified live (matte pane/sticky values byte-identical to before; glass values the intended composite), `test:visual` regenerated — 161/161.
  **Re-reported same day — the sticky-column seam was NOT fixed by the above, and the reason is the lesson.** `--pane-surface-solid` was supposed to equal what the pane composites to. I *reasoned* about that composite instead of measuring it and wrote `oklch(.22 .017 250)`. Verification then checked that the token resolved to the value I intended — which it did — so the gate passed and the defect shipped. Prem reported it a second time with a screenshot.
  Root cause of the miss: I verified the declaration, not the rendered result. A translucent fill (`--pane-surface`, .62 alpha) laid over a multi-stop gradient backdrop and then blurred cannot be composited in your head; the only authority is the painted pixel. Sampling the actual screenshot showed the column at rgb(21,27,34) against rows at rgb(9,16,25) — about twelve 8-bit steps, plainly visible, exactly what was reported.
  Fix: sample a real table row inside a real pane from a screenshot, convert back to oklch, use that. Dark glass `.22 .017 250` → `.171 .022 255`; glass-light `.975 .005 250` → `.969 .006 255` (that one was off by only ~2 steps, corrected anyway — same bug, not "close enough"). Both token comments now state the values are MEASURED, show the failure mode, and say to re-measure if `--pane-surface`, `--canvas-backdrop`, or `--surface-blur` changes, since all three feed the composite.
  Verification method changed accordingly: pixel-sampled both sides of the column boundary at five different row heights per theme. Result Δ=0–2/255 dark, Δ=0–1/255 light, and the residual is the canvas gradient varying down the page rather than a step at the boundary. **Standing lesson: for any token whose correct value is a COMPOSITE of other layers, the verification must sample rendered pixels — asserting the token equals the value you chose is circular and will pass while the defect is on screen.**
  Also fixed in passing: `lint:tokens` correctly rejected my first draft of these comments, because writing `rgb(9,16,25)` in prose trips the raw-value grep. Reworded to "8-bit steps" — the gate is right to be that strict, and the comment reads better without the literals.
- **Asset column pinning removed (2026-08-07).** Direct feedback, small scope: "remove pinning from asset col." `EntityAssetTable.tsx` dropped `useTableStickyColumns` entirely (import + hook call + the `plugins={{ stickyColumns }}` prop) — the Asset column now scrolls with the rest of the row instead of staying fixed at the start edge.
  This retires the entire class of bug the last two entries were fixing: `--table-sticky-background` had no more consumers once the plugin was gone, so removed the declarations that fed it (`EntityAssetTable.module.css`'s `.animatedTable` block, `PageShell.module.css`'s `.pane` declaration). `--pane-surface-solid` — the token whose measured value took two rounds to get right — lost its only consumer and was removed outright (all 4 theme files) rather than left declared-but-dead, per the no-dead-code rule. `--surface-1-solid` was kept: its own comment already framed it generically ("sticky table columns, and anything else that scrolls content underneath itself"), so it remains real, reusable architecture for the next thing that needs an opaque-occlusion twin of a translucent surface — the tooltip fix from the same session already uses it independently, confirming that's a real second consumer and not a hypothetical one. Comments referencing the removed pin (`EntityAssetTableCells.tsx`'s Asset-cell comment, `astryx-bridge.md`'s tooltip entry) corrected rather than left stale.
  Gate: `lint:tokens` clean (theme parity dropped 41→40 tokens, confirming the removal registered identically across all 4 files), `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, live-verified (`position: static` on the Asset cell, confirming no pin), `test:visual` regenerated (real layout change to the table).
- **Glass restraint pass + cross-theme neumorphic shading + side-edge glow (2026-08-07).** Direct architect order after a reference-comparison discussion (Raycast screenshot → Linear screenshots): pull glass back from Raycast's saturated/dramatic register toward Linear's restraint, and separately add Linear's subtle neumorphic surface shading to every theme's opaque chrome (confirmed in scope via AskUserQuestion — every theme, not glass-only). Scoped via `merlin-theme-token` skill before touching any file, per the skill's own protocol (locate tier → change protocol → verify), not implemented straight from the request.
  **Glass restraint** (`theme.glass.css`/`theme.glass-light.css` only): `--surface-material`'s saturate step `--saturate-180` → `--saturate-140` in dark glass (glass-light was already at `-140`); `--spec-alpha` roughly halved in both (.14→.08 dark, .22→.12 light); `--canvas-backdrop` chroma cut by roughly half in both, same two-pool-plus-gradient structure retained (the earlier finding — a uniform backdrop blurs to itself and reads flat — didn't change, only how saturated the pools are). All three anchored to the Linear reference specifically, superseding the Raycast-anchored values from two days prior; still explicitly TASTE DIALS per their own in-file comments, now flagged as re-anchored rather than re-derived from first principles.
  **New cross-theme primitive** (`tokens.base.css`): `--shading-raised`/`--shading-pressed`, paired inset box-shadows (light-top/dark-bottom, and the inverse) built from two new semantic tokens per theme, `--shading-light`/`--shading-dark`. Values: default `oklch(1 0 0/.5)` / `oklch(0 0 0/.06)` (white-based, since the surface is near-white); ops-dark `oklch(1 0 0/.06)` / `oklch(1 0 0/.02)` (both white-based — black would vanish against an already-near-black surface); glass `oklch(1 0 0/.04)` / `oklch(0 0 0/.10)` and glass-light `oklch(1 0 0/.10)` / `oklch(.24 .012 250/.06)` — both deliberately near-zero, not `none`, so the same unconditional rule works in every theme rather than each consumer needing a theme check (reasoning: stacking a second soft-light cue on an already-translucent, already-blurred surface reads as mud, confirmed by comparing the Linear and glass references side by side rather than assumed).
  **Consumers, confirmed to carry no existing box-shadow before adding** (checked live via `getComputedStyle`, not assumed): `.astryx-list-item[aria-selected='true']:not(:hover)` (selected-row cue, distinct from the pre-existing hover rule's flat background wash; the `:not(:hover)` guard is a readability note, not functional — box-shadow paints under an opaque background regardless), `.astryx-button:not(:disabled)` (every variant, unconditional — the cue is surface texture, not which action a button represents), `PageShell.module.css`'s `.pane` and `ArtifactStack.module.css`'s `.artifactCard` (pane-scale version of the same cue, same rule applied to both so the two panes read as one surface class).
  **New token + consumer, side-edge glow** (`--edge-glow`, `none` in matte themes — a flat `--edge` border already does a divider's job there, real paired box-shadow in both glass themes): applied to `#astryx-app-shell-main` (AppShell's own stable content-area id, confirmed live to carry neither border nor shadow before adding) — the sidebar/content seam. Distinct from `--edge-highlight`, which is top-edge-only ("light catching a raised lip"); this is the same phenomenon on a vertical seam, per the Linear reference ("note this border effect that side has light kind of thing").
  Live-verified per-theme via computed styles on all four consumers simultaneously (not sequential eyeballing) — every value resolved exactly as authored: `--edge-glow: none` and zero visible main-shadow in default/ops-dark, real paired shadow in glass/glass-light; `--spec-alpha`/`--surface-material` confirmed at the new restrained values; button/row shading present in all four at the intended per-theme alpha. Visual screenshots (glass, glass-light) confirm the calmer register — near-monochrome backdrop, no strong specular sweep, consistent with the Linear reference rather than the prior Raycast-anchored look.
  `tokens-spec.md`'s Material section gained three new subsections (Restraint pass, Neumorphic shading, Side-edge glow) rather than folding into the existing prose, since these are three separable additions with three separable rationales. `astryx-bridge.md` logged both new consumers with their pre-change baseline.
  Gate: `lint:tokens` clean (43 tokens × 4 files, up from 40 — 3 new: `--shading-light`, `--shading-dark`, `--edge-glow`), `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, `test:visual` regenerated and confirmed via a second clean run — 161/161 both times.
- **Sidebar footer border/margin + collapsed-rail brand mark (2026-08-08).** Two small direct-feedback fixes, same session.
  **Footer border/margin**: Astryx's `SideNav` wraps the `footer` prop in its own internal div carrying a hairline `border-top` in its own compiled StyleX output (confirmed live via `getComputedStyle` before touching anything — no prop suppresses it). Cleared and given the requested bottom margin via `:global(.astryx-side-nav) > div:has(> .footerStack)` in `Sidebar.module.css`, reaching the wrapper (no stable class of its own) through the one stable ancestor class plus the one child class already under our control. Real bug caught and fixed mid-task: the first draft wrote `.astryx-side-nav` as a bare selector inside the `.module.css` file — CSS Modules scope every bare class by default, so it silently compiled to a locally-hashed class matching nothing on the page (confirmed by inspecting the actual compiled stylesheet, not assumed from the source). Fixed with `:global(...)`; logged in `astryx-bridge.md` as a standing gotcha for any future global-class reference from inside a module file.
  **Collapsed-rail brand mark**: `SideNavHeading`'s own source (read directly, not guessed) shows `heading` text disappears entirely when collapsed with no fallback, but its `icon` prop is explicitly built for exactly this case (`isCollapsed && icon` → icon-only render; own doc example: `<SideNavHeading icon={<AppIcon />} heading="My App" />`). New `BrandMark.tsx`/`.module.css`: tries `/logo.svg` (confirmed live it doesn't exist yet — only unrelated placeholder marks under `public/logos/`), `onError` falls back to a plain letter-M glyph in `--ink-primary`, same `onError`-state pattern `AssetLogo.tsx` already established for the identical "asset may not exist" case elsewhere in this codebase, chosen over inventing a new one. No code change will be needed when a real `logo.svg` is added — confirmed via AskUserQuestion this was the wanted behavior now rather than blocking on the asset.
  Live-verified across all four themes: border/margin identical in every theme (0px border, 16px margin), brand-mark fallback present and correctly following `--ink-primary`'s per-theme value (light ink in the three dark-register themes, dark ink in glass-light).
  Gate: `lint:tokens` clean (unchanged token count — no new tokens, pure component/module work), `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, `test:visual` regenerated (real layout change to the sidebar footer + a new element in the collapsed rail, present in every fixture showing the shell).
  **Same-day correction**: reported live — the fallback M rendered ALONGSIDE "Merlin" in the expanded rail ("M sign should not be in front of logo in expanded pane"), not just in collapsed mode. Root cause: `SideNavHeading` pairs `icon` + `heading` side by side whenever both are given (confirmed in its own source — this is its documented behavior, the doc's own example shows exactly this pairing for a genuine product icon), so passing a truthy `<BrandMark />` element as `icon` triggered that pairing unconditionally, regardless of what `BrandMark` internally rendered to. `BrandMark` now takes `isCollapsed` and returns `null` when expanded, rather than fighting `SideNavHeading`'s designed pairing behavior from the caller side — `Sidebar.tsx` already tracked `isCollapsed` locally, so this is a one-line prop thread, not new state. Confirmed live both states: expanded shows only "Merlin" with no stray gap (the `icon &&` wrapper `<span>` still mounts since the passed element itself is truthy, but wraps nothing visible), collapsed still shows the M. `test:visual` regenerated again as part of the same baseline-refresh cycle below.

Prem sent a second, clearer Linear screenshot and re-questioned the token directly ("why does this have edge glow?") — re-examining that reference under direct scrutiny showed no visible sidebar/content seam at all in Linear's own UI; the panel reads as one continuous surface, and the only edges present anywhere in the screenshot are around its floating AI panel (a genuinely different pattern — a detached floating object, not a divider). The original read was a misinterpretation, not a taste call to walk back.
  Removed cleanly: the token declaration in all four theme files (real value in `glass`/`glass-light`, `none` in `default`/`ops-dark`), and its one consumer (`#astryx-app-shell-main`'s `box-shadow` rule in both glass files) — `#astryx-app-shell-main` is back to only its `background-color` rule. `tokens-spec.md`'s Material section and `astryx-bridge.md`'s consumer log both updated to record the retraction and why, rather than silently deleting the paper trail.

  **Root cause 1 — a real regression, not a taste call.** `.astryx-card.panelProvisional` in all four theme files carried `border: 1px solid var(--edge)` — byte-identical to `.panelFlat`. Checked against STATE.md's own 2026-07-29 ratified design ("Panel elevation fix" entry): panelProvisional was specified as "completely transparent (no background, no border)," genuinely distinct from panelFlat's outline-only treatment. The border had silently drifted back in at some point without a corresponding STATE.md entry recording the change — caught only because the visual result (Recommended pane reading as a bordered box, same weight as the confidence Panel above it) contradicted the written design intent. Fixed to `border: none` in all four theme files, restoring the original ratification.
  **Root cause 2 — a real inconsistency, two separate components.** `SceneSummary.module.css`'s `.kicker` (used for "Reasoning"/"Recommended" inline in SceneSummary) hardcoded `color: var(--accent-signal)`, while `Panel`'s own title (the confidence pane directly above it, same scene) renders via plain `<Text type="label">` ink — two different heading treatments stacked in one node. Checking for a sibling instance turned up a SECOND occurrence: the standalone `Recommendation` registry node (`Recommendation.tsx`, a different component, not called by SceneSummary) hardcoded `<Text type="label" color="accent">Recommended</Text>` the same way. Both fixed to plain ink — `SceneSummary.module.css`'s `.kicker` → `--ink-secondary`, `Recommendation.tsx`'s label → `Text`'s own default (no `color` prop). Node-vocabulary.md's register principle (accent reserved for real signal, not routine labels) already argued against both; this makes the code match the principle.
  Live-verified across all four themes via computed styles on both fixes simultaneously: kicker color correctly resolves to `--ink-secondary` per theme, card border `0px none` and background transparent in every theme, no drift.
  Gate: `lint:tokens` clean (no token count change — value edits only), `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, `test:visual` regenerated twice (once for the theme-file border fix, again after the `Recommendation.tsx` fix was found and applied) — full run confirmed clean.

Root cause: Portfolio and Risk are static `DashboardPage` routes (Phase 13, no `SceneRenderer`), whose header renders through `DashboardSummaryHeader.tsx` — a THIRD, separate component from both `SceneSummary` and `Recommendation`, so neither earlier fix reached it. It had never received the `panelFlat`/`panelProvisional` treatment at all (`<Card variant="default" padding={4}>`, no class — Astryx's bare raised-card default) and carried the same `color="accent"` label. Fixed identically: `panelProvisional` class added, label dropped to plain `<Text type="label">`. One fix covers both Portfolio and Risk pages, since they share this same component.
  Live-verified on both pages via computed styles: border `0px none`, background transparent, `panelProvisional` present, label color resolves to `--ink-primary` per theme on both `/portfolio-dashboard` and `/risk-dashboard`.
  Standing note: this is the third of three independent components carrying the identical two-part bug (bordered/backgrounded provisional content + hardcoded accent label) — SceneSummary's inline kicker, the standalone Recommendation node, and now this static-page header. No fourth consumer found on inspection, but worth a `grep -rn 'color="accent"'` sweep if a fourth is ever reported rather than assuming this is truly the last one.
  Gate: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, `test:visual` regenerated and reconfirmed via a second clean run.
- **Two-tier panel system retired; page-section consistency ruling (2026-08-08).** Direct architect order, reversing (not correcting a drift in) the 2026-07-29 "permanent facts bordered, provisional reasoning borderless" design — reaffirmed as recently as this same session's earlier entry. Confirmed via AskUserQuestion before touching anything, given the scope: "each section must go with that pane that is using same class so we can ensure consistency." Also folded in three smaller, related fixes reported in the same message.
  **Architecture change**: `panelProvisional` retired entirely — merged into `panelFlat` (all four theme files, comment-only trace left explaining the retirement and citing the reversal). `SceneSummary.tsx`'s Summary/Recommendation cards and `DashboardSummaryHeader.tsx` now pass `panelFlat`. `node-vocabulary.md` principle 2's "visual mechanism" paragraph rewritten to match: the fact/reasoning register distinction is now enforced through typography alone (three-voice face + kicker label), never chrome — logged as SUPERSEDED with the prior text preserved in the diff, not silently overwritten.

(2) `asset-discovery.scene.json`'s three summary metrics (Candidates above 6% / Reference — NordBond 2029 / Highest yield) had never authored `size: "compact"` — the `metric` node's own prop schema has supported it since Phase 8E, this is simply the first scene JSON to need it; added to all three, matching `EntityStatistics.tsx`'s own compact treatment on the entity detail page.

Removed the divider render, the now-unused `Divider` import, and updated the stale row-anatomy comment that documented it. Rows still read distinctly via spacing/hover alone (confirmed live).

Confirmed via `lsof`/`ps` before touching anything, asked before killing another project's process (approved), stopped the Tracr instance, then had to restart Merlin's own Storybook too since it didn't auto-retry the bind once freed. `curl .../index.json` used to positively confirm which project's stories were being served before and after, rather than assuming from a screenshot alone.

- **Workbench pane sizing switched to fixed ratios (2026-08-09).** Direct architect order, reported live against a real screenshot: opening chat + an artifact from Discover produced visible overlap between the transcript pane's reasoning-trail text and the artifact panel's own content. Confirmed via AskUserQuestion before implementing, given the ambiguity between two contradictory statements in the original ask (50/50 for 2 panes vs. "artifact/chat defaults to 33%") — resolved as: transcript and artifact each claim a fixed 33% share whenever open; content always takes whatever's left (content alone = 100%, content + one side pane = 67/33, content + both side panes = 34/33/33). Also confirmed: replace the ACTIVE sizing mechanism, but leave the resizable-drag code in place, dormant, for a future re-enable — not deleted.
  **Root-cause investigation, not assumed**: read `Frame.module.css`, `usePaneVisibility.ts`/`usePaneFitCollapse.ts`, `ArtifactStackMount.tsx`, and `ArtifactStack.tsx` fully before concluding anything. The prior sizing was NOT a percentage system at all — `.contentColumn` was `flex: 1 1 auto` (grow-to-fill), `.transcriptPane` was `flex: 1 1 480px` (shrinkable from a fixed basis), and the artifact pane's width was driven entirely by `ArtifactStack.tsx`'s `useResizable` hook via an inline `width={resizable.size}` on its Card — three independent, uncoordinated sizing strategies sharing one flex row, with no floor-based collapse triggering until real overflow. That's a plausible mechanism for the reported overlap, though the exact frame it occurred on wasn't captured live (see the correction below).
  **Change**: `Frame.module.css` — `.contentColumn` → `flex: 1 1 0%` (grow to fill, no fixed basis); `.transcriptPane` → `flex: 0 1 33%` (fixed basis, no grow); new `.artifactPane` class, same `flex: 0 1 33%`, `min-width: 360px` (the generic `ARTIFACT_PANE_FLOOR`). `ArtifactStackMount.tsx` now applies `layout.artifactPane` as its wrapper's className (previously unclassed, width driven entirely by an inline `minWidth: sceneFloor` — that inline style is KEPT, but now only raises the floor above the class's generic 360px for scenes needing more, e.g. `comparison`/`concentration-map`, never drives the active width). `ArtifactStack.tsx`'s `Card` no longer receives `width={resizable.size}` — only `width: '100%'` when maximized (a genuinely separate state); `useResizable`/`ResizeHandle`/the `stackWidth` store-sync effect are all still wired and still functional (dragging still visually resizes the handle and updates the store), they're just no longer the source the Card's actual rendered width reads from — a deliberate no-op preserved for a future re-enable, per direct order.
  **Live-verified via computed styles, not just a screenshot**: 1-pane (content alone, no side panes) confirmed via the `flex: 1 1 0%` mechanics (untested edge case, follows directly from the CSS, not separately reproduced); 2-pane content+transcript confirmed live at exactly 67%/33% of row width; 3-pane content+artifact+transcript (the exact scenario from the bug report — Discover → chat → NordBond 2029 investigation) confirmed live at 32%/33%/33%, and a DOM walk found zero descendant elements crossing outside their own pane's box (no overlap in the settled state).
  **Self-correction, logged rather than silently fixed**: my own first look at the reproduced 3-pane screenshot read it as still overlapping — restated that as fact before actually checking. The DOM measurement taken in the same turn already showed clean, non-overlapping boxes; re-examining the same screenshot against that measurement showed three genuinely distinct columns with no text bleed. The initial "still broken" claim was a misread, corrected in the same turn before reporting anything to the architect — worth remembering: a first visual impression of a screenshot is not itself verification once a DOM measurement is already in hand that contradicts it.
  Gate: `lint:tokens` clean (no token change — flex/class values only), `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, `test:visual` regenerated (11 shell.spec.ts baselines changed, exactly the multi-pane investigation-flow screenshots, as expected) and reconfirmed via a second clean run.
  Open item, not actioned: CLAUDE.md's Phase 18 section still describes "proportional/shrinkable with a real floor" as the tier-2 responsive design — this change supersedes that description for the CURRENT (desktop, ≥1024px) sizing behavior specifically, not the <1024px responsive-tier work Phase 18 actually scopes. Flagging the terminology drift rather than editing CLAUDE.md myself (law file, not a session log).
- **Sticky title bar bleed-through fixed — same root cause, third and fourth consumers (2026-08-09).** Reported live with a screenshot: the artifact panel's sticky title bar ("NordBond 2029 — Settlement Failure Investigation") visibly showed scrolled-past content bleeding through it once the panel was scrolled. Initially worth distinguishing from the pane-width overlap fixed earlier the same day — this is a genuinely different bug (translucency, not layout).
  **Reproduced and diagnosed live before touching anything**: opened chat, ran the NordBond investigation, scrolled the artifact pane's own content, confirmed via `elementsFromPoint` that z-index/stacking order was actually correct (ruled out a stacking bug) — then checked the title bar's actual computed `background-image` and found `oklch(0.19 0.015 250 / 0.4)`: the gradient's "opaque" stop was only 40% alpha. Root cause: `ArtifactStack.module.css` and `PageShell.module.css` both set `--pane-title-bg: var(--surface-1)` — the SAME bug pattern as the sticky-table-column and tooltip fixes from 2026-08-07 (a consumer needing an OPAQUE surface silently inheriting a translucent one once glass shipped `--surface-1` with alpha), just two more consumers that fix never reached. Same ruling applied: a sticky element content scrolls under exists specifically to occlude it, so translucency there is wrong regardless of how translucent the pane's own resting body is elsewhere. Fixed both to `--surface-1-solid` (PageShell.module.css's own comment corrected in the same edit — it had also drifted to reference the now-retired `--pane-surface-solid` token).
  **Self-correction, same session**: mid-verification, my own screenshot showed the transcript pane's trail-step text sitting close to an ArtifactCard preview ("Ready" chip) and I nearly reported it as a second overlap bug. This time checked geometry FIRST with precise leaf-node selectors before concluding anything (an earlier same-day mistake used an imprecise selector that matched a whole scroll container instead of the actual text row, producing a false-positive "overlap"): the real elements had a clean ~8px gap between them, zero geometric overlap — visual crowding at that screenshot's zoom level, not a bug. Not reported as a fix because there was nothing to fix.
  Live-verified via computed styles across all four themes simultaneously: `--pane-title-bg` resolves to a fully opaque value in every theme (no `/ N` alpha suffix) — `rgb(255,255,255)` in default, `oklch(0.19 0.015 250)` in ops-dark/glass, `oklch(0.97 0.004 250)` in glass-light.
  Gate: `lint:tokens` clean (no token count change — value/comment edits only), `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, `test:visual` regenerated.
- **Resize handle hidden + gap asymmetry root-caused as the same thing (2026-08-09).** Direct feedback, two asks bundled together: "hide the resize hand from artifacts" and "ensure gaps between pane are same." Measured before touching anything: the row's own `gap: var(--space-16)` was ALREADY numerically identical on both sides (16px/16px, confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect`) — the perceived asymmetry was `ArtifactStack.tsx`'s `<ResizeHandle>` pill (16px wide, `left:746→762` at one test width) rendering visually inside the content↔artifact gap only, with nothing occupying the artifact↔transcript gap the same way. One fix closes both: removed `<ResizeHandle>`'s JSX (and its now-unused `ResizeHandle` import) — `useResizable`/the `stackWidth` store-sync effect stay fully wired, per the standing "keep the mechanism, hide the affordance" order from earlier the same day. Re-enabling is one JSX block away, documented inline. `Frame.module.css`'s own header comment (which explicitly named the ResizeHandle as living independently inside that gap) updated to match — it would have read as false/stale documentation otherwise.
  **Third item, reported as "still... overlap of chatpane" — investigated thoroughly, could not reproduce.** Given two of three prior "overlap" reports this session turned out to be misreads once checked against real DOM geometry (the transcript/artifact-card proximity, caught before reporting; a screenshot glance that a same-turn measurement already contradicted), treated this one with the same discipline rather than pattern-matching to "another overlap bug" and guessing a fix blind. Swept multiple viewport widths (1738px down to 1300px, spanning from ample headroom through the fit-collapse threshold), forced a restore→re-collapse cycle, and ran a systematic cross-pane bounding-box overlap check (not eyeballing) at each step — found zero genuine overlaps post the same-day title-bar-opacity fix; `usePaneFitCollapse` correctly collapses the least-recently-active pane before any real overflow occurs, and the restore chip (mis-identified as absent in an early check due to a wrong CSS selector — corrected before concluding anything) works as designed. Reported this honestly as "could not reproduce, needs a fresh repro" rather than either declaring it fixed or shipping a speculative change against an unconfirmed bug.
  Live-verified the two shipped fixes: handle absent from DOM (`aria-label="Resize artifact stack"` query returns null), both gaps measured at exactly 16px with no visual occupant in either.
