- **Auto-collapse disabled + all three panes unified to one shared floor (2026-08-09).** Direct architect order, third pane-layout report in one day — this time with a screenshot showing genuine overlap at a real narrow width (sidebar collapsed), correctly self-diagnosed by the architect as a min-width mismatch: content/artifact/transcript had three DIFFERENT floors (420/360/320px), so at intermediate row widths the smallest-floor pane (transcript) got squeezed while its neighbours were still shrinking toward their own larger floors — real overlap, not a misread this time. Confirmed via AskUserQuestion before touching anything, since two genuinely separate decisions were bundled: (1) disable `usePaneFitCollapse` entirely ("disable this mechanism and keep the code for later" — same treatment as the ResizeHandle removal earlier the same day), and (2) unify all three floors to one shared value ("should be all three the same"). Both confirmed.
  **Disable, not delete**: `usePaneVisibility.ts` no longer calls `usePaneFitCollapse(rowRef, collapseCandidates)` — the call site removed, `usePaneFitCollapse.ts` itself untouched. `rowRef` stays returned/attached to the row DOM node regardless (Frame.tsx's own ref target either way). `collapsedPane` now always reads its own store default (`null`, since nothing calls `setCollapsedPane` anymore) — `showTranscript`/`showArtifact` correctly reduce to `naturalShowTranscript`/`naturalShowStack` directly, which is the intended behaviour, not a side effect to work around. `PaneFitCollapseHarness.tsx` (its own dedicated Storybook-only test surface, explicitly documented as "not a product surface") still calls the hook directly, so `paneFitCollapse.spec.ts` is unaffected by disabling it in the real app — confirmed by grep before assuming so.
  **Unified floor**: `paneFloors.ts`'s `TRANSCRIPT_PANE_FLOOR`(320)/`ARTIFACT_PANE_FLOOR`(360) collapsed into one `PANE_FLOOR = 420` — the HIGHEST of the three previous values, not a new/lower number picked freely. Reasoning recorded in both `paneFloors.ts` and `Frame.module.css`: content's 420px floor had its own live-verified legibility rationale on record ("keeps EntityDetailPage's own 2-column reflow legible at the floor") — lowering it to match the smaller panes would reopen a settled question, raising the two smaller panes up to the already-verified number does not. `Frame.module.css`'s `.contentColumn`/`.transcriptPane`/`.artifactPane` all now read `min-width: 420px`; `ArtifactStack.tsx`/`ArtifactStackMount.tsx` updated to import/consume `PANE_FLOOR` (their own per-scene `getSceneMinWidth` raise-above-floor mechanism for wide nodes like comparison/concentration-map is unchanged, just now raises above the new shared base).
  Live-verified at two widths, not just the failure point: 1150px (below the shared floor, sidebar collapsed, matching the reported scenario) — all three panes measured at EXACTLY 420px each, zero overlap, row correctly extends past the viewport (scrollable) instead of compressing panes into each other; 1450px (above the floor) — proportional 34/33/33 split still holds (434/452/452), confirming the normal case is unaffected.
  Gate: `lint:tokens` clean, `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, `test:visual` regenerated.
- **"Pop"/"no-pop" two-variant panel chrome system (2026-08-10).** Direct architect order, same session as the pane-layout fixes above: page panes should get a thin neumorphic outline, "slightly stronger [outline], inner weaker [shading]," plus a formal two-variant system — "pop" (outline + shadow, feels raised) for page panes and named "outer panes" (chat, artifacts, portfolio, entity detail container), vs. "no-pop" (outline only, no shadow — the existing `panelFlat` treatment, explicitly kept unchanged as-is).
  **New primitive**: `tokens.base.css`'s `--shading-pop-raised`, derived via `color-mix(in srgb, var(--shading-light/-dark) 65%, transparent)` from the EXISTING `--shading-light`/`--shading-dark` semantic tokens rather than a second set of raw per-theme values — any future retune of the base shading pair propagates automatically, and the two registers (raised vs. pop-raised) can never drift apart the way two independently-authored value sets eventually would. Weaker than `--shading-raised` on purpose: once an outline is doing the job of defining a pane's edge, the inset no longer has to carry that weight alone.
  **Three consumers, one architecture decision**: applied per-consumer (not one universal CSS class) because `.pane` (a plain div) and `.artifactCard` (an Astryx `Card` with a specificity-matched `.artifactCard:global(.astryx-card)[data-variant='default']` override) are structurally different selector types — a shared class would either under- or over-specify depending which it targeted. All three now carry `border: 1px solid var(--edge)` (the SAME token `panelFlat`'s outline already uses — one edge language at two strengths, not a second colour) + `box-shadow: var(--shading-pop-raised)`:
    - `PageShell.module.css`'s `.pane` — was shadow-only (`--shading-raised`, no border); now border added, shadow weakened. Covers page panes AND, transitively, Portfolio/Risk (`DashboardPage.tsx`) and Entity Detail (`EntityDetailPage.tsx`) — both already render through `PageShell`, confirmed by reading each file, so no separate change was needed for "portfolio" or "detail container."
    - `ArtifactStack.module.css`'s `.artifactCard` — border was explicitly zeroed by a 2026-08-02 ruling ("fill-only, instead of outline"); that ruling is superseded, not accidentally reversed — logged as such in the file's own comment, citing this order. Border re-added using this codebase's own `--edge` token, never Astryx's own unbridged default border colour.
    - `Frame.module.css`'s `.transcriptPane` — the one genuinely new judgment call, flagged as such rather than presented as settled: this pane had ZERO visual chrome before (pure flex-sizing box, content painted directly against the app background). Given `background-color: var(--pane-surface)` (the same token `.pane` uses, chosen for consistency with the other full-height/page-scale pane, over `.artifactCard`'s smaller-scale `--surface-1`) plus the same border+shadow pair, plus `border-radius: var(--radius-12)` + `overflow: hidden` so the rounding is real. `backdrop-filter` deliberately NOT added — no glass material assigned to this pane, unlike `.pane`/`.artifactCard`.
  **Live-verified in all four themes** via computed-style checks (not screenshot-only) on all three consumers: default (border `1px rgba(255,255,255,0.1)`, box-shadow `.325`/`.039` alpha — exact 65% mixes of the base `.5`/`.06` shading values, confirming `color-mix()` resolved correctly in-browser, not just parsed), ops-dark (border `0.08` alpha matching theme file, shadow `.039`/`.013` — 65% of `.06`/`.02`), glass (transcript pane's new background/border/shadow all real and visible against the frosted register; artifact pane's restored border coexists cleanly with its own `blur(24px) saturate(1.4)` backdrop-filter, no conflict), glass-light (border `0.12` alpha, shadow `.065`/`.039` — 65% of `.10`/`.06`). A first screenshot glance at the glass-theme 3-pane layout looked like overlap between the artifact and transcript panes; a `getBoundingClientRect()` check in the same turn proved clean 16px gaps at every boundary (content ends 756.7, artifact 772.7–1270.4, transcript starts 1286.4) — corrected before reporting, per this session's established discipline of trusting DOM geometry over a screenshot glance.
  **Visual regression regeneration hit a real Playwright quirk, not a code bug**: `pnpm test:visual -- --update-snapshots --workers=1` left `shell.spec.ts`'s `shell-base-query-default` and `shell-unresolved-query-glass` baselines stale (dated Aug 3/Aug 9, never overwritten) despite the flag, while every other baseline in the same run updated fine — confirmed via file mtimes, not assumption. Root cause: Playwright's own stability-retry path (a screenshot taken once, found unstable, retaken 100ms later) does not always re-trigger the baseline-write step of `--update-snapshots` on the retry — a known edge case, not something this session's code changes caused. Fixed by deleting `shell.spec.ts-snapshots/*.png` outright and regenerating from zero (this session's established delete-then-regenerate pattern) rather than trusting a partial update.
  Gate: `lint:tokens` clean (one new primitive, present in the shared `tokens.base.css` tier so no per-theme parity entries needed), `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, `test:visual` full suite regenerated and reconfirmed via two consecutive clean runs (161/161 both times).
- **3-pane split changed from fixed-33%-basis to true equal-share (2026-08-10).** Reported live with two screenshots and a precise repro path: New investigation (Home) → submit query → close Artifacts → Discover → close Chat → open a NordBond entity page → open Chat → open Artifacts. Investigation had several false starts worth recording, not just the fix itself.
  **False leads ruled out first, each with live evidence**: (1) An initial claim of visual overlap at 1996px/1250px/narrower widths could not be reproduced across an exhaustive sweep — real clicks, JS-dispatched clicks, frame-by-frame polling during the open transition, both themes — every attempt showed clean 16px gaps. Confirmed via `lsof`/`curl .../index.json` that port 6006 was genuinely serving Merlin's own Storybook build (a second, unrelated Tracr Storybook process was present in `ps` output but not actually bound to the port), ruling out a stale-build explanation. (2) The architect's own repro produced a screenshot showing the artifact pane displaying a STALE, unrelated investigation ("Tokenized Assets..." instead of a NordBond-specific one) — traced to `artifactStore.ts`'s `toggleStack`, which by design only shows/hides the pane and never changes `openArtifactId` (its own comment: "this hides the pane, it doesn't discard which artifact was open"). Confirmed via AskUserQuestion this is correct, working-as-designed behavior, not a bug — no NordBond-specific investigation existed yet in that repro, so reopening Artifacts correctly showed the last real one. (3) A `getBoundingClientRect()` reading during this investigation found a `.astryx-base-table` extending ~40px past its own scroll wrapper — initially looked like a real bleed, but `elementsFromPoint()` at the actual boundary coordinate showed no paint-level overlap; a table's un-clipped bounding box naturally exceeds its `overflow-x: auto` scroll container's box in CSS (that's how horizontal table scrolling works) — a geometry reading that looked alarming but wasn't a visual bug, caught before reporting it as one.
  **Real bug, once repro'd precisely at the architect's own reported width (1800px)**: not overlap — the CONTENT pane rendered visibly narrower (481px) than the artifact/transcript panes (498px each) once all three were open, even though the gaps between them measured equal (16px). Root cause: the prior "fixed-ratio workbench split" (2026-08-09) gave content `flex: 1 1 0%` (grow-to-fill-remainder) while artifact/transcript each got `flex: 0 1 33%` (fixed basis) — its own comment admitted the 3-pane case lands at "34/33/33... not worth a calc()/:has() selector to close." Confirmed via AskUserQuestion that this WAS the reported issue, and that the fix should make every pane count use a true equal share rather than special-casing the 2-pane ratio (today's 67/33 content-favored split when only one side pane is open becomes 50/50 under the new rule — a deliberate, confirmed change, not a side effect).
  **Fix**: `Frame.module.css` — `.transcriptPane` and `.artifactPane` both changed from `flex: 0 1 33%` to `flex: 1 1 0%`, matching `.contentColumn`'s existing rule exactly. All three panes now carry the identical flex-grow/shrink/basis triple, so flexbox distributes remaining row space evenly across however many are open — an exact split, not an approximation, and no per-count special-casing needed.
  Live-verified at three widths: 1800px (the reported width) — all three panes now 491/491/493px (the 2px variance is `getSceneMinWidth` raising the artifact pane's own scene-specific floor slightly, not a layout bug), gaps still 16px; 1300px and 1150px (below/at the shared 420px floor) — all three panes clamp to exactly 420px each, no overlap, confirming the equal-share change didn't regress the earlier floor-unification fix. 2-pane case (Home, content+artifact only) confirmed at exactly 746/746px (50/50, the new confirmed rule).
  Gate: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, `test:visual` regenerated (`shell.spec.ts-snapshots` deleted and rebuilt from zero, 16 baselines across all 4 themes × 4 states) and reconfirmed via two consecutive clean runs (161/161 both times). `lint:tokens` currently fails on a pre-existing, already-uncommitted change to `theme.glass.css` (a raw hex value) that predates this session's work and was not touched here — flagged, not fixed, as outside this task's scope.
- **Per-scene artifact-pane floor raise removed (2026-08-10, same-day follow-up).** Architect hard-refreshed and confirmed the equal-split fix above worked at the originally reported width, then reported a DIFFERENT, more precise symptom on a different scene ("Tokenized Assets Above 6% Yield," which contains a `comparison` node): "gap between page pane and artifacts... too big... should be same as between artifacts and chat... some panes don't scale down lower than certain point... something blocking them... it stops at some point."
  **Root cause, confirmed live at 1600px**: `getSceneMinWidth` (Phase 18) raised the artifact pane's `min-width` to 480px for any scene containing a `comparison`/`concentration-map` node — 60px above the shared 420px floor every other pane used. Content and transcript both clamped to 420px while artifact clamped to its own higher 480px floor: pixel GAPS stayed equal (16px, confirmed) but pane WIDTHS didn't (420/480/420), and the wider artifact pane reading as visually unbalanced is exactly what the architect's own diagnosis described — a floor genuinely blocking one pane from shrinking in step with its neighbors.
  **Fix, confirmed via AskUserQuestion given a real tradeoff**: removed the per-scene floor mechanism entirely rather than raising all three panes together when one node needs more room — every pane now always uses the flat `PANE_FLOOR` (420px), full stop, matching the architect's own stated model ("each screen equal weight"). This reopens a previously-fixed legibility risk on record: `registry.ts`'s own comment (now deleted) documented 480px as `comparison`'s "verified true minimum," found after an earlier Phase 18 bug where a smaller floor caused real column clipping. Surfaced this tradeoff explicitly before deleting anything; architect confirmed removal anyway — equal-weight panes take priority over this one node's floor for now, and any future comparison/concentration-map cramping is a separate problem to solve (e.g. in-node reflow) rather than pane-width special-casing.
  **Changes**: `ArtifactStack.tsx` — `useResizable`'s `minSizePx` reverted from `getSceneMinWidth(activeArtifact?.scene, PANE_FLOOR)` to plain `PANE_FLOOR`; `sceneFamily`'s neighboring `getSceneMinWidth` import removed. `ArtifactStackMount.tsx` — the inline `minWidth: sceneFloor` style override removed entirely (the `.artifactPane` CSS class's own `min-width: 420px` is now the only floor, no per-mount override); `openArtifactId`/`artifacts` store reads and the `getSceneMinWidth` call removed since nothing in this component needs them anymore. `engine/sceneMinWidth.ts` deleted outright (orphaned — its only two call sites were both removed in this same change). `registry.ts` — the `RegistryEntry.minWidth?: number` field and its explanatory comment removed from the interface; `comparison` and `concentration-map`'s `minWidth: 480`/`minWidth: 400` entries (plus comparison's own verified-minimum comment) removed from their registrations.
  Live-verified at 1600px on the exact scene that showed the bug: artifact pane's computed `min-width` now reads `420px` (was `480px`), all three panes render at 425/425/427px (previously 420/480/420) — visually balanced, matching content and transcript. Scrolled to the `comparison` table ("Candidates compared") at this narrower width to check the legibility tradeoff directly: text renders fully legible (no clipping), the table simply has more columns than fit and scrolls horizontally within its own `overflow-x: auto` wrapper (the same scrollable-table mechanism confirmed correct earlier this session) — an acceptable, visible tradeoff rather than a silent regression.
  Gate: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, `test:visual` run twice with zero baseline changes (this scene/width combination isn't part of `shell.spec.ts`'s own fixed sequence, so the fix didn't touch any tracked screenshot) — 161/161 both times. `lint:tokens`'s pre-existing unrelated failure (`theme.glass.css`, not touched by this session) persists, same as the prior entry.
- **Content↔artifact gap double-counted PageShell's own page margin (2026-08-10, third same-day round on pane spacing).** Architect reported the gap symmetry issue a THIRD time, precisely: "content↔artifact gap bigger than artifact↔chat gap" — narrower and more specific than the prior two reports, which turned out to each be genuinely different bugs (unequal pane WIDTHS from a fixed-ratio split, then an unequal pane FLOOR from a per-scene minWidth raise). This one was a genuine third bug, not a residual of either earlier fix.
  **Root cause, found by walking the DOM chain between `.contentColumn` and the actual visible `.pane` element**: `PageShell.tsx` wraps every page's `.pane` in its own inline-styled div with `padding: 0 var(--space-24) var(--space-32)` — a real, intentional outer page margin (24px horizontal, 32px bottom), confirmed still wanted on its left side (mirrors the sidebar's own margin) and its bottom (matches the composer dock's own bottom padding, an existing direct-feedback rule). The RIGHT 24px, though, stacked on top of `Frame.module.css`'s own row `gap: 16px` whenever a side pane (artifact/transcript) sat next to content — giving content↔artifact a real 40px total while artifact↔transcript (neither of which carries this same page-margin wrapper) stayed at the row's plain 16px. Confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` on the actual `.pane`/`.artifactCard` elements, not the flex wrappers: gap literally measured 40px vs 16px, non-uniform padding stacking, not a rendering illusion.
  **Fix, confirmed via AskUserQuestion on the wiring approach given CSS Modules' per-file class hashing prevents a plain cross-file sibling selector**: `Frame.tsx` now sets a CSS custom property, `--content-has-sibling-pane` (1/0, not a boolean, so it plugs directly into a `calc()`), on `.row` itself — `showArtifact || showTranscript`, both already destructured there for `usePaneVisibility`. `PageShell.module.css` consumes it: `padding: 0 calc(var(--space-24) * (1 - var(--content-has-sibling-pane, 0))) var(--space-32)` — the right padding collapses to 0 exactly when a side pane is present (the row's own gap already separates them at that point), and stays the full 24px page margin when content is alone. Zero prop-threading through the ~8 page components that call `<PageShell>` — the variable inherits down the DOM tree from `.row` to `.contentColumn` to `PageShell`'s own root automatically, CSS custom properties' own default behavior.
  **Structural cleanup alongside the fix, not scope creep**: the padding lived on a bare inline-styled `div` inside `PageShell.tsx` (`style={{ display: 'flex', ..., padding: '0 var(--space-24) var(--space-32)' }}`) — CLAUDE.md's inline-style-for-positioning ban, pre-existing before this session but directly in the way of wiring the new conditional (a `calc()` needs to live in a real CSS rule, not an inline style string). Converted both of `PageShell.tsx`'s previously-inline-styled wrapper divs to real classes (`.root`, `.margin` in `PageShell.module.css`) as part of the same change, carrying their original comments forward rather than dropping the history they documented.
  Live-verified via `getBoundingClientRect()` on the real `.pane`/`.artifactCard` elements (not the flex wrappers) across three states: 3-pane (Discover content + artifact + chat) — both gaps now exactly 16px (was 40px/16px); 2-pane (Home content + artifact only) — 16px; 0-side-pane (content alone) — right margin correctly still the full 24px, `--content-has-sibling-pane` correctly reads `0`. One screenshot confirmed visually symmetric spacing on both sides of the artifact pane.
  Gate: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, `test:visual` 161/161 twice (no tracked baseline touches this padding — `shell.spec.ts`'s own sequence stays on Home, which never exercises `PageShell`). `lint:tokens`'s pre-existing unrelated `theme.glass.css` failure persists, unchanged by this entry.
- **Page-section gap standardized to --space-16, system-wide (2026-08-10, fourth same-day round).** Architect clarified precisely, after confirming Portfolio/Entity Detail's pane-to-pane spacing was already correct from the prior fix: the STILL-wrong gap was a different one entirely — the space between top-level sections WITHIN a page's own content (Discover's "Notable movers"/"Trending"/"Newly Added" row down to "Discover Assets"; Entity Detail's Trend/Statistics/About stack), not the space between the three side-by-side workbench panes. Portfolio's own internal section rhythm (between its metric-grid/ring-gauge/bar-series rows) was named as the correct reference value; Discover and Entity Detail's was visibly larger. Explicitly named as "a global system wide gap size which needs to be documented," not a one-page fix.
  **Root cause, confirmed by measuring the real CSS `gap` on each page's own top-level content container, not eyeballing**: `PageShell.module.css`'s `.paneBody` (the shared content grid for every page routed through `PageShell` — Discover, Entity Detail, Watchlist, Data Sources, and Portfolio/Risk transitively via `DashboardPage`) used `gap: var(--space-24)`. `DashboardLayout.tsx`'s own Astryx `Grid gap={4}` (Portfolio/Risk's actual section layout) resolves to 16px. `SceneGrid.tsx` — the artifact pane's own top-level scene layout, used by every investigation — ALSO uses `Grid gap={4}` = 16px. Three independently-authored "space between sections" values existed (`.paneBody`'s 24px vs. two matching 16px instances), never compared side by side until this report; `.paneBody` was the outlier, not Portfolio.
  **Fix, confirmed via AskUserQuestion on both the value and where to document it**: `.paneBody`'s gap changed from `--space-24` to `--space-16`, matching `DashboardLayout`/`SceneGrid` exactly — no code change needed in either of those, they were already correct by construction. Documented as a standing, named law in `node-vocabulary.md`'s Shell section (not `tokens-spec.md`, which is explicitly Meridian-owned/project-agnostic canvas input, "implement verbatim" — a Merlin-specific layout convention like this belongs in Merlin's own living design-law document instead, alongside the routing/layout laws already recorded there): "`--space-16` is the ONE documented gap between top-level sections within a page's content... any new page-level content wrapper uses `--space-16` for this purpose — this is now the standing decision, not a per-page judgment call."
  Live-verified: `.paneBody`'s computed `gap` reads `16px` (was `24px`) on Discover; one screenshot confirmed the Notable-movers-row-to-Discover-Assets gap now reads visibly tighter, matching the internal card padding rhythm rather than sitting apart from it.
  Gate: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, `test:visual` regenerated (this touches every page rendered through `PageShell`, real diffs this time — 84 baseline files changed, file sizes shifted consistent with a real content-gap change, not a false positive) and reconfirmed via two consecutive clean runs (161/161 both times). `lint:tokens`'s pre-existing unrelated `theme.glass.css` failure persists, unchanged.
- **Page-section gap fix, part two: redundant per-page wrapper gaps found and removed (2026-08-10, same-day follow-up).** Architect reported the Discover gap STILL looked too large even after the `.paneBody` fix above, with a screenshot showing a stray divider line in otherwise-empty space between "Notable movers" and "Discover Assets" — a strong signal something was double-spacing, not that the fix hadn't landed.
  **Root cause, confirmed by counting `.paneBody`'s actual DOM children before assuming the CSS gap alone explained it**: `.paneBody` had only ONE child on Discover, not three — meaning `EntitiesPage.tsx` was wrapping its own top-level sections in a SEPARATE inline-styled `<div style={{ display: 'grid', gap: 'var(--space-32)' }}>`, nested one level inside `.paneBody`, silently shadowing the just-fixed 16px with its own independently-authored 32px. Same pattern, one level deeper than the bug already fixed this session. Grepped every `gap: 'var(--space` inline style across `components/shell/*.tsx` (31 hits) and filtered to the ones structurally matching this exact role — a grid-gap div that is the DIRECT child of `<PageShell>`, wrapping that PAGE's own top-level sections (not a sub-list's internal row gap, not a within-section field layout, which are legitimately different, smaller-scale decisions and were left alone). Three matched: `EntitiesPage.tsx` (32px), `DataSourcesPage.tsx` (32px), and `InvestigationsPage.tsx` (24px, deliberately NOT touched — see below). `EntityDetailPage.tsx` was checked and confirmed clean: its sections pass straight through to `PageShell`'s `children`, inheriting `.paneBody`'s 16px directly, no redundant wrapper — it was never actually broken, despite being named in the original report (a symptom of the SAME underlying `.paneBody` bug the prior entry already fixed, not a second bug of its own).
  **Fix, scope confirmed via AskUserQuestion**: removed the wrapping `<div style={{ display: 'grid', gap: ... }}>` entirely from `EntitiesPage.tsx` and `DataSourcesPage.tsx` — their top-level section children (movers strips/DiscoverAssetsSection/"Other tracked entities" on Discover; the three `PageSection`s on Data Sources) now pass straight to `PageShell`'s `children`, inheriting `.paneBody`'s 16px automatically, same posture as `EntityDetailPage`. `DataSourcesPage.tsx`'s `<ConnectSourceDialog>` (a modal, not a page section) stayed a sibling outside the removed wrapper, unaffected. `InvestigationsPage.tsx`'s own `--space-24` grid was inspected and deliberately left alone: it wraps a list of MODULE GROUPS within the module view (one structural row per module, `List` components grouped together), a materially different role than "space between a page's distinct top-level sections" — not the same bug, and the architect hadn't reported this page, so left as-is per "touch only named/planned files" rather than speculatively normalized.
  Live-verified via direct DOM child count + gap measurement (not just a screenshot): Discover's `.paneBody` now has 3 direct children (was 1) with real 16px/16px gaps between them; Data Sources confirmed similarly (16px between its two populated sections, third "child" being the off-screen dialog, expected). One screenshot per page confirmed visually tighter, consistent spacing matching the internal card padding rhythm.
  Gate: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, `test:visual` regenerated and reconfirmed via two consecutive clean runs (161/161 both times). `lint:tokens`'s pre-existing unrelated `theme.glass.css` failure persists, unchanged.
- **Page-section gap fix, part three: the horizontal gap between Discover's own three cards (2026-08-10, same-day follow-up).** Architect flagged one more instance directly: the gap BETWEEN "Notable movers"/"Trending"/"Newly Added" themselves (the three side-by-side cards), not the vertical gap below them (already fixed) or the pane-to-pane gap (fixed earlier).
  **Root cause**: `EntityDiscoveryStrips.tsx`'s own 3-column grid (`gridTemplateColumns: 'repeat(3, 1fr)'`) used `gap: var(--space-24)` — a fourth independently-authored instance of the same undocumented drift this whole session has been chasing, this time on the horizontal axis of one specific component rather than a page-level vertical wrapper. Confirmed live via computed style + measured `getBoundingClientRect()` gaps before touching anything: 24px/24px between the three cards.
  **Fix**: one-line change, `--space-24` → `--space-16`, matching the now-documented system rule in `node-vocabulary.md`. Checked for siblings of the same shape before considering this done: `RelatedEntitiesStrip.tsx`'s own `auto-fit` grid uses `--space-12` — a structurally different, smaller-scale case (dense venue/sector-peer chip cards, not full section panels) and NOT reported, so left untouched per "touch only named/planned files" rather than speculatively normalized to match.
  Live-verified: computed `columnGap` and measured `getBoundingClientRect()` gaps between all three cards both read `16px`/`16px` (was `24px`/`24px`); one screenshot confirmed visually tighter, consistent horizontal spacing matching the vertical section gap already fixed.
  Gate: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 33/33, `test:visual` regenerated and reconfirmed via two consecutive clean runs (161/161 both times). `lint:tokens`'s pre-existing unrelated `theme.glass.css` failure persists, unchanged.
- **Thinking trail redesign: connected timeline, status icons, expandable search-results card (2026-08-10/11, planned via EnterPlanMode, architect-approved before any code).** Architect requested a full visual redesign of the reasoning trail against a reference screenshot: a git-log-style connected vertical timeline, status icons (clock→check) replacing the trail's own prior kind-icon ruling, a persistent pulsing "Thinking…" indicator, an expandable/scrollable mock web-search-results card on search-kind steps (logo + title + domain + "N results" count), and a terminal "Done" row. Explored first (StepRow/ThinkingTrail/contracts/trailPlayer/Astryx primitives/node-vocabulary.md's own Trail law, motion tokens), then planned via EnterPlanMode/AskUserQuestion before writing any code, since this genuinely amends two lines of already-ratified design law rather than adding something net-new.
  **Two law amendments, confirmed explicitly, not silently overridden**: (1) StepRow's icon was ratified as kind-based ("our steps don't have a failure state, and kind is the meaningful fact here") — now status-based (clock/check), since a kind icon on a git-log rail node answers the wrong question once the rail exists. (2) "Active state is the only animated thing on screen at that moment" (principle 5) — now scoped to StepRow's own rows only; a new, separate persistent pulse (ThinkingIndicator) runs continuously for the trail's whole active lifetime alongside per-row settle animation, a deliberate confirmed exception, not a blanket repeal. Both amendments logged verbatim in `node-vocabulary.md`'s Trail vocabulary section in the same change, with the superseded original law quoted rather than deleted.
  **Contract**: `contracts/thinking.ts` gained `WebResultSchema` (`{ id, title, domain }`) and an optional `ThinkingStep.webResults` field — additive only, `sources`/`ThinkingStepSource`/`confidenceQualifier` completely untouched. `webResults` is a deliberately separate register from `sources`: `sources` cites internal system provenance (MarketTape, RiskLens) and renders as the canonical SourceChip pill everywhere it's used (trail, panel attributions, recommendations) — SourceChip's own "identical everywhere" law is explicitly preserved, not touched by this change at all. `webResults` cites external web pages and renders only in the new SearchResultsCard, trail-only, search-kind-steps-only.
  **The connecting rail — the real CSS problem, solved without JS height math or a shared overlay**: each `StepRow` draws only the segment BELOW its own icon (`position:absolute`, `left:11px` centers a 2px line on the 24px icon box, `top:0`, `bottom: calc(-1 * var(--space-12))` — extends past the row's own bottom edge by exactly the trail's row gap so it reaches the next row's icon center with no seam). Stacked segments concatenate into one continuous line purely from each row's own `position:relative` box — confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect()` that this holds even through variable-height rows (a settled row carrying SourceChips or the SearchResultsCard, which push the next icon further down than a fixed gap alone would reach): rail `bottom` and next icon `center` matched within ~12px (the icon's own half-height) at every seam, including across the results card. The rail element is conditionally rendered (`!isLast`) rather than styled to zero height — the last row (always the synthetic Done row once complete) simply has no rail element in the DOM. `2px` rail width is a flagged, accepted single-use geometry constant (no `--border-width-*` scale exists), same posture as `trail.css`'s own existing raw clip-path calc. Icon box gained a permanent (not just active-state) opaque circular backdrop (`--surface-1`, `z-index:1`) so the rail passes visually behind each icon, git-log-node style — previously the circular backdrop only existed while a step was active.
  **StepRow.tsx**: `KIND_ICONS` lookup table deleted outright; icon is now `isActive ? 'clock' : 'check'` — both already existed in Astryx's closed `IconName` type and are real, non-fallback SVGs in the installed `theme-stone` package (confirmed by reading the installed package directly before assuming, per this project's "check real APIs" rule) — zero new icon components needed, no `DocumentIcon.tsx`-style gap-fill required, unlike a genuinely-missing icon name would have. `kind` stays imported/used (still gates the SearchResultsCard render), just no longer drives the icon. Also fixed a pre-existing inline-style violation while restructuring the icon box anyway (`style={{ width: 'var(--space-24)', ... }}` moved into the new `.iconBox` CSS class) — not scope creep, the box's classes were already being rewritten for the status-icon/rail work.
  **SearchResultsCard** (new): Astryx `Card` (`padding={2}`, `variant="muted"`, `height={192}` — `Card`'s own `height` prop triggers real `overflow:auto` scrolling, confirmed by reading the installed component's actual implementation, not just its type declarations, since the plan needed to be sure this wasn't just documented behavior) wrapping a right-aligned "N results" header (plain flex row, not `List`'s own `header` prop, which is `aria-labelledby`-bound to the list's semantic label, not a place for a right-aligned count) above an Astryx `List`/`ListItem` — `startContent` = Astryx `Avatar` (`name={result.domain}`, no `src`, so its own initials-fallback IS the logo placeholder — zero hand-rolled colored squares), `label` = title, `endContent` = domain. `height={192}` flagged as a second single-use raw value — `Card`'s `height` prop is `SizeValue` (px/string), not token-aware, so a raw JS number is mechanically the only way to hand it a value at all.
  **ThinkingIndicator** (new): reuses the exact existing `merlin-step-pulse` keyframe/duration/easing (`--duration-480`/`--ease-standard`) verbatim — zero new motion values invented, per the token-only rule. Own `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce { animation: none }` override, confirmed live via Playwright's `page.emulateMedia({ reducedMotion: 'reduce' })` (the correct testing mechanism — a bare JS `matchMedia` shim was tried first and correctly abandoned as not actually exercising the CSS media query).
  **The "Done" row**: a real `StepRow`, synthesized client-side by `ThinkingTrail.tsx` once `isComplete` (`{ id: '__done__', kind: 'verify', label: 'Done', durationMs: 0 }`, never persisted, never scene-validated) — not a bespoke component. `kind: 'verify'` is a type-satisfying placeholder only, invisible now that icon selection is status- not kind-driven; since it always renders `isActive:false`, it automatically gets the settled check-icon treatment with zero special-casing inside StepRow. The `"N reasoning steps · X.Xs"` Collapsible trigger stays keyed on real `steps.length`, deliberately excluding Done from the count.
  **Mock data**: `engine/mockSearchResults.ts` (new) — one small fixed placeholder list (5 generic finance/reference-site entries), `mockSearchResultsFor(kind)` returning it for `search`-kind, `[]` otherwise. Injected via a thin map in `presentScene.ts` (the single confirmed call site shared by both `submitQuery.ts` and `liveChannel.ts`, per that file's own doc comment) immediately before `playTrail()`, guarded by `!step.webResults` so a scene later authoring real per-scenario results is respected automatically with no mock override — matching the architect's own "later will incorporate it dynamically per scenario" framing exactly. The SAME mapped array is reused for `settleTurn`'s persisted `trail` (previously `scene.thinking` verbatim), not just the live `playTrail` call — confirmed deliberately during planning, so a re-viewed settled turn still shows the results card, not only the live-playing one.
  **Isolated CSS Modules quirk found and worked through during implementation, not left as a plan gap**: `ThinkingIndicator`'s pulse needed `merlin-step-pulse`, a bare (non-Modules) global keyframe defined in `trail.css` — `ThinkingTrail.tsx` had never imported that file (only `StepRow.tsx` had), so it needed its own `import './trail.css'` line added for the keyframe to resolve at all once `ThinkingIndicator` mounted inside it.
  **Plan deviation, reported not hidden**: the approved plan specified `isFirst`/`isLast` props on StepRow to suppress a rail segment "above" the first row; implementation found this prop unnecessary once the actual mechanism was built — each row only ever draws its rail DOWNWARD (toward the next row), so there is no "segment above" a first row to ever suppress in the first place. Shipped with `isLast` only; `isFirst` was never added, and the stale plan-era comment referencing it was caught and corrected before commit.
  Live-verified across all four themes (Storybook `Trail/ThinkingTrail`'s three new stories — `Running`, `SearchStepExpanded`, `Complete` — plus `Trail/StepRow`'s new `SearchWithResults` story, `Trail/SearchResultsCard`'s three states, `Trail/ThinkingIndicator`): rail continuous through every seam including across the results card and SourceChips/ConfidenceBadge rows (confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()`, not just a screenshot glance, after an initial visual read of the full-page screenshot mistakenly suggested the rail might be discontinuous — it wasn't, the line is just genuinely subtle at `--edge`'s low-alpha default-theme value, same as the reference screenshot's own restraint), status icons correctly flip clock→check per settle, "4 results"/"5 results" counts correct, avatar-initial logo placeholders render, scrollable card cuts off correctly at its fixed height, Done row terminates the rail cleanly with no trailing segment, reduced-motion freezes the persistent pulse. Full live end-to-end loop also verified in the real `Shell/Frame` story (not just isolated component stories): submitted a real query, watched the trail play and settle, confirmed the mock results card appeared on the real search step with real mock data, confirmed the artifact panel opened correctly alongside it.
  Gate: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 40/40 (33 prior + 7 new — `contracts/thinking.test.ts`, new file, `webResults` backward-compatibility + `WebResultSchema` validation cases, following `malformed.test.ts`'s own existing style since no `thinking.test.ts` previously existed), `lint:tokens` clean for every file this change touched (confirmed via a scoped grep, since the pre-existing unrelated `theme.glass.css` hex-value failure from earlier in this session still fails the full repo-wide gate and is not this change's to fix). `test:visual`: `shell.spec.ts-snapshots` deleted and regenerated from zero (16 baselines, all 4 themes × 4 states) — hit one genuinely reproducible failure along the way (`shell-landing-glass-light`, 0.03 diff ratio, a sub-pixel text-rendering shift on a screenshot that never renders the trail at all) that survived one retry before a targeted single-file baseline delete+regenerate resolved it; two full consecutive clean runs afterward (161/161 both times) confirmed stable, not masked.
- **New skill: `merlin-layout-law`, plus its first proactive sweep (2026-08-13).** Self-audit requested after the thinking-trail redesign: the `--space-16` page-section-gap law had already been fixed reactively FOUR separate times this session (pane-to-pane spacing, `.paneBody`, the three-card horizontal gap, each only after Prem independently spotted and reported it) — the same underlying bug (an independently-authored spacing value never compared against siblings) surfacing repeatedly because nothing forced a cross-codebase check the first time a spacing law was ratified. Architect approved closing this structurally: a new skill, `~/Merlin/.claude/skills/merlin-layout-law/SKILL.md`, triggered whenever a spacing/gap/alignment value is being ratified as system standard rather than fixed locally — mandates sweeping every sibling usage in the SAME change (not waiting for each to be independently reported), verifying via `getBoundingClientRect()`/computed styles rather than a screenshot for any measurable claim, and registering the law in `node-vocabulary.md` explicitly.
  **First sweep run against the existing `--space-16` law**, immediately, same session: grepped `gap: var(--space-24)`/`gap: 'var(--space-24)'`/`gap: 'var(--space-32)'` and every Astryx `Grid gap={N}` across `components/shell/` and `components/nodes/`. Found three more real violations, all fixed: `InvestigationsPage.tsx`'s module-group wrapper (`--space-24`→`--space-16`), `MarketPulsePage.tsx`'s card grid (`gap={3}`/12px→`gap={4}`/16px), and `LandingState.tsx`'s suggestion-chip grid (`gap={3}`→`gap={4}`, confirmed in-scope via AskUserQuestion specifically because it's documented elsewhere as an echo of Market Pulse's own data — the two renderings needed to match — with its `minWidth` re-derived from 200px to 210px for the new gap math and live-verified via `getBoundingClientRect()` that all three cards still fit one row with no wrapping). Two candidates correctly excluded on inspection rather than swept blind: `Transcript.tsx`'s `--space-24` (a different register — vertical space between chat turns, not sibling page sections) and `EntityDetailPage.tsx`'s `--space-24` (a gap WITHIN one section's own sub-columns, not between top-level siblings — the same exclusion already made correctly during the original investigation). `StatusGrid.tsx`'s `gap={3}` also excluded, same reasoning as the already-excluded `RelatedEntitiesStrip.tsx` — a registry node's own denser status-row register, not this law's relationship.
  **Self-inflicted bug caught by `tsc`, not shipped**: the first pass of these three fixes used JSX-comment syntax (`{/* ... */}`) placed directly inside two ternary expressions' parenthesized branches (`InvestigationsPage.tsx`, `MarketPulsePage.tsx`) — valid as a JSX child, invalid as a bare expression inside `(...)`. Both broke Storybook's live compile (confirmed via a real browser navigation showing Vite's "Failed to fetch dynamically imported module" error page, not just assumed) before `tsc` was run; both found and fixed by converting to `//` line comments in the same pass, then reconfirmed clean via `tsc` and a live Storybook reload before calling the sweep done — verification actually caught something, not a formality.
  Gate: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 40/40 (unchanged, no contract touched), `lint:tokens` clean on all three touched files, `test:visual` (`shell-landing-*` baselines regenerated — the only tracked screenshots touching any of the three fixed pages, confirmed via grep before assuming a full regen was needed) reconfirmed via two consecutive clean runs (161/161 both times). Registered in `node-vocabulary.md`'s existing page-section-gap law entry as a dated follow-up, not a new law.
- **Playwright HTML report enabled, no longer discarded every run (2026-08-13, self-audit point 5).** Confirmed via reading `playwright.config.ts` before acting: only `reporter: [['list']]` (terminal text) was configured — Playwright was never generating an HTML report at all this session, so nothing was actually being "thrown away" as originally suspected; the report simply didn't exist to keep. `.gitignore` already had `playwright-report`/`test-results` entries pre-staged (evidently anticipated, never activated), so enabling it was a genuinely zero-risk one-line config change, not new tooling.
  **Change**: `reporter` now `[['list'], ['html', { outputFolder: 'playwright-report', open: 'never' }]]` — `list` stays the primary gate-evidence format (CLAUDE.md's session protocol), `html` additionally writes a real static, browsable report to `playwright-report/index.html` on every `test:visual` run; `open: 'never'` so it doesn't try to auto-launch a browser mid-agent-session. Ran `test:visual` once to confirm generation, then live-verified the report itself isn't a broken shell: served it via a temporary local `python3 -m http.server`, navigated to it, confirmed real content ("All 161 / Passed 161 / Failed 0," per-test breakdown with file:line links) — not just confirmed the file exists.
  **Path, for direct access without a live session**: `/Users/przemek/Merlin/playwright-report/index.html`, regenerated fresh on every `test:visual`/`pnpm verify` run, gitignored (never committed, matching `test-results`' existing treatment) — open it directly in a browser after any local test run to see pass/fail/diffs without asking for a summary.
  Gate: config change only, verified by running `test:visual` once (161/161, same as before — reporter changes don't affect test outcomes) and confirming the generated report renders real data.
- **Side chat pane's "pop" border/background/shadow reversed (2026-08-14).** Architect reported the side transcript pane (chat, opened alongside Discover/an entity page) should have no visible border/background at all — "same way like new investigation AI chat has no border." Initial confusion resolved through iteration: first read as the composer INPUT's own chrome (checked — Astryx's `ChatComposer` internal input wrapper renders identically, same `rgba(255,255,255,.1)`/28px-radius pill, on both Home's landing composer and the real ChatBar; not the target, both already matched). Architect corrected: the target was the PANE's own container chrome (`.transcriptPane`'s border+background+shadow, added 2026-08-10's "pop" order), not the composer. Confirmed scope via AskUserQuestion before touching anything, since removing it affects the whole side pane (transcript/trail scroll area too, not just the composer dock) — architect confirmed whole-pane removal, not composer-only.
  **Fix**: `Frame.module.css`'s `.transcriptPane` — `background-color: var(--pane-surface)`, `border: 1px solid var(--edge)`, `border-radius: var(--radius-12)`, `box-shadow: var(--shading-pop-raised)` all removed; only layout properties remain (`display`, `flex-direction`, `flex`, `min-width`, `min-height`). `--search-results-surface: var(--surface-2)` custom property kept (SearchResultsCard.module.css's own ambient-aware background override still needs a value to consume now that this pane's own background is gone — same `--surface-2` value as before, just no longer framed as "one step up from `--pane-surface`" since that token no longer applies here). The 2026-08-10 "pop" order's own comment block kept verbatim as historical record (not deleted) with a new comment directly above explaining the reversal and citing this order — same "supersede, don't silently overwrite" discipline used for prior law reversals this session.
  Live-verified: computed `border`/`background`/`boxShadow` on `.transcriptPane` all resolve to none/transparent/none; two full-flow screenshots (default and glass themes, Discover page with chat pane open) confirm no visible pane box around either the trail/transcript scroll area or the composer dock — content sits directly on the ambient background, matching Home's own composer context exactly. Glass theme specifically checked since the pane also lost its `backdrop-filter`/glass-material framing as a side effect (never had one on this pane in the first place — confirmed, not assumed).
  Gate: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 40/40 (unchanged), `lint:tokens` clean on the touched file (pre-existing unrelated `theme.glass.css` failure persists, not this change's). `test:visual`: `shell.spec.ts-snapshots` deleted and regenerated from zero (16 baselines, every state with the transcript pane visible is affected) and reconfirmed via two consecutive clean runs (161/161 both times).

Filed after Phase 18's own gate line, before "## 9. Session protocol."

Confirmed via AskUserQuestion to file exactly as ordered rather than guess a renumber.

- **Phase 19 (Entity Detail tabs + Analyst Consensus) — GATED (2026-08-14).** Full implementation, gate passed, live-verified.
  **Tab-content split** (proposed via AskUserQuestion, architect-approved verbatim): Overview = Trend + Analyst Consensus (new, gated on `entity.analystConsensus`) + Related entities + Investigations — the "at a glance" summary. Financials = Statistics + About side by side (unchanged content, moved wholesale from the old single-scroll page). Coverage = news-feed, only rendered as a tab when `entity.news` exists (so an entity with no authored coverage — e.g. Zenith Protocol — correctly shows only 3 tabs, no dead Coverage tab; live-verified). Historical Data = the same `EntityTrend` component reused unchanged (confirmed no larger underlying dataset exists to make it "fuller" than Overview's own chart — the 90-day series is already complete), removed from Overview to avoid the same chart appearing twice.
  **`analyst-consensus` node**: new registry entry, `contracts/props/analyst-consensus.ts` (`AnalystConsensusSchema` + `AnalystConsensusPropsSchema`), `components/nodes/AnalystConsensus.tsx` + `.module.css`, `AnalystConsensus.stories.tsx` (Happy/Partial/Empty). Astryx checked directly (installed `.tsx` source, not just types) — no distribution-bar or multi-marker-axis primitive exists (`ProgressBar` is explicitly single-segment only per its own doc comment; `Slider`'s `marks` prop is the nearest partial primitive but is an interactive input control, wrong register for passive fact display) — justifies custom SVG/CSS per `merlin-new-node` skill's Astryx-first check. Two-part layout: segmented bearish/neutral/bullish distribution bar (`--viz-1`/`--viz-2`/`--accent-signal`, no red/green performance coloring per principle 8 — same "share, not performance" discipline as `concentration-map`) + a labeled low/average/high/current price-position axis (three small `--ink-secondary` markers, one larger `--accent-signal`-filled "current" marker with a `--surface-1` ring, geometry-verified correct via `getBoundingClientRect()`/computed-style before trusting a visually-ambiguous low-res screenshot — see below). Self-caught inline-style violation during the build (first draft copied `RingGauge.tsx`'s existing inline-style pattern; rewrote to a CSS module before committing, per CLAUDE.md's structural ban — noted but did not propagate `RingGauge.tsx`'s own pre-existing violation into new code).
  **Data**: `universe/analystConsensus.json`, two entries — South Bow Corp (12 analysts, 1/4/7 split, $34.50–$52.00 range) and Zenith Protocol (9 analysts, 2/3/4 split, $2.60–$5.25 range). Both entries' `priceTargets.current` cross-checked to exactly match each entity's real authored price in `universe/entities.json` ($40.34 / $3.47) before finalizing, per the internal-consistency rule. `entityDetail.ts`'s `resolveEntityDetail()` extended with Zod `safeParse`-validated resolution (not a blanket cast like `news`/`datasets`, since this hand-authored JSON has no other validation pipeline checking it).
  **Scoping**: gating is purely data-driven (`{entity.analystConsensus && (...)}`, identical pattern to the existing `{entity.news && (...)}` conditional) rather than a type/tag-string check — `entity.type` in this universe is free-text description ("Equity — Pipeline & Infrastructure"), not a clean enum, so a tag-matching gate would have been both redundant and fragile; the universe file itself is the source of truth for which entities are in scope (only South Bow Corp and Zenith Protocol currently have entries, matching the order's "crypto/stocks only" instruction without a second enforcement mechanism).
  **Astryx API note**: `TabList`/`Tab` from `@astryxdesign/core/TabList` — controlled (`value`/`onChange` on `TabList`), `Tab` takes a `label` prop (not children) for its visible text. Confirmed by reading the installed package source directly, not assumed from memory.
  **Verification**: `tsc`, `vitest` (40/40), `lint:tokens`/theme-parity clean (one pre-existing failure in `theme.glass.css`'s already-uncommitted `--surface-0` hex value predates this session, untouched here, not this phase's scope). Live-verified all 4 tabs + analyst-consensus across all 4 registered themes (default/ops-dark/glass/glass-light) via Storybook's `shell-frame--default` story through a real Discover-page click-through to both South Bow Corp and Zenith Protocol — not just isolated node stories. One false-alarm during verification: a `fullPage: true` Playwright screenshot of the ops-dark Overview tab appeared to be missing the Analyst Consensus/Related/Investigations sections entirely; `getBoundingClientRect()` and an element-scoped screenshot of the same live DOM node both confirmed full correct rendering (opacity 1, correct position, visually legible) — the full-page screenshot capture itself was unreliable for this tall a page, not the app. Recorded as a reminder that element-scoped capture is the trustworthy method per this project's own visual-verification guardrail, not just a preference.
  `test:visual`: added `nodes-analystconsensus--happy` to `nodes.spec.ts`'s `NODE_STORIES` list (this project's "one baseline per node's Happy story, 4 themes" convention); 4 new baselines generated, reviewed, and confirmed stable across two consecutive clean runs. No `EntityDetailPage`-specific baselines existed before or needed regenerating (no visual spec targets that page directly — it's reached only through live shell navigation, not an isolated story). Full `test:visual` suite re-run clean: 165/165 passed, zero regressions from the tab restructure.
  No dev server available in this repo (`app/` has never had an `index.html` committed, confirmed via `git log --all`, pre-existing and out of this phase's scope) — all live verification used the established `shell-frame--default` Storybook story + Playwright, matching the pattern already used by `shell.spec.ts`.
  Gate passed. Stopping per one-phase-at-a-time rule.

- **FIX — `theme.glass.css`'s `--surface-0` hex value converted to spec (2026-08-15).** Small standalone order, architect-ruled as real debt correctly left untouched during Phase 19 (per scope discipline — flagged, not fixed, in that phase's own gate). `--surface-0: #0E111340` (a dark tint at ~0.25 alpha) replaced with `--surface-0: transparent;`, matching `tokens-spec.md`'s own glass spec line 71 exactly and mirroring `theme.glass-light.css:39`'s already-correct identical value + comment for the same "canvas shows through — app supplies deep backdrop" purpose. The two values were NOT pixel-identical (hex was a genuine ~25%-alpha dark wash, not truly transparent) — confirmed via direct alpha computation, not assumed — but no visual regression surfaced across any of the 165 `test:visual` baselines, meaning no currently-tested view exercises `--surface-0` in a spot where that delta would show; spec correctness (not just pixel-identical output) is what governed the fix. Real consumers checked before/after: `NotificationBell`, `ArtifactStackControl`, `PaneTitleBar`'s gradient, `GeoPanel`'s SVG stroke, `#astryx-app-shell-main` — all are canvas-region consumers where "shows through" is the documented intent, so `transparent` is correct for all of them.
  Gate: `lint:tokens` fully clean (previously the sole repo-wide failure). `tsc` clean, `vitest` 40/40, `test:visual` 165/165, zero regressions. No other file touched.

  **Components built**: all 5 registry nodes (contract + component + story each, CSS modules only, zero inline-style violations — deliberately didn't propagate `RingGauge.tsx`'s/`ConnectSourceDialog.tsx`'s own pre-existing violations into new code, same self-correction as Phase 19). `AssetKeyStatsTable`/`AssetSnippetCard` built as shell components (`components/shell/`), not registry nodes, per the corrected scope — both wrap `MetricGrid`+`Metric` directly.

Confirmed: new registry node delegating to TimeSeries via composition (not extending TimeSeries's own schema/props, not duplicating its rendering logic).
  **A real bug found and fixed during live verification, not just claimed clean from tsc/vitest**: TrendChart's first working version rendered South Bow Corp's authored `compareSeries` (a 6-point monthly "Meridian Exchange Energy Index") garbled — correct for its first ~2 weeks, then collapsed to a flat 0 for the rest of the 90-day chart. Root cause: `TimeSeries.tsx`'s existing multi-series row-building pairs series POSITIONALLY by array index against the first series' own x-values (`line.points[index]?.y ?? 0`), an assumption true for every prior usage (Phase 12's dual-line overlays, always identical date ranges) but false for a compare series covering genuinely fewer real dates than an 85-point daily primary. Since editing the shared, reused-not-rebuilt `TimeSeries.tsx` was out of scope, fixed at the `TrendChart` level: when a compareSeries is present, both the primary and compare series are trimmed to the compare series' own real coverage window (verified live: chart now correctly shows only June 1–July 2, both lines meaningful throughout, no fabricated pre-benchmark values). Confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect`-adjacent visual re-check after the fix, not just re-reading the diff.

What WAS retired: `EntityDetailPage.tsx`'s entire old rendering (replaced wholesale) and its three now-orphaned sub-components (`EntityTrend.tsx`, `EntityStatistics.tsx`, `EntityAbout.tsx` — deleted, confirmed zero other consumers via grep before deleting).
  **Deep-link mechanism**: `pageStore.ts`'s `openEntityDetail` gained an optional second param (`target?: {tab, itemId?}`), backward-compatible with all 3 existing single-arg call sites (`EntitiesPage.tsx`, `HoldingsPage.tsx`, `DashboardPage.tsx`, all unchanged). New one-shot `entityDetailTarget` state, same pattern as the existing `focusModule` (InvestigationsPage's own consumer) — `EntityDetailPage.tsx` reads it once via `useEffect`, switches tabs, clears it. Overview's own preview-card clicks use local `setActiveTab` directly (same-page, no store round-trip needed) — the store mechanism exists for cross-page deep-links, a real, separate use case, not redundant with the local switch.
  **Live verification**: full click-through (Discover → South Bow Corp AND Zenith Protocol → all 6/5 tabs respectively) across default/ops-dark/glass. Confirmed: price header (with/without after-hours, correct green/red delta via `--accent-ok`/`--accent-alert`), all 3 Overview deep-links (Analyst Consensus→Analysis, Earnings→Earnings, Notable Price Movement→News) land on the correct tab with only that tab's content showing; Financials tab (Key Statistics + About, dense MetricGrid); period-toggle interaction on TrendChart (SegmentedControl, not TabList — "controls a value, not a view," confirmed via Astryx doc comment); Zenith Protocol's absent-field paths (no Earnings tab since `earningsHistory` is absent, no after-hours row, no compareSeries — all correctly omitted, not empty-rendered).
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean. `test:visual` full suite 165/165, two consecutive clean runs, zero regressions from touching `pageStore.ts`/deleting 3 files — no new baselines needed (Entity Detail has no dedicated visual spec, same situation as Phase 19).

Architect supplied two reference screenshots (a Perplexity-style equity detail page: 2-column layout, logo header, directional green trend chart with a bottom glow, Key Statistics, a new "Key Issues" bull/bear split card, "Stories & Analysis" cards with thumbnail images, a vertical "Notable Price Movement" timeline, and a narrow right-rail with Analysis/Consensus/peers) plus a live walkthrough of what changes vs. Phase 20's own Overview. Filed after Phase 20 WO-2's own gate line, before "## 9. Session protocol." Phase number (21) derived by grepping CLAUDE.md's own phase list — next open number after 20.
  **Three real conflicts surfaced and resolved via AskUserQuestion before filing, not guessed**: (1) the reference's green/red trend-line-and-glow is directional price-performance coloring, which principle 8 has banned everywhere except a plain delta numeral/arrow since Phase 14 — confirmed as a deliberate, NAMED, SCOPED exception (TradableAsset's own trend chart only, via `--delta-up`/`--delta-down`, not a general relaxation) rather than silently expanding the existing carve-out; (2) the "glow" effect described as a generic reusable pane primitive — confirmed it lives as a token/CSS-module class (position × clip variants), not a new registry node or a new prop on the existing `Panel` node, keeping it a presentation-only style hook; (3) "Key Issues" — confirmed as a real `TradableAssetSchema` extension with authored bull/bear content for both golden fixtures, not placeholder text, preserving WO-1's "fixtures are the schema's acceptance test" discipline rather than letting it drift.
  **Three more resolved on the same pass**: header identity image reuses the existing `AssetLogo.tsx` (sized up), not new per-asset artwork; story-card thumbnails are token-colored placeholder blocks, not a new image-asset pipeline (same honest-placeholder posture as Data Sources' own public catalog); the right-column cards KEEP the WO-1 deep-link behavior (click → jump to Analysis/News tab) inside the new 2-column shape, rather than the redesign silently replacing that IA with static always-expanded content.
  **Block content**: 2-column Overview layout (Astryx `Grid`/`GridSpan`, 2:1 — confirmed no true fractional-width primitive exists before choosing this shape); the glow primitive; `TrendChart`'s directional-color amendment (scoped to it and its Overview-embedded form only — `TimeSeries.tsx` itself, the bind node reused by scene-driven surfaces, stays on `--accent-signal`, unchanged); `keyIssues` schema field + new `KeyIssuesCard` node (full node-addition test required); "Notable Price Movement" renamed/restyled as a vertical timeline (existing `TimelineEntry` schema unchanged — visual/IA change only; explicitly left open in the order itself whether this needs its own node-addition test given the rename, to be judged and reported at build time, not silently decided either way); "Stories & Analysis" rename with placeholder images.
  Not yet gated — implementation begins immediately following this entry.

- **Phase 21 — GATED (2026-08-15).** Full implementation, gate passed, live-verified across all 5 registered themes.
  **`keyIssues` schema checkpoint**: presented per the order's own promise before authoring fixture content; architect approved as-is, no schema change. Content authored for both fixtures using only already-established facts (South Bow Corp: the capacity-expansion filing/regulatory review, dividend durability; Zenith Protocol: ZIP-14 buyback durability, post-move valuation vs. analyst consensus) — one real fixture bug caught and fixed in the same pass: a `sources: []` entry failed the schema's own `.min(1)` (no bare data-derived claim can cite zero sources); resolved by attributing to `LedgerWatch` via a new `zenith-protocol-analyst-consensus` ref rather than leaving it empty.
  **`key-issues-card`**: new registry node, full node-addition test (tested explicitly against `analyst-consensus`, `ai-rationale-rail`, and `comparison`, all ruled out). **Real color bug found and fixed during live verification, not just claimed clean from tsc/vitest**: first version used `--accent-signal`/`--viz-1` (matching `analyst-consensus`'s own bearish/bullish pairing) for the Bullish/Bearish view labels — live screenshot showed "Bullish view" invisible in `default` theme and, on inspection, `--accent-signal`/`--viz-1` share the identical hue (225) in `ops-dark`/`glass`/`glass-light`, differing only in lightness (legible as adjacent bar-fill blocks, not as small text). A same-session fallback (`--viz-1`/`--viz-2`, blue/green) was flagged and rejected via AskUserQuestion before shipping — green-for-bullish reads as literal good/bad framing, too close to principle 8's own ban even without the actual ok/alert tokens. Final: `--viz-1`(blue)/`--viz-4`(purple), verified live as genuinely distinct and legible in both `default` and `ops-dark`.
  **glow primitive**: `theme/glow.module.css`, position (bottom/top-left/top-right) × clip (clipped/unclipped) composable classes, color via `--glow-color` custom property, zero registry/Panel-prop coupling — confirmed generic by reading the source (no selector scoped to any TradableAsset-specific parent), satisfying the gate's "reusable outside TradableAsset context" requirement without a throwaway story.
  **TrendChart directional-color exception**: implemented as a LOCAL `--accent-signal` override on a narrow wrapper around only `<TimeSeries>`, not the whole `TrendChart` component — a real bug caught before shipping (not after): the first draft scoped the override to the whole component, which would have silently recolored the period-toggle `SegmentedControl`'s own focus ring too (`SegmentedControlItem.tsx` reads `--color-accent`, which itself resolves `var(--accent-signal)` lazily at the point of use, following inheritance — confirmed by reading both the component source and each theme file's own bridge line before scoping narrowly). Verified live in both directions: South Bow Corp's compare-series-trimmed June window (net down, renders `--delta-down`/red — mathematically correct for that slice, not a bug) and Zenith Protocol's full 90-day range (net up, renders `--delta-up`/green).
  **Notable Price Movement**: restyled as a vertical timeline reusing `StepRow.module.css`'s own git-log-style connecting-rail mechanic (confirmed as real, already-verified precedent before reusing it) — same registry key, same `TimelineEntry` schema, ruled a restyle not a new node-addition test per the order's own explicit escape hatch, decision logged in node-vocabulary.md's Phase 21 section as instructed.

  **AssetLogo.tsx extended**, not replaced: additive `size` prop (`'default' | 'large'`, defaults to the original 32/24 pixel pair so every existing grid/table/strip call site is unaffected), `'large'` uses real `--space-48`/`--space-32` tokens (not invented values — the first draft used non-existent `--space-56`/`--space-44`, caught by grepping the actual token scale before shipping).
  **2-column layout**: `AssetOverviewTab.tsx` rebuilt on Astryx `Grid columns={3}` + `GridSpan columns={2|1}` (confirmed no true fractional-width primitive exists in Astryx before choosing this integer-ratio approach). File stayed under the 200-line budget (142 lines). One self-caught inline-style violation during the rewrite (two `style={{ display: 'grid', gap: ... }}` objects, copied from the file's own prior pattern) — fixed with a proper `AssetOverviewTab.module.css` before shipping, not left in.

Added retroactively alongside Phase 21's own two new entries (`TrendChart`, `KeyIssuesCard`) — 30 new baselines total (6 nodes × 5 themes), not 10.
  **Live verification**: full click-through on South Bow Corp across default/ops-dark/glass/safe-one (Zenith Protocol's absent-field paths — no earnings, no compareSeries — already covered in Phase 20's own verification, not re-walked here since Overview's structural changes don't touch those conditionals). Deep-links reconfirmed working inside the new 2-column shape (Analyst Consensus card → Analysis tab, correct tab highlighted). Console errors confirmed as the same pre-existing benign `liveChannel` SSE retry loop (mcp-server not running), not new.

- **Phase 21 — post-gate bug fix: `AssetOverviewTab`'s right column stretched to the left column's height (2026-08-15).** Architect-reported live: "the side section has two big gaps," identifying `AnimatedListItem`'s own module class (`_animatedItem_95elv_8`) via browser inspector — the right (narrow) column had visible empty space below its real content.
  **Root cause**: `AssetOverviewTab.tsx` used Astryx `Grid columns={3}` + `GridSpan columns={2|1}` for the 2:1 split (per Phase 21's own filed block). `GridSpan.tsx`'s own source (`node_modules/@astryxdesign/core/src/Grid/GridSpan.tsx`) hardcodes `height: '100%'` in its `baseStyles.span`, doc-commented "make span fill grid cell and stretch children" — independent of the outer `Grid`'s `align` prop. First fix attempt (`align="start"` on the outer `Grid`) did NOT resolve it — confirmed via direct `getBoundingClientRect()` measurement that both `GridSpan` cells stayed forced to an identical 1600px even with `align-items: start` correctly applied. The real cause is `GridSpan`'s own unconditional inline `height: 100%`, a closed-component behavior with no prop to disable.
  **Fix**: removed `Grid`/`GridSpan` from `AssetOverviewTab.tsx` entirely, replaced with plain CSS Grid — new `.layout`/`.column` classes in `AssetOverviewTab.module.css` (`grid-template-columns: 2fr 1fr; gap: var(--space-16); align-items: start`), each column sized only by its own content. Root cause and rationale documented in both the component's own header comment and the CSS file's comment, so a future session doesn't reintroduce `Grid`/`GridSpan` here.
  **Verification**: live-measured both columns post-fix (left 1600px, unchanged real content height; right 1122px, now its own genuinely shorter height — no forced stretch), confirmed by screenshot showing no visible gap below the right column's last card. Full gate re-run after the fix: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean. `test:visual` full suite: one contended run produced 24 failures, all `renderer.spec.ts` `renderer-fixtures--*` cases (unrelated to this change — none touch `AssetOverviewTab`/Entity Detail), root-caused via `error-context.md` to `page.waitForSelector('#storybook-root')` timing out with the root resolving hidden 35× — a `Test timeout of 30000ms exceeded`, not a pixel diff; per CLAUDE.md's own "suspect the environment before the code" rule, re-ran in isolation (no other work competing for CPU) and got 55/55 (renderer spec alone) then 235/235 (full suite) clean, two consecutive passes, zero real regressions, zero new baselines needed (no `AssetOverviewTab`-specific story/spec exists, matching the precedent already logged for `EntityDetailPage` since Phase 19).
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a bug fix within already-gated Phase 21 scope, not new design law.

- **Phase 21 — post-gate follow-up: chart full-bleed, inner Y-axis, glow colored by trend (2026-08-15).** Architect-reported live, four asks: (1) the price+chart block should sit inside a real pane; (2) the chart itself should be full-bleed edge-to-edge within that pane; (3) the Y-axis numbers should read on the inner side of the chart, flush left, no reserved margin; (4) the bottom glow should be colored by the trend's own direction, not `--accent-signal`.
  **Pane**: `AssetOverviewTab.tsx`'s price+chart block was a bare glow div with no `Card`/`Panel` — now wrapped in a real `Panel` (no title, matching the reference's plain framing). The glow itself moved OUT of `AssetOverviewTab` and INTO `TrendChart.tsx` — the component now owns its own pane-edge glow, colored by the same `directionalColor()` value that colors its own line/area-fill (previously computed only for the chart line; now also threads into `--glow-color`). One function, one source of truth for "which way is this trend sloping," reused for both the line and the glow, per CLAUDE.md rule 6 (no callback props out of a registry node — confirmed as a real constraint before landing on "compute once, use twice inside the same component" rather than lifting state to the caller).
  **Full bleed**: new `TimeSeries.tsx` additive prop `bleed` (default `false`, every existing usage/story/scene unaffected) — when true, `ComposedChart`'s own `margin` zeroes out and a new `TrendChart.module.css` `.chartBleed` class cancels the enclosing `Panel`'s own inline padding via `margin-inline: calc(-1 * var(--container-padding-inline-start))`, the same negative-margin mechanic `Panel.tsx` already documents borrowing from Astryx's own `Table` bleed behavior. Verified live via `getBoundingClientRect()`: chart SVG left edge now matches the Panel's own left edge (938px SVG inside a 940px panel, 2px accounted for by border).
  **Inner axis, two real bugs found and fixed before landing**: (1) first attempt set `YAxis width={0}` to reserve zero space — this silently killed the ENTIRE axis render (recharts' own `CartesianAxis.js`: `if (width <= 0) return null`, confirmed by reading the installed package source after live-measuring zero `<text>` nodes at width=0) — fixed by using `width={1}`, the smallest value that survives recharts' own guard while still visually reserving nothing. (2) the tick's `dx` offset originally undershot, landing labels 3px to the LEFT of the SVG's own edge (bleeding out of the pane) — fixed by increasing `dx` from 4 to 8, reverified via `getBoundingClientRect()` that the tick now sits 1px inside the SVG edge, correctly "on the inner side." `TabularTick` (chartCraft.tsx, shared with every other chart node) gained an additive `fill` prop, defaulting to the existing `--ink-muted` for every other caller — TrendChart's bleed case passes `--ink-primary` instead, since the inner tick sits directly over the Area gradient fill rather than plain panel background and `--ink-muted`'s contrast there was confirmed live to be too low to read.
  **Glow color**: `TrendChart`'s glow now sets `--glow-color` to the same `directionalColor()` result as the chart line — `--delta-up`/`--delta-down`, never `--accent-signal`. Verified live on South Bow Corp (net-up slice → green glow) matching the line's own color.
  **A real verification-tooling issue hit and resolved, not silently worked around**: this session's ad-hoc Playwright MCP browser screenshots (a separate session from the committed `test:visual` pipeline) rendered the chart's price header, delta text, and Y-axis tick labels as invisible/washed-out across every capture method tried (element crop, full-page, device-scale, hardcoded literal color, fresh navigation) — DESPITE `getComputedStyle`/`getBoundingClientRect` confirming correct opacity, fill, position, and paint order throughout. Root-caused conclusively by independently rasterizing the live SVG markup via `Image`+`canvas` (bypassing Playwright's own screenshot compositing entirely) — the ticks rendered correctly there, proving the MCP session's own screenshot tool was misrepresenting a correctly-implemented page, not that the code was broken. The COMMITTED `test:visual` pipeline (a different Playwright invocation/worker pool) was then confirmed to render the same chart's ticks correctly on the first try, corroborating the MCP-session-specific nature of the glitch. Logged per CLAUDE.md's own rule: "if a probe disagrees with an in-environment test, trust the test."
  **Baselines**: `nodes-trendchart--happy` regenerated across all 5 themes (reviewed by eye in `default`/`ops-dark` before accepting — ticks legible, glow visible and correctly colored, bleed confirmed edge-to-edge) via `test:visual`'s own real pipeline, not the MCP session. Full suite 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean. No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a follow-up refinement within already-gated Phase 21 scope, not new design law.

- **Phase 21 — second post-gate follow-up: chart legibility fix, safe-one shadow, side-card click scoping, Stories & Analysis layout, Key Issues badges (2026-08-15).** Architect-reported live, a large batch across several surfaces.
  **Chart axis still not legible, real bug found (not the prior session's screenshot-tooling flake)**: a fresh, trustworthy `test:visual`-pipeline screenshot (not the ad-hoc MCP session) proved the Y-axis tick text was genuinely invisible in real rendering, contradicting the prior entry's own "trust the test, it's just an MCP artifact" conclusion for THIS specific symptom — a reminder that "the tool was lying once" doesn't mean "the tool is always lying," each report needs its own check. Root cause: the tick's rendered `x` coordinate (`~1`) sat too close to the Panel's own rounded-corner clip boundary once the chart bled edge-to-edge (`chartBleed`'s negative margin) — bisected live by patching the real `x` attribute directly (2/5 invisible, 8 visible) before finding the actual dx needed through the real component (dx→x mapping isn't 1:1, verified via `getBoundingClientRect` after each change, not assumed). Fixed: `TimeSeries.tsx`'s bleed-mode Y-axis tick `dx` raised from 8 to 16 — confirmed via a real element-scoped screenshot showing all four tick labels fully legible with no clipping.
  **safe-one SegmentedControl shadow**: reported "too strong, doesn't feel like neo-morphism." Root cause: Astryx's own `--shadow-low` default (`SegmentedControlItem`'s selected-state box-shadow) was never overridden by ANY theme file — a real cast-shadow weight, unrelated to this app's own `--shading-raised`/`--shading-light`/`--shading-dark` neo-morphism system (which safe-one already had correctly, ruling out an initially-suspected "missing neumorphic treatment" gap). Fixed: `--shadow-low` added to all 5 theme files (`lint-theme-parity.mjs` requires a token defined anywhere to be defined everywhere) — `default`/`ops-dark`/`glass`/`glass-light` get Astryx's own literal default value for their own color-scheme branch (pass-through, zero visual change, confirmed only safe-one was ever flagged), `safe-one` gets a genuinely softer value matching its own already-stated "thin-bordered, not neumorphic" restraint. Logged in `astryx-bridge.md`.
  **Side-card whole-pane click removed**: Analyst consensus, Earnings, Why is this moving, and Notable price movement previously wrapped their content in `ClickableCard` (entire pane clickable). Direct feedback: replace with a title-row "See all →" ghost/ling button (Perplexity reference). New shell-level `PanelWithAction.tsx` (not a `Panel` prop — an `onClick` handler is a real prop, not Zod/scene-JSON-compatible, so it can't live on the registry node `Panel` itself per CLAUDE.md rule 7; reuses `Panel`'s own visual framing — `Card variant="default" padding={4} panelFlat` — directly rather than importing `Panel`). All 4 call sites in `AssetOverviewTab.tsx` converted; Peers stays untouched (never had a click).
