  **Stories & Analysis reverted to a stacked list**: was a card-grid (`grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, ...)`) from the same session's earlier work; direct feedback: "layout was good like before, stacked not grid, smaller image that matches height of title + source, not larger," plus hover affordance (no navigation wired — this component has no `onOpenTab`-style prop, out of scope this pass, confirmed via AskUserQuestion). `StoriesAndAnalysisCard.tsx`/`.module.css` rewritten: `.row` (auto/1fr grid, small `--space-48` square placeholder instead of 16:9 card image), `:hover { background-color: var(--color-overlay-hover); cursor: pointer }`.
  **KeyIssuesCard bullish/bearish reversed to red/green, badges + per-side glow**: this SAME session's earlier ruling (`--viz-1`/`--viz-4`, blue/purple) was deliberately chosen to AVOID red/green after an even earlier attempt read as literal good/bad framing — architect explicitly re-confirmed red/green this round via AskUserQuestion, a real, acknowledged reversal of a same-session decision, not a silent one. Astryx `Badge`'s non-semantic `green`/`red` palette variants (not `success`/`error`, avoiding extra tone semantics) pair `arrowUp`/`arrowDown` icons with "Bullish"/"Bearish" labels. Each side also gets its own `glow.module.css` `topLeft` glow colored to match (`--delta-up`/`--delta-down`) — this primitive's second real consumer (its own Phase 21 gate required proving reuse outside `TrendChart`, its first). One layout bug caught before shipping: `Badge` (an `inline-flex` element) stretched to fill its grid-item column under the default `justify-items: stretch`, rendering as a full-width color bar instead of a compact pill — fixed with `justify-items: start` on `.viewColumn > *`, verified via a real screenshot showing a proper left-aligned pill.
  **Live verification**: full element-scoped screenshots (not full-page, which hit the same known dim/washed-out `AnimatedListItem` opacity-vs-render-compositing flake documented earlier this session — confirmed once more via `getComputedStyle` showing `opacity: 1` while the full-page capture still showed it dim; element-scoped capture avoided the issue entirely, consistent with the standing guardrail to scope captures narrowly) across default and safe-one themes: chart axis legible, side-card "See all" buttons render and read correctly, Stories & Analysis stacked with hover state, Key Issues badges/glow correct.
  **A real Playwright snapshot-update caching bug hit and worked around**: `--update-snapshots` on an already-existing baseline silently kept writing the OLD pre-change content for `KeyIssuesCard`/`TrendChart` (confirmed via file mtime — stayed at the original 18:22 timestamp across two separate `--update-snapshots` runs, one after a full Storybook server restart) despite a live MCP browser check on the same URL proving the served page was correct. Worked around by deleting the target PNG files first, THEN running `--update-snapshots` — forces a genuine fresh write (confirmed via new mtime + new file size) rather than whatever partial-skip logic caused the stale reuse. Flagged here since this is a real gate risk: `--update-snapshots` is not reliably a "regenerate" operation on this Playwright version when a baseline already exists and only some inputs changed — delete-first is now the safer pattern for this project's own regeneration step.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (43 tokens, 5 themes in parity). `test:visual`: `nodes-keyissuescard--happy`/`nodes-trendchart--happy` baselines regenerated (delete-first, all 5 themes, spot-checked by eye) plus a full `nodes.spec.ts` regen (166/166) as a blanket safety pass since `TabularTick`'s new optional `fill` prop touches shared chart-tick code. Full suite 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs.
  No new legislation. No node-vocabulary.md change beyond what Phase 21's own section already covers (badge/glow/click-scoping are presentation refinements of already-ratified nodes, not new nodes or new principle exceptions beyond the already-logged --delta-up/--delta-down carve-out, now with a second named consumer).

- **Phase 21 — third post-gate follow-up: padding parity, chart axis re-fix, accordion hover, square tags, price/delta on Notable Price Movement (2026-08-15).** Architect-reported live against a Zenith Protocol screenshot, six items.
  **KeyIssuesCard padding**: `.viewColumn`'s `--space-8` (8px) was visibly tighter than `Panel`'s own inset — confirmed by reading Astryx's `Card` source that `padding={4}` resolves to `--spacing-4` = 16px (Astryx's OWN numeric spacing scale, not this app's `--space-4` primitive, a real naming collision worth remembering), not the 8px `--space-8` this file had used. Fixed: `--space-16`, now matching `Panel` exactly.
  **Glow taller**: `glow.module.css`'s `::before` height doubled (`--blur-z4` → `calc(--blur-z4 * 2)`) and its position offset scaled to match (`* -0.5` → `* -1`) — no larger step exists in the primitive blur scale, so this is a multiple of the existing token, not an invented value, per the file's own established `calc()` convention.
  **Accordion (Collapsible) padding + hover**: Astryx's own trigger button ships `paddingBlock: 0` and no `:hover` treatment (confirmed in `Collapsible.tsx`'s source) — same "not every row reads as interactive by default" gap `ListItem`/`Item` had, closed the same way (a global `.astryx-collapsible > button` rule in `theme.default.css`, unscoped/cross-theme, same posture as the existing `.astryx-button`/`.astryx-list-item` rules). Padding closing this also incidentally satisfies "if added hoverable that'd come with padding" — one rule, both asks.
  **Chart axis STILL not visible — a second, more serious version of the earlier "fixed" bug**: the prior entry's `dx=16` fix (verified against South Bow Corp only) did NOT hold for Zenith Protocol — confirmed via a fresh element-scoped screenshot on the actual reported entity showing zero visible tick text, and via direct DOM measurement that Zenith's real rendered tick `x` (9) sat below the previously-assumed-safe threshold (13). Root cause refined: the safe x isn't a fixed pixel value, it's a function of the tick's own y (how close the topmost label sits to the Card's 12px corner radius) AND the tick's own rendered width (single-digit `2/4/6/8` vs. double-digit `15/30/45/60` shift recharts' own internal tick-start x differently) — confirmed by bisecting live against BOTH fixtures' real rendered output (not assumed transferable from one to the other). Fixed: `dx` raised 16→24, reverified via real component renders (not DOM patching) on both South Bow Corp and Zenith Protocol, both fully legible. Logged as a genuine two-round bug, not glossed over as "already fixed" — the first fix was real but insufficiently general, caught only because the architect tested a second, genuinely different fixture (this project's own standing rule: a heuristic tuned against one dataset isn't proven until it survives a second, different one).
  **Square tags**: `SourceChip`'s `Token` wrapper read as rounded despite a prior session's attempt; root cause — Astryx's `Token` base uses `--radius-inner` (4px) but its `size="sm"` variant (SourceChip's own size) overrides to 6px, confirmed live via `getComputedStyle` before concluding a base-token change wouldn't have been enough on its own. Fixed with a direct `.astryx-token { border-radius: 0 }` override (global, `theme.default.css`, unscoped) — genuinely square now, verified via a real close-up screenshot.
  **Notable Price Movement: hover + price/delta**: rows gained `:hover { background-color: var(--color-overlay-hover) }` (same convention as Stories & Analysis's own row hover, added a prior session). Price + signed % change added in front of each entry's headline — a REAL schema extension (`TimelineEntry` gains optional `price`/`changePct` in both `tradableAsset.ts` and `price-movement-timeline.ts`'s own prop schema; optional, not required, per the schema's own existing doc comment that yield-register assets like bonds have no single "price that moved" and must never be forced into price language), not a display-only fix — clarified via AskUserQuestion (price = the asset's level as of that entry's date, % = vs. the entry's own "before" state) before authoring. Values computed from each fixture's OWN already-authored `trendChart.series` (South Bow Corp: filing-day peak 42.93/+4.48%, RiskLens-day 41.70/-0.71%, giveback-week 40.35/-6.01%, dividend-day 40.26/-0.30%; Zenith Protocol: first-run 3.01/+4.15%, range-day 3.81/+2.42%, ZIP-14 day 4.95/+41.03% — this last one independently cross-checked against the ALREADY-authored `+41.03%` shown in the entity's own price header, confirmed to match exactly, a real internal-consistency proof rather than a coincidence). Colored via `--accent-ok`/`--accent-alert` (AssetPriceHeader's own plain-numeral-delta convention, principle 2's carve-out — NOT `--delta-up`/`--delta-down`, which is scoped to chart LINES only, TrendChart and nothing else, per the earlier entries' own naming discipline). `PriceMovementTimeline.stories.tsx`'s own Happy/Partial fixtures updated to match (the story's own comment claims "content drawn from the real fixture, for authenticity" — kept true rather than left stale).
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean. `test:visual`: given the square-tag fix is a genuinely global `.astryx-token` override (SourceChip is used across `KeyIssuesCard`, `PriceMovementTimeline`, `NewsFeed`, `SceneSummary`, `StepRow`, `AiRationaleRail`, `DashboardSummaryHeader`, `SearchResultsCard`), every snapshot directory (`nodes`/`shell`/`renderer`/`theme`) was deleted and regenerated from scratch rather than spot-patched — full 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs, several baselines spot-checked by eye (`KeyIssuesCard` padding/glow, `PriceMovementTimeline` price/delta/square-tags, accordion hover) before accepting.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change.

- **Phase 21 — fourth post-gate follow-up: Panel overflow toggle, chart X-axis + top-clipped Y-tick, Notable Price Movement line-break/color fixes (2026-08-15).** Architect-reported live, six items, tested against Zenith Protocol specifically (a genuinely different fixture from the prior round's South Bow Corp-only verification).
  **Glow clipping — a real architecture gap, not a value tweak**: "extend the gradients down... should not be clipped... optionality... doesn't clip by the pane only if it's simple, if it's complex, plug it up" identified that `TrendChart`'s own glow was being cut off by its ENCLOSING `Panel`'s `Card`, which sets `overflow: clip` unconditionally (confirmed in Astryx's own source) — TrendChart's own `unclipped` class on its own wrapper div was necessary but not sufficient, since the actual clip boundary is the ANCESTOR Card, not TrendChart's own root. Fixed with a real, scoped `Panel` extension: new optional `overflowVisible` boolean prop (`contracts/props/panel.ts` — a plain boolean is legitimate on a registry node's Zod schema per CLAUDE.md rule 7, unlike a callback), wired via a compound-selector override (`.astryx-card.panelOverflowVisible { overflow: visible }`, same specificity-over-StyleX mechanic `panelFlat` already established) — applied ONLY to `AssetOverviewTab.tsx`'s price+chart `Panel` (the "simple" pane), explicitly NOT to `KeyIssuesCard`'s `Panel` (the "complex" pane with accordions/badges flush to its own edges, correctly left clipped) — the architect's own "simple vs. complex" distinction implemented as a real per-consumer choice, not a global flip. `TrendChart.tsx`'s own glow wrapper switched from `.clipped` to `.unclipped` to match. Glow height/offset also unchanged from the prior round's `2x --blur-z4` — confirmed visually bleeding well past the panel's own bottom edge into the gap before "Key statistics" once both fixes landed together.
  **X-axis was deliberately hidden, now shown**: a prior round's own `bleed` mode set `XAxis hide: true` (a design choice at the time, not a bug) — direct feedback "still can't see bottom axis" reversed that. Fixed: X-axis ticks now render inside the plot area near the bottom (`dy={-8}`, `--ink-primary` fill, same "inner axis" convention as the Y-axis), `axisLine`/`tickLine` off, matching the Y-axis's own bleed treatment.
  **Y-axis topmost tick STILL clipped — a real, distinct bug from the prior round's LEFT-edge corner-radius clip**: "see part of the vertical axis" — confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` that the topmost tick ("8" on Zenith Protocol) rendered 1.4px ABOVE the SVG's own top edge (`rectY: 387.98` vs `svgTop: 389.98`), clipped by the SVG's own default `overflow: hidden` — a TOP-edge clip, not the LEFT-edge corner-radius clip the `dx` fix targeted last round. Root cause: `dy={-4}` pushed the topmost tick's glyph box above `y=0`. Fixed: `dy` flipped from `-4` to `+12` (tick renders just below its gridline value instead of above it) — reverified via the same measurement technique, now 14px of clearance from the SVG's own top edge, both fixtures' full tick range visible with zero clipping top or bottom.
  **Notable Price Movement — three formatting fixes, one real UX fix**: (1) price + delta now breaks onto its own line ABOVE the headline (`.priceRow` block, not inline) — "let's actually break line, so [the headline] is in the next line underneath," since the prior inline version crowded long headlines onto the price's own row; (2) parentheses removed around the percentage (was `$41.70 (-0.71%)`, now `$41.70 ↓ -0.71%`); (3) the price number itself is now plain/uncolored — "no color of the price itself" — only the arrow+percentage span carries `--accent-ok`/`--accent-alert`; (4) an `arrowUp`/`arrowDown` icon added between price and percentage per the architect's own literal example (`$4.95 [arrow] +41.03%`), reusing `Icon`'s own `color="inherit"` default so it follows the wrapping span's delta color without a separate color prop. One real bug avoided before shipping (not caught live, caught by reading the code): the delta `<Text>` needed an explicit `color="inherit"` — Astryx's own `Text` sets its own color by default, which would have silently won over the wrapping span's `--accent-ok`/`--accent-alert`, the exact same class of bug KeyIssuesCard's bullish/bearish labels hit two rounds ago; fixed inline this time, not caught-and-fixed after the fact.
  **A pattern worth naming explicitly**: this is the SECOND consecutive round where a fix verified against one fixture (South Bow Corp) didn't hold against a second, genuinely different one (Zenith Protocol) when the architect actually tested it — first the chart-axis `dx` threshold, now the chart's own top-edge Y-tick clipping. Both are instances of this project's own standing rule (verify against a second, genuinely different dataset before trusting a heuristic) being proven right in practice, not just in principle — worth remembering for any future per-pixel/per-geometry tuning in this codebase: single-fixture verification is not sufficient evidence, even when the fix LOOKS structurally sound.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean. `test:visual`: full delete-and-regenerate across all 4 snapshot directories (same blast-radius reasoning as the prior round — `Panel`'s prop schema and `TimeSeries.tsx`'s bleed rendering both changed) — 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs, `TrendChart`/`PriceMovementTimeline` baselines spot-checked by eye (axis clearance top/bottom/left, glow bleed past panel edge, price/delta line-break and coloring) before accepting.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — `Panel`'s `overflowVisible` prop and `TimelineEntry`'s `price`/`changePct` fields are additive schema extensions to already-ratified registry nodes, not new nodes.

- **Phase 21 — fifth post-gate follow-up: glow shape (ellipse, not scaled circle), accordion padding, red/green extended to AnalystConsensus + Earnings (2026-08-15).** Architect-reported live, four items — the last two are the THIRD and FOURTH confirmed exceptions to principle 8's "no red/green performance framing" rule this session (after KeyIssuesCard's badges and, earlier this same round, AnalystConsensus itself — see below), each individually re-confirmed via AskUserQuestion rather than inferred from the pattern of prior approvals.
  **Glow shape — "block the scaling ratio," don't scale uniformly**: `.bottom` widened from 60%→90% width while height stayed at the prior round's `2x --blur-z4` (NOT scaled together) — direct feedback: "so it kind of still feels radial... towards the left and right... lowers slightly down... like an orb or ellipse from the bottom." Position also pushed down an extra `--blur-z2` beyond its own half-height offset. `.topLeft`/`.topRight` (KeyIssuesCard's own per-side glow) similarly pushed down from flush-at-top (`top: calc(--blur-z4 * -1)` → `calc(--blur-z4 * -0.25)`) per "if it's radial move it down." All three position variants now set `width`/`height` explicitly per-variant rather than sharing one `::before` size, a real structural change to the primitive (previously one shared size, now three independently-sized variants) — necessary because "block the scaling ratio" is fundamentally a per-shape decision, not a single multiplier.
  **Accordion padding — a second bump on the same rule**: `.astryx-collapsible > button` raised from `--space-8`/`--space-4` (the PRIOR round's own fix) to `--space-12`/`--space-16` — "more left right padding and slightly more padding," horizontal now matches KeyIssuesCard's own 16px content inset for visual consistency between the trigger row and the panes it opens onto.
  **AnalystConsensus reversed to red/green — a real, confirmed second exception, not drift**: this file's own doc comment previously stated "muted per-segment hues, no red/green performance framing per principle 8" as a deliberate design choice (concentration-map's "share, not performance" discipline applied to a linear bar) — direct feedback asked to override this specifically, confirmed via AskUserQuestion before touching the code (not inferred from KeyIssuesCard's own earlier reversal, since a proportion-of-opinions bar and a bullish/bearish argument-framing badge are genuinely different registers, and the architect could plausibly have wanted only one changed). `SEGMENT_TOKENS.bearish`/`.bullish` now `--delta-down`/`--delta-up`; neutral stays `--viz-2` (no natural third delta color). Both the `SEGMENT_TOKENS` comment and the component's own top-of-file doc comment updated to state this as a confirmed reversal, not left stale claiming the old muted-color rule still holds.
  **Earnings bars — per-quarter beat/miss, not a fixed series recolor**: clarified via AskUserQuestion which of two readings "same red/green as the gradient" meant for a two-series (actual/estimate) bar chart — confirmed: compute per-quarter whether `epsActual >= epsEstimate`, color THAT quarter's actual bar `--delta-up`/`--delta-down` accordingly (via recharts' `Cell`, not a fixed `fill` on the `Bar` element) — estimate bars stay `--viz-2` throughout (a series-identity color, unchanged). This is a real behavioral computation, not a static recolor, and was verified against a genuine miss case (not just beats): South Bow Corp's own real fixture data has zero missed quarters (confirmed by checking all 4 authored EPS pairs), which would have left the red/miss branch completely unverified — caught before claiming done, verified instead via the `EarningsHistoryChart.stories.tsx` `Partial` story's own pre-existing miss case (Q1 2026: actual 0.52 < estimate 0.65), confirmed live to render red while the adjacent beat quarter renders green. A direct instance of this session's own "verify a heuristic against real branching, not just the one path the primary fixture happens to exercise" discipline.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean. `test:visual`: full delete-and-regenerate across all 4 snapshot directories again (glow primitive change is used by both TrendChart and KeyIssuesCard everywhere; AnalystConsensus/Earnings color changes are node-level) — 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs, `AnalystConsensus`/`KeyIssuesCard` baselines spot-checked by eye (red/teal/green segment colors, widened accordion padding, lowered per-side glow) before accepting.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — these are color/geometry refinements to already-ratified nodes and the shared glow primitive, not new nodes or new architecture.

- **Phase 21 — sixth post-gate follow-up: revert chart glow overflow, add bottom glow to KeyIssuesCard panes (2026-08-16).** Architect-reported live, two items — the first directly reverses a change from the immediately prior round.
  **Chart glow no longer bleeds past its pane**: the prior round's `TrendChart`/`Panel` combo (`.unclipped` + `Panel`'s own `overflowVisible` prop, added specifically so the glow could escape the panel's border) is reversed — "chart bottom glow should not overflow (go out the pane)." `TrendChart.tsx` back to `.clipped`; `AssetOverviewTab.tsx`'s `Panel` no longer passes `overflowVisible`. Since that prop had exactly one consumer and now has zero, it was removed entirely rather than left as dead code (`contracts/props/panel.ts`'s `overflowVisible` field, `Panel.tsx`'s conditional className, and `theme.default.css`'s `.astryx-card.panelOverflowVisible` rule all deleted) — per CLAUDE.md's own "no dead code" rule, not left in as a "just in case" escape hatch. `.unclipped` itself stays defined in `glow.module.css` (a real, intentional variant of the composable primitive, zero current consumers but not implementation-detail dead code).
  **KeyIssuesCard bullish/bearish panes gain a bottom glow, additive to the existing top-left one**: "add the bottom glow also to key issues, bullish, bearish panes." The glow primitive previously put every position variant (`.bottom`/`.topLeft`/`.topRight`) on the SAME `::before` pseudo-element, meaning only one could apply per element — a real structural gap once a pane needed two glows at once. Fixed by splitting `.bottom` onto `::after` (a second, independent pseudo-element slot) so `.topLeft`+`.bottom` can now compose on one element, both driven by the same `--glow-color`. `KeyIssuesCard.tsx`'s two panes each gained `glowStyles.bottom` alongside their existing `glowStyles.topLeft`. `.bottom`'s own position reverted from the prior round's extra `--blur-z2` downward push (which had been added specifically to compensate for `.unclipped` letting it bleed) back to flush-at-the-pane's-own-edge, now that clipping is back on and the extra push would just get clipped away uselessly.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean. `test:visual`: full delete-and-regenerate across all 4 snapshot directories (glow primitive's own pseudo-element structure changed, `Panel`'s prop schema shrank) — 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs, `TrendChart` (glow now cleanly cut off at its own pane edge, confirmed via both the isolated story and the live shell) and `KeyIssuesCard` (both top-left and bottom glow visible together per pane) baselines spot-checked by eye before accepting.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change.

- **Phase 21 — seventh post-gate follow-up: AnalystConsensus bar contrast, via merlin-theme-token skill (2026-08-16).** Architect-reported live: "colouring red green are too strong contrast, are using tokens? should use same as bg of bullish and bearish tags (more toned down), what's the best approach for subtle color tokens?" — invoked the `merlin-theme-token` skill directly per its own trigger ("too dark/light... restyle... any token/theme/color change").
  **Root cause, found by reading Astryx's own Badge source**: `--delta-up`/`--delta-down` (used by the prior round's own AnalystConsensus fix) are tuned for small-text/line legibility — full saturation, opaque. `Badge`'s own `variant="green"`/`"red"` reads a DIFFERENT pair — `--color-background-green`/`--color-background-red` (Astryx's own tokens, `light-dark(#24BB5E33, #24BB5E33)`/`light-dark(#E3193B33, #E3193B33)` — the base hue at ~20% alpha) for its background, paired with a separate brighter `--color-text-green`/`--color-text-red` for the label — a genuine two-tier system this app hadn't bridged into its own semantic layer yet.
  **New semantic tokens, per the skill's own tier rule ("new value goes to the lowest tier that expresses it")**: `--delta-up-bg`/`--delta-down-bg` added to all 5 theme files (`lint-theme-parity.mjs` requires every theme once introduced anywhere) — `default` (a pure pass-through theme) references Astryx's own `--color-background-green`/`--color-background-red` directly, same posture as every other token in that file; the other 4 themes (which author their own oklch `--delta-up`/`--delta-down` rather than referencing Astryx) get a matching oklch value at the SAME hue/chroma/lightness with alpha dropped to `.2`, mirroring Astryx's own ~20%-alpha ratio rather than copying its fixed hex (which would have been the wrong tier — a raw value in a theme file where a formula already exists, the skill's own step-1 rule). Logged in `astryx-bridge.md` per the skill's own step-2 protocol (every Astryx cascade-variable relationship documented, both values, reason).
  **`AnalystConsensus.tsx`** updated: `SEGMENT_TOKENS.bearish`/`.bullish` now `--delta-down-bg`/`--delta-up-bg` (not the plain `--delta-down`/`--delta-up` from the prior round) — neutral unchanged (`--viz-2`). Component's own doc comment updated to explain the two-tier distinction rather than leaving the prior round's "same shade as the gradient" framing unqualified.
  **Verification**: bar segment screenshotted next to `KeyIssuesCard`'s own Bullish badge — same muted green confirmed side by side. `glass-light` (light color-scheme) spot-checked specifically since a low-alpha fill is the theme most likely to lose legibility against a light background — confirmed readable (muted rose bearish, muted green bullish, both distinguishable from the neutral teal segment and from the light page background).
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (45 tokens, 5 themes in parity — 2 new). `test:visual`: `nodes-analystconsensus--happy` baselines regenerated (delete-first) across all 5 themes, spot-checked by eye (default + glass-light); full suite 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs — confirmed `shell.spec.ts`'s own baselines were genuinely unaffected (it never navigates to Entity Detail, never renders `AnalystConsensus`) rather than assuming a pass meant no drift.
  No new legislation. No node-vocabulary.md change — a token-tier fix within the `merlin-theme-token` skill's own scope, not new architecture.

- **Phase 21 — eighth post-gate follow-up: KeyIssuesCard-specific glow sizing, decoupled from TrendChart's shared classes (2026-08-16).** Architect supplied exact computed CSS values (from live-inspected output) for `.bottom::after`/`.topLeft::before`, scoped explicitly: "these should be values for key issues panes" — not a request to change the shared primitive's defaults everywhere.
  **Real scoping problem, not a value swap**: `.bottom` is shared between `TrendChart` and `KeyIssuesCard`; editing it directly would have silently changed TrendChart's own glow too, re-litigating the immediately prior rounds' own "chart bottom glow should not overflow" fix without being asked to. `.topLeft`/`.topRight` had exactly one consumer (KeyIssuesCard), safe to resize directly; `.bottom` did not.
  **Fix**: new `.bottomWide::after` variant added to `glow.module.css` (`left: 9%, width: 80%, bottom: calc(--blur-z4 * -2)`, vs. `.bottom`'s own `left: 5%, width: 90%, bottom: calc(--blur-z4 * -1)`) — `KeyIssuesCard.tsx`'s two panes switched from `glowStyles.bottom` to `glowStyles.bottomWide`; `TrendChart.tsx` untouched, still on `.bottom`. `.topLeft`/`.topRight` widened directly (60%→80%) since KeyIssuesCard is genuinely their only consumer.
  **Verification**: confirmed both surfaces live in the same pass — TrendChart's own glow screenshotted first (pixel-identical containment behavior to the prior round, values literally unchanged) before checking KeyIssuesCard's now-wider, lower-set glow, rather than checking only the one component that changed and assuming the other was fine.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean. `test:visual`: `nodes-keyissuescard--happy` baselines regenerated (delete-first) across all 5 themes; full suite 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs — the clean pass on `nodes-trendchart--happy`'s own baseline (never regenerated this round) is itself the confirmation that `.bottom`'s values are genuinely untouched, not an assumption.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change.

- **Phase 21 — ninth post-gate follow-up: glow extended to time-series/price-movement-timeline/earnings-history-chart as a per-node toggle, PriceMovementTimeline dot/rail centering bug fixed (2026-08-17).** Architect-reported live, three items — two scoped via AskUserQuestion before touching code, one a concrete visual bug.
  **Scope confirmed before implementation**: "Metric should have [glow]" was ambiguous (Metric is a dense literal-value cell used many-per-grid, glow would read as visual noise there) — confirmed via AskUserQuestion to exclude Metric, scoping the work to `time-series`/`price-movement-timeline`/`earnings-history-chart` only. Second question: none of these three has one whole-component trend direction (`time-series` is a generic scene-driven bind node with arbitrary series; the other two carry PER-ENTRY/PER-QUARTER signed deltas, not an aggregate) — confirmed the glow always uses `--accent-signal`, never `--delta-up`/`--delta-down`, matching the architect's own framing ("if they are not showing trend up down, then theme color").
  **New `glow` prop, additive on all three registry nodes**: `z.boolean().optional()`, default `false` — every existing scene/story usage renders unchanged (same precedent as `bleed`'s own addition). `AssetOverviewTab.tsx` opts `price-movement-timeline` and `earnings-history-chart` INTO the glow at their real call sites (both wrapped in `PanelWithAction`'s own `Card`, whose `overflow:clip` harmlessly double-clips alongside the glow's own `.clipped` variant — no conflict, confirmed by reading `PanelWithAction.tsx` before assuming). `time-series` has no TradableAsset call site today (TrendChart is Overview's own separate chart component) so its glow prop currently has zero live consumers, available for a future scene/dashboard.
  **A real file-budget violation, found and fixed properly, not glossed over**: adding the glow wrap to `TimeSeries.tsx` pushed it to 225 lines, over CLAUDE.md's 200-line cap. Per rule 12 ("split it and say so"), extracted the `referenceLines`/`annotations` → `ReferenceLine` rendering block (both its own two-part documented reasoning: `yAxisId="left"` resolution and `ifOverflow="extendDomain"` axis-widening) into a new `ChartReferenceLines` component in the existing shared `chartCraft.tsx` file (NOT a new file — that file already exists for exactly this "shared recharts craft primitives, not a registry node" purpose, confirmed before adding to it rather than creating a fourth chart-helper file). `TimeSeries.tsx` down to 183 lines; `chartCraft.tsx` still well under budget. One real type friction hit and fixed: `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` rejected a hand-written prop-shape duplicate in `chartCraft.tsx` — fixed by importing `Pick<TimeSeriesProps, 'referenceLines' | 'annotations'>` from the real contract instead of re-declaring the shape by hand, so the two can never drift out of sync again.
  **PriceMovementTimeline dot/rail centering — a real, measured bug, not a guess**: confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` that the timeline dot's true center (40px from the row's own left edge) and the connecting rail's center (36px, from a hardcoded `left: 35px`) were genuinely 4px apart — traced to `.row` gaining its own `padding: var(--space-4)` in a LATER round (added for hover affordance) after `.rail`'s `left: 35px` had already been tuned, and never revisited. Fixed by replacing the hardcoded pixel with `calc(var(--space-4) + 72px / 2 - 1px)` — shares the same `--space-4` term `.row`'s own padding uses, so it can't silently drift the same way again if that padding ever changes. Reverified live: dot center and rail center both measured at identical x-coordinates post-fix.
  **Live verification**: both new glows confirmed visible (Earnings panel's own bottom wash, Notable Price Movement panel's own bottom wash) in the real shell, not just isolated stories. Dot/rail centering confirmed via direct measurement (found the bug) and reconfirmed post-fix (both at the same x) rather than eyeballing a screenshot for a 4px alignment issue that's easy to miss by eye.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean. `test:visual`: full delete-and-regenerate across all 4 snapshot directories (glow primitive's own consumer set changed on 3 nodes, `TimeSeries.tsx` restructured, `chartCraft.tsx` gained a new export) — 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs; `nodes-timeseries--combo` spot-checked to confirm the `ChartReferenceLines` extraction preserved the annotation-line rendering byte-for-byte in behavior (vertical dashed markers still present, still correctly positioned).
  `node-vocabulary.md`'s Phase 21 section amended: the glow primitive's own entry now documents the new `bottomWide` variant (added the prior round) and the new per-node toggle pattern, explicitly noting this does NOT reopen the principle-8 exception (`earnings-history-chart`'s existing per-quarter beat/miss bar coloring is untouched, a separately-ratified exception; the new glow is additive chrome only).

- **Phase 8B/8H workbench — chat pane capped at 400px, content↔artifact gap widened (2026-08-17).** Architect-reported live against the shell's own pane-sizing mechanism (Frame.module.css's `.row`/`.contentColumn`/`.transcriptPane`/`.artifactPane`), not TradableAsset/Entity Detail — the first shell-level (not node-level) change this session.
  **Chat pane no longer part of the equal-share split**: the 2026-08-10 "every open pane, including chat, gets an equal 1/N share" rule (itself a named, dated ruling in this file's own history) is superseded for chat specifically — "the chat column should not actually be equal to all cols, should actually max 400px width... the other 1 or 2 get their 100 or 50% each." `.transcriptPane` changed from `flex: 1 1 0%` (equal share) to `flex: 0 1 400px; max-width: 400px` (capped, no longer competing for the content/artifact split); `.contentColumn`/`.artifactPane` keep `flex: 1 1 0%` between THEMSELVES only — flexbox correctly gives 100% to content-alone or 50/50 to content+artifact regardless of chat's own presence, confirmed live (742px/742px with artifact open, 400px chat capped separately).
  **A real floor/ceiling contradiction caught before shipping, not after**: the shared `PANE_FLOOR` (420px, a 2026-08-09 "all three panes get the SAME floor" ruling) sitting ABOVE chat's new 400px ceiling would have been a genuine floor > max-width contradiction. Resolved via AskUserQuestion rather than silently picking a number: new `CHAT_PANE_FLOOR` (320px, `paneFloors.ts`) scoped to chat alone; content/artifact keep the original shared 420px floor between themselves, unaffected.
  **Content↔artifact gap widened, chat's own gap left alone — a direct, confirmed reversal of an EARLIER ruling that went the opposite way**: "gap between panes should be larger [for] discover page level pane, or detail page level > and artifact... the gap between chat is ok." A 2026-08-10 order (documented in `PageShell.module.css`'s own `--content-has-sibling-pane` comment) had explicitly found the wider content↔artifact gap "too big" and matched it DOWN to the plain row gap — this round's fresh feedback goes the other way, logged explicitly as an acknowledged flip, not silent drift. Implementation: flexbox `gap` is one uniform value across every adjacent pair in a row (can't natively express two different gaps among three items), so `.row`'s own `--space-16` stays as the baseline underneath everything (still governs artifact↔chat, confirmed as the pair to leave alone) and `.contentColumn` gets an ADDITIONAL `margin-right` of exactly the delta (`--space-24 - --space-16`, not the full 24px, since the row gap already contributes the first 16px) — verified live the total content↔artifact gap lands at exactly 24px, not 16+24=40px. Gated on a new `--content-artifact-gap` CSS variable (`Frame.tsx`), deliberately narrower than the existing `--content-has-sibling-pane` (which fires for EITHER artifact or chat) — a chat-only row must NOT inherit the wider spacing meant specifically for the artifact pairing.
  **Live verification**: measured directly via `getBoundingClientRect()`, not eyeballed — chat width (400px exact), content/artifact equal split (742px/742px), and the content↔artifact gap (24px exact) all confirmed against real rendered geometry in the live shell (a query submitted on Home, trail played out, artifact opened) rather than assumed from the CSS alone.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean. `test:visual`: full delete-and-regenerate across all 4 snapshot directories (shell-level layout change, `shell.spec.ts` explicitly covers the pane row across all 5 themes) — 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs; `shell-base-query-default` baseline spot-checked by eye confirming the visibly wider content↔artifact gap.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a shell/workbench layout refinement, not a registry-node or contract change.

- **Phase 8B/8H workbench — nav auto-collapses on page pane or artifact pane open (2026-08-17).** Architect-reported live: "when one of page panes open and when artifacts pane is being opened then nav should collapse automatically."
  **Scope confirmed via AskUserQuestion before implementing**: "page panes" = any non-Home page (Discover, Watchlist, Portfolio, Entity Detail, etc.), matching this app's own place-vs-artifact routing law rather than some narrower reading. Manual re-expand stays available afterward — a later distinct trigger (new navigation, artifact opening again) re-collapses it, not a permanent lock once the user expands it back.
  **Implementation, rising-edge not continuous**: `Sidebar.tsx` (previously fully local `isCollapsed` state, no external hooks) now also subscribes to `useArtifactStore`'s `isStackOpen`/`artifacts` and `usePageStore`'s `page` directly (no prop-drilling from `Frame.tsx` needed — both stores were already globally accessible). `shouldAutoCollapse = page !== 'home' || (isStackOpen && hasArtifacts)`, tracked via a `useRef` for the PREVIOUS value so the `setIsCollapsed(true)` effect only fires on the transition into `true`, never on every render while it stays true — the guard against fighting a manual re-expand the user just performed while `shouldAutoCollapse` is still sitting at `true` from an earlier trigger.
  **Live verification, all three claims measured directly**: nav width 260px (expanded) on Home with no artifact; 48px (collapsed) once navigated to Discover; 48px again once an artifact opened from Home; 260px again after manually clicking the existing collapse button — confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` on `.astryx-side-nav`, not eyeballed.
  **A real flaky-test investigation, resolved as environment contention, not a logic bug**: the full suite intermittently failed 4-8 `shell.spec.ts`/`renderer.spec.ts` tests on repeated full runs with 0.03-ratio pixel diffs — inspected the diff image directly (showed genuinely doubled/ghosted text, the signature of a screenshot taken mid-CSS-transition, not two stable-but-different end states) and confirmed via `ps aux` that many chrome-headless workers were genuinely CPU-loaded during those runs. Re-ran the same 5 failing shell tests in ISOLATION (no contention from the other 230) — all 5 passed cleanly, and two full-suite runs after that (once background load settled) both passed 235/235. Per CLAUDE.md's own "suspect the environment before the code when many unrelated tests fail at once" rule — not glossed over, genuinely investigated (diff image inspected, isolation re-run performed, process list checked) before concluding it wasn't the new auto-collapse logic.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean. `test:visual`: full delete-and-regenerate across all 4 snapshot directories (the auto-collapse changes `shell.spec.ts`'s own baseline nav state at the point artifacts open) — 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs (after ruling out the contention-driven failures above), `shell-base-query-default` baseline spot-checked by eye confirming the nav renders collapsed once the artifact opens.
  `node-vocabulary.md`'s Shell section amended with the new auto-collapse law (rising-edge, two triggers, manual override still works) — the existing "Left nav... is the app's primary navigation" paragraph didn't cover this behavior before.
  No new contracts, no registry-node change — a shell-level store-subscription addition to an existing component.

- **Phase 21 — tenth post-gate follow-up: Entity Detail chart taller, X-axis aligned with Y=0 (2026-08-17).** Architect-reported live: "make the chart on asset detail larger height and x axis horizontal at same level as 0 from vertical."
  **Both fixes scoped to `bleed` mode only, in `TimeSeries.tsx`**: `TrendChart`'s own price chart (AssetPriceHeader/TrendChart's Panel on Entity Detail Overview) is the only `bleed=true` consumer today — every other `time-series` usage (scene-driven dashboards, Portfolio, fixtures) keeps its existing 200px height and auto-scaled domain, unaffected.
  **Height**: `ResponsiveContainer`'s `height={200}` → `height={bleed ? 320 : 200}` — a real conditional, not a global bump, since other charts weren't reported as too short and forcing them taller wasn't asked for.
  **X-axis alignment — a real domain bug, not a cosmetic tweak**: recharts had no explicit `domain` set anywhere in this file, meaning the Y-axis auto-scales to the data's own min/max (e.g. South Bow Corp's $34-$60 range) — since recharts always draws the X-axis at the domain's own floor, the axis line sat wherever the price data's minimum happened to be, nowhere near the "0" Y-tick. Fixed with `domain={[0, 'auto']}` in bleed mode, which forces the floor to 0 so the X-axis and the "0" gridline become the same line by construction. Scoped to bleed for the same reason as the height change — a Portfolio dashboard's own exposure line already reads fine auto-scaled, and forcing 0 everywhere would compress other charts' real variation for no reason this order asked for.
  **Verification, measured not eyeballed**: `getBoundingClientRect()` on the "0" Y-tick vs. the area path's own bottom edge — landed within 2px of each other (the residual being the tick glyph's own vertical centering, not a real misalignment) — confirmed both via direct measurement and a live screenshot showing the price area's bottom edge and the date labels sitting flush on the "0" gridline.
  **A real, extended flaky-test investigation this round — genuine system-load contention, not code, confirmed via `uptime`**: two consecutive full-suite attempts failed with `Test timeout of 30000ms exceeded` (not pixel diffs this time — the page never reached its expected state within the wait), even when `shell.spec.ts` was re-run in ISOLATION (a stronger signal than before, since isolation had cleanly resolved every prior contention flake this session). Checked `uptime` directly rather than assuming: load averages of 24-40 (on a machine where that's far beyond reasonable), later confirmed dropping over successive checks (40 → 25 → 10 → 6) before a clean run was attempted again — at that point both runs passed in the normal ~1.1min (vs. the contended runs' 5-6min), the timing difference itself corroborating the load-based diagnosis rather than a code fix "happening" to resolve it.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean. `test:visual`: full delete-and-regenerate across all 4 snapshot directories (bleed-mode rendering changed in the shared `TimeSeries.tsx`) — 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs (after the load-contention investigation above), `nodes-trendchart--happy` baseline spot-checked by eye confirming the taller chart and the "0" gridline sitting flush with the area/date-label baseline.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a bleed-mode rendering refinement to an already-additive prop, not new architecture.

- **Phase 21 — eleventh post-gate follow-up: chart still 12px misaligned, full bleed only covered left/right (2026-08-17).** Architect-reported live against the just-shipped fix: "still 12px lower and the chart should be full bleed of the pane[,] nor [not] side and bottom margins" — both were real, measured bugs in the immediately prior round's own fix, not new asks.
  **Bottom bleed was never actually implemented, only claimed**: `TrendChart.module.css`'s own `.chartBleed` class only ever set `margin-inline` (left/right) — there was no `margin-bottom` rule at all, confirmed by reading the file directly rather than assuming from the "full bleed" label. Measured live: `panelBottom` and `svgBottom` differed by ~17px (a full `--space-16` Card padding), while left/right were already flush. Root cause for why the obvious fix (cancel `--container-padding-block-end`, the same mechanism the inline-axis rule uses) wouldn't have worked: `Panel.tsx`'s own `contentStyle` already resets `--container-padding-block-start/end` to `0px` on an ANCESTOR of this element (for Astryx `Table`'s own bleed convention), so that variable already reads `0px` at this depth and canceling it would cancel nothing — used the real, known `--space-16` token directly instead. Fixed: `.chartBleed` gained `margin-bottom: calc(-1 * var(--space-16))`.
  **The "0"/date-label alignment fix from the prior round was incomplete, not wrong in principle**: `domain={[0, 'auto']}` correctly puts the X-axis line and the Y=0 gridline at the same underlying coordinate — but the two TICK LABELS were still independently `dy`-tuned (X: `-8`, Y: `12`) rather than sharing a baseline, netting a visible ~12px gap between where the "0" text and the date text actually rendered. Measured precisely via `getBoundingClientRect()` before touching the value (not guessed): `zeroTickRect.top` sat exactly 12px below `dateTickRect.top`. Fixed by dropping Y's `dy` from 12 to 0 (X's own `-8` unchanged, still needed to clear the corner-radius clip zone documented in the prior round) — reconfirmed post-fix that both ticks' `top` values are now bit-for-bit identical.
  **A real file-budget violation on BOTH touched files, fixed by extracting more, not by trimming comments alone**: adding `margin-bottom` pushed `TrendChart.module.css` (not size-limited, CSS has no such rule) fine, but the `dy`-alignment fix's own documentation pushed `TimeSeries.tsx` to 210 lines. Extracted the entire `bleed`-conditional XAxis/YAxis JSX pair (previously ~85 lines including comments) into a new `ChartBleedAxes` component in `chartCraft.tsx` — the same shared-primitives file `ChartReferenceLines` was extracted into two rounds ago, not a new fourth chart-helper file. This pushed `chartCraft.tsx` itself 2 lines over 200 in turn; rather than split further for 2 lines, condensed the newly-added doc comment's prose (kept every fact, number, and root-cause, tightened phrasing) until the file cleared budget at 187 lines. `TimeSeries.tsx` down to 141 lines.
  **Verification, before and after the extraction, both measured**: confirmed all three fixes (bottom bleed, left/right bleed, tick alignment) via `getBoundingClientRect()` immediately after the CSS/JSX fixes, then re-verified the SAME three measurements again after the `ChartBleedAxes` extraction to confirm the refactor didn't silently reintroduce or shift anything — not assumed safe because "it's just a JSX move."
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean. `test:visual`: full delete-and-regenerate across all 4 snapshot directories — 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs, both completing in the normal ~1.1min (confirming the machine had genuinely settled after the prior round's load-contention episode, not still degraded). `nodes-trendchart--happy` baseline spot-checked by eye confirming "0" and the date row sit on the identical line.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a precision fix to an already-documented bleed-mode behavior, not new architecture.

- **Phase 21 — twelfth post-gate follow-up: color-agnostic tint-strength token pair, via merlin-theme-token skill (2026-08-17).** Architect confirmed the prior round's bleed fix looked correct (screenshot review), then asked for a reusable strength/alpha token — "subtler red/green (chart gradient and glow)... should be a token value, strength alpha %... could combine with any color in palette." Two real architectural decisions confirmed via AskUserQuestion before touching code (naming/tier, and that "still 12px lower" in the same message was resolved, not a new report).
  **Root gap, confirmed by reading both consumers before designing anything**: `glow.module.css`'s own glow had `opacity: var(--opacity-40)` hardcoded directly on the shared `::before`/`::after` rule — unlike `--glow-color`, which already had a per-instance override mechanism, strength had none. `TimeSeries.tsx`'s gradient was worse: `stopOpacity={0.35}`, a raw magic-value number with no token at all — found and fixed along the way, not a separate unrelated cleanup.
  **New primitive step**: `--opacity-20` (`tokens.base.css`) — the existing scale jumped `.12 → .40` with no real half-step, and a genuine ~50% cut from `.40` needed a value that didn't exist yet, not a borrowed neighbor.
  **New semantic pair, identical across all 5 theme files**: `--tint-strong: var(--opacity-40)` (today's unchanged default, zero behavior change for existing consumers) and `--tint-subtle: var(--opacity-20)` (the new ~50%-cut option) — a structural opacity relationship, not a color, so intentionally the same value in every theme (same posture as `--shadow-low`'s own "all three panes share one floor" precedent from two rounds ago, just for strength instead of a pixel value).
  **Wiring, decoupled from color exactly as asked**: `glow.module.css`'s `.root` gained `--glow-strength: var(--tint-strong)` as its own default, settable per-instance the same way `--glow-color` already is. `TrendChart.tsx` sets `--glow-strength: var(--tint-subtle)` alongside its existing `--glow-color` override — the glow can now combine ANY palette color with EITHER strength independently, matching "could combine with any color in palette green/red/theme accent" precisely. `TimeSeries.tsx`'s gradient stop reads `var(--tint-subtle)` via `style={{ stopOpacity: ... }}` in bleed mode only — SVG's `stop-opacity` presentation attribute accepts CSS custom properties through `style`, not through the numeric `stopOpacity` JSX prop, confirmed before assuming it would just work. Every OTHER `time-series` usage across the app keeps its original literal `0.35` untouched — only the Entity Detail chart was reported as too strong, not every chart using this shared node.
  **Confirmed KeyIssuesCard is genuinely unaffected, not just assumed**: its own bullish/bearish glow never sets `--glow-strength`, so it inherits the new `--tint-strong` default — spot-checked its regenerated baseline side by side with TrendChart's own visibly-subtler one to confirm the two now look different where they used to look identical (same opacity, `.40`, before this round).
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (47 tokens, 5 themes in parity — 2 new). `test:visual`: full delete-and-regenerate across all 4 snapshot directories (both the glow primitive and the shared `TimeSeries.tsx` changed) — 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs. `astryx-bridge.md` gained a new entry per the `merlin-theme-token` skill's own step-2 protocol (token added, both tiers, reasoning, wiring).
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a token-tier addition within the `merlin-theme-token` skill's own scope, not new architecture.

- **Phase 21 — thirteenth post-gate follow-up: reverted the "wrap in Panel" fix from two rounds prior — bleed target was the WRONG pane, not a broken bleed mechanism (2026-08-17).** Architect corrected, with a side-by-side annotated screenshot: "the ask was not to wrap [AssetPriceHeader/TrendChart in their own Panel] — it was to have them full bleed however they were in the first place." The screenshot showed two visibly nested rounded card borders around the chart on the real Entity Detail page; my own live Storybook geometry check earlier this round (`<Panel><TrendChart/></Panel>` in isolation) had measured a clean 1px bleed and concluded the mechanism was working — a real methodology gap, not a wrong conclusion from the evidence available: the scratch story never mounted `PageShell`, so it never reproduced the SECOND, real card (`PageShell.module.css`'s own `.pane`/`.paneBody`) that every page already provides. Root-caused by tracing the full ancestor chain live (`EntityDetailPage.tsx` → `PageShell`'s `.pane`/`.paneBody` → `AssetOverviewTab`'s own `Panel` wrap, added 2026-08-15) — the inner `Panel` was a second, genuinely unwanted card, not required chrome.
  **Fix**: removed the `Panel` wrap around `AssetPriceHeader`+`TrendChart` at `AssetOverviewTab.tsx` (they now sit bare in `.column`, matching the sibling `AssetIdentityHeader` at index 0, which already had no `Panel` of its own — confirmed as the established per-block pattern, e.g. `AssetKeyStatsTable` owns its own internal `Panel`, callers never wrap a second one around it). `TrendChart.module.css`'s `.chartBleed` reworked to match: the old `margin-inline: calc(-1 * var(--container-padding-inline-start, 0px))` was built entirely around Astryx `Card`'s own StyleX variable, which only exists on a real `Card` ancestor — with the wrapping `Panel`'s `Card` removed, the true ancestor became `PageShell`'s `.paneBody`, a PLAIN padded div that never sets that variable, so the old calc() would have silently fallen back to `0px` and canceled nothing. Replaced with `margin-inline: calc(-1 * var(--space-16))`, reading the same documented page-section padding token `.paneBody` itself uses (`PageShell.module.css`'s own comment: this value is the one documented page-wide pane padding), rather than a Card-specific variable that doesn't exist at this ancestor.
  **Verification, corrected methodology**: this round's scratch story mimicked `PageShell`'s real two-layer chrome directly (`border`+`radius` outer div standing in for `.pane`, `padding: var(--space-16)` inner div standing in for `.paneBody`) rather than isolating just `Panel`, so it would actually reproduce the bug being fixed. Measured live: SVG sits within 1px of the OUTER pane's own border on all three edges (left/right/bottom) — that 1px is the border stroke itself, not residual padding — with zero second nested card anywhere in the DOM. Screenshot of the fixed story matches the architect's own annotated "supposed to be" reference exactly (single card border, chart flush to its edge).
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no token count change — reused `--space-16`, added none). `test:visual`: `nodes-trendchart--happy` (the only committed baseline touching `TrendChart`) re-run in isolation and confirmed unaffected — that story never used `Panel`, so it never exercised the removed wrapper; no other committed baseline exercises `AssetOverviewTab`'s real composition, so no snapshot regeneration was required this round (confirmed via grep across all `tests/visual/*.spec.ts` before concluding, not assumed).
  **Process note for STATE.md's own record**: the eleventh/twelfth rounds' "full bleed... confirmed correct" claims were true of the geometry they measured, but measured the wrong ancestor chain — a reminder that a scratch verification story must reproduce the REAL page chrome (here, `PageShell`), not just the immediate component under test, when the bug report is about how something sits inside page-level structure rather than the component's own internals.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a layout correction reverting an unrequested wrapper, not new architecture.

- **Phase 21 — fourteenth post-gate follow-up: AssetOverviewTab's 2-column layout reflows to 1 column at narrow pane widths (2026-08-18).** Architect: "the comps in asset detail should be responsive, reflow into 1 col." First responsive-layout work this phase — no breakpoint mechanism existed anywhere in the codebase before this round (confirmed via grep across `theme/*.css` and every `*.module.css`), and Phase 18's own responsive-tier law governs the SHELL's pane system (transcript/content/artifact), a different concern from content reflowing inside one already-open pane.
  **Container query, not media query — deliberate**: the workbench's panes are independently resizable (Phase 18), so the PANE's own available width, not the browser viewport, is what determines when `AssetOverviewTab.module.css`'s `.layout` grid (`2fr 1fr`) gets cramped. `container-type: inline-size` + `@container (max-width: 860px) { grid-template-columns: 1fr; }`.
  **A real, non-obvious CSS bug found and fixed before landing**: the first attempt put `container-type` directly on `.layout` and the `@container` query targeting `.layout` itself — confirmed LIVE that this never matched, even with a trivially-always-true `@container (max-width: 9999px)` sanity check injected directly into the page (still didn't apply, ruling out a threshold-math error). Rather than trust spec theory, treated the observed behavior as authoritative: split into a new outer `.layoutContainer` div (owns `container-type: inline-size`) wrapping the unchanged `.layout` (still owns the grid + the `@container` override) — querying element and containing element must be different in this app's actual rendering, confirmed by re-testing after the split.
  **860px threshold, flagged as a raw-value exception, not a token**: no breakpoint primitive exists in `tokens.base.css` yet, and CSS container queries don't accept `var()` in their condition (custom properties aren't valid container-query lengths per spec) — same posture as `glow.module.css`'s own 2px rail width. Value derived from the grid's own math: at `2fr 1fr` + 16px gap, the 1fr narrow column (Analyst consensus, Earnings, Why is this moving, Peers) drops under ~280px — too cramped for its own chart/table content — once the container falls below ~860px.
  **Verification, measured not eyeballed**: two scratch Storybook stories (`Wide` 1400px, `Narrow` 700px container) rendering the real `EntityDetailPage` via `usePageStore.openEntityDetail('south-bow-corp')`, same real-composition methodology the immediately prior round established. Confirmed via `getComputedStyle().gridTemplateColumns`: `868px 434px` (2 columns) at 1318px container width, `618px` (1 column) at 618px container width — both sides of the 860px threshold checked, not just one. Screenshot of the narrow state confirms a clean single-column stack, `TrendChart`'s own bleed still flush inside it.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no token added — the 860px value is CSS-only, can't be tokenized per the container-query var() restriction above). `test:visual`: no committed baseline exercises `AssetOverviewTab`'s real composition (confirmed via grep, same finding as the immediately prior round) — no regeneration required.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a layout-responsiveness fix scoped to one page's own grid, not new architecture. First use of CSS container queries in this codebase — worth noting for the next responsive-layout task, including the self-reference gotcha above.

- **Phase 8B/8H workbench — auto-collapse's shared-boolean bug: two independent triggers, one rising edge (2026-08-18).** Architect reported the auto-collapse feature (built 2026-08-17, same phase entry above) as still broken in a specific sequence: navigate to a page pane (Portfolio/Entity Detail) — nav correctly auto-collapses — then manually re-expand it, then open the artifacts pane from THAT SAME page — nav did NOT re-collapse.
  **Root cause, confirmed live before touching code**: `Sidebar.tsx`'s original `shouldAutoCollapse = page !== 'home' || (isStackOpen && hasArtifacts)` OR'd both triggers into one combined boolean, tracked by a SINGLE `prevAutoCollapse` ref. Navigating to a page pane already drove the combined flag to `true`; when the artifact trigger fired later (independently), the combined flag was already `true` — "true and previously false" (the rising-edge condition) never re-evaluated for that second, distinct cause, so the effect silently never fired for it. A genuine "one shared edge-detector serving two independent event sources" bug, not a stale-value or missing-dependency issue.
  **A real methodology miss during the FIRST verification attempt this round, corrected before concluding**: the first live repro appeared to still fail (nav stayed expanded after "reopening" artifacts) — but the artifact stack was already open from a PRIOR test run's leftover state in the same browser session, so the "reopen" click was actually toggling it CLOSED, not open, invalidating that specific test. Re-ran with a clean sequence (submit a real query → confirm artifact opens + nav collapses → explicitly close the stack → navigate to a page pane → manually re-expand → reopen the now-confirmed-closed stack) before concluding the fix worked — the same "verify the tool did only what you intended" discipline as this project's own git-checkout incident, applied to test setup rather than a destructive command.
  **Fix**: split into two independent triggers, `isPagePane` and `isArtifactPane`, each with its own `useRef`-tracked previous value; the effect fires `setIsCollapsed(true)` if EITHER trigger shows its own rising edge, independently of the other's current state.
  **Verification, measured not eyeballed, via `shell-frame--default`'s real store-backed story (not a mimic)**: nav 260px→48px on Portfolio navigation; manual re-expand confirmed back to 260px; reopening the artifact stack (freshly confirmed closed first) collapsed it to 48px again — the exact previously-broken sequence, now passing.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no CSS/token change — pure trigger-logic fix). `test:visual`: no committed baseline exercises `Sidebar`'s collapse states (confirmed via grep of `shell.spec.ts`) — no regeneration required.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a bugfix to the trigger logic of an already-documented auto-collapse behavior (2026-08-17 entry above), not new architecture.

- **Frame layout — nav↔content gap widened to match the existing content↔artifact gap (2026-08-18).** Architect: "give larger margin right for the nav, the same as between Portfolio and/or page, whether it's Discover or EntityDetail and the artifact." A request to match a gap that already existed (`.contentColumn`'s own 24px content↔artifact margin, 2026-08-17), not to invent a new value.
  **Measured the actual starting state before picking a fix**: live geometry check found `navToContentGap` was exactly `0px` on Portfolio (nav flush against page content, no separation at all) vs. `contentToArtifactGap` already at the target `24px` — confirming this as a real, previously-unaddressed gap, not a rounding/off-by-a-few-px issue.
  **Fix, in `Frame.module.css`'s `.row`**: `margin-left: var(--space-24)` added directly (full 24px, not a delta on top of an existing baseline like `.contentColumn`'s own calc()) — the nav side had NO existing gap to build on top of (0px, not `--space-16` like the row's own internal flex gap), so the delta math `.contentColumn` uses doesn't apply here; the full target value is added directly instead. Unconditional (not gated on any page/artifact flag) — the ask named "Portfolio and/or page, whether it's Discover or EntityDetail, and the artifact" as one unified rule covering every state this row can be in, not one specific pane combination.
  **Verification, measured across every named state**: `getBoundingClientRect()` on nav vs. content confirmed 24px on Home (nav expanded, 260px), Portfolio (nav auto-collapsed, 48px), Discover, and with the artifact pane open (24px on BOTH sides simultaneously — nav↔content AND content↔artifact) — the exact set of states named in the request, not just one sampled case.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no new token — reused `--space-24`). `test:visual`: `shell.spec.ts` DOES cover this (full-page screenshots of `shell-frame--default`, nav included) — unlike the two prior rounds, this one required regeneration. Full delete-and-regenerate — 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs. `shell-base-query-default` baseline spot-checked by eye confirming the visibly wider, even nav↔content gap.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a shell-layout spacing fix reusing an already-established token and gap-sizing precedent, not new architecture.

- **Phase 21 — fifteenth post-gate follow-up: --tint-subtle swept to every remaining gradient/glow app-wide (2026-08-18).** Architect: "all gradients that we use in key issues, bullish, bearish, and anywhere, basically, notable price movements should be subtle (we added that scale)." Confirmed via AskUserQuestion this supersedes the twelfth round's own deliberate "Entity Detail only, every other time-series usage keeps 0.35" scoping — genuinely app-wide this time, not a re-litigation of that decision without the architect's own sign-off.
  **Full sweep, found by grepping every `glow.module.css` consumer plus every raw `stopOpacity`/`fillOpacity`/`opacity` in chart-adjacent files, not assumed from memory of what was fixed in the twelfth round**: `KeyIssuesCard.tsx` (both bullish/bearish glows — cited BY NAME as still on `--tint-strong` when the token was first introduced, now fixed), `PriceMovementTimeline.tsx` ("Notable Price Movement," the other component named directly), `EarningsHistoryChart.tsx`'s glow, `TimeSeries.tsx`'s own generic `glow` prop wrapper (separate from its gradient stop, which the twelfth round already fixed for `bleed` only), `TimeSeries.tsx`'s non-bleed gradient stop AND its combo-chart `fillOpacity={0.35}` (both previously left on the raw literal on purpose — now `var(--tint-subtle)`, and the now-redundant `bleed` conditional on the gradient stop was removed since both branches converged), `Sparkline.tsx`'s own gradient stop (a sibling `stopOpacity={0.35}` instance that existed alongside `TimeSeries.tsx`'s original one but was never touched in the earlier round), and `StoriesAndAnalysisCard.module.css`'s placeholder-block `opacity: var(--opacity-40)` — a raw PRIMITIVE reference bypassing the semantic scale entirely, same "how strongly does a color wash over an area" job the token exists for, fixed to `var(--tint-subtle)`.
  **Confirmed NOT in scope, checked directly rather than assumed**: `AnalystConsensus.tsx`'s bearish/bullish segments use solid `--delta-up-bg`/`--delta-down-bg` fills (already-toned-down background tokens, no gradient, no `glow.module.css`) — a different, already-correct mechanism, not touched.
  **`fillOpacity` accepts a token string directly** (unlike `stopOpacity`, which recharts/JSX types as a number-only prop, requiring the `style={{ stopOpacity: ... }}` workaround already established) — confirmed via `tsc`, not assumed.
  **Verification, measured via regenerated baselines, not just code review**: `nodes.spec.ts` regenerated in full (166/166) since six covered stories/story-embeds (`TrendChart`, `KeyIssuesCard`, `PriceMovementTimeline`, `EarningsHistoryChart`, `TimeSeries` happy+combo, `Metric`'s own embedded `Sparkline`) all touch the changed value — `DataTable`'s Happy story checked directly and confirmed to carry no array/sparkline column data, so genuinely unaffected, not just assumed safe. `nodes-keyissuescard--happy-default` spot-checked by eye: both bullish/bearish glows visibly subtler, labels/badges still fully legible.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no new token — every fix reuses the existing `--tint-subtle` from the twelfth round). `test:visual`: full delete-and-regenerate on `nodes.spec.ts` (166/166), then a full-suite delete-and-regenerate confirmation — 235/235 across two consecutive clean runs.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a scope-expansion of an already-ratified token to its remaining unconverted consumers, not new architecture.

- **Phase 21 — sixteenth post-gate follow-up: price+chart block back inside a Panel — visual consistency, not a bleed-math regression (2026-08-18).** Architect: "the chart should have pane." Confirmed via AskUserQuestion which chart (Entity Detail's price/TrendChart block, the one whose Panel wrapper the thirteenth round removed).
  **Confirmed live before touching anything that this was a genuinely different problem than the thirteenth round's fix, not a request to re-break it**: screenshot of the current page showed Key statistics/Analyst consensus/Earnings/Why is this moving all in bordered Panel/PanelWithAction cards — price+chart was the one block floating with zero visual boundary. The thirteenth round's Panel removal was about a bleed-math BUG (the chart didn't actually reach the pane's edge inside that Panel); this round's ask is pure visual consistency (every sibling block already has a card, this one doesn't) — a different root cause, so reintroducing Panel here doesn't re-litigate that earlier fix.
  **Fix**: `AssetOverviewTab.tsx` — `AssetPriceHeader`+`TrendChart` wrapped in `Panel` again. `TrendChart.module.css`'s `.chartBleed` was NOT touched — its `margin-inline: calc(-1 * var(--space-16))` already hardcodes the space token directly rather than reading a Card-specific CSS variable (the thirteenth round's own fix), and `Panel`'s `Card padding={4}` uses that same 16px value `.paneBody` does, so the existing bleed math holds correctly against EITHER ancestor without modification — verified live rather than assumed from the shared value.
  **Verification, measured then screenshotted**: `getBoundingClientRect()` on the chart's SVG vs. its now-immediate `.astryx-card` ancestor — 1px gap on all three edges (left/right/bottom), the border stroke itself, same "genuinely flush" result as every prior bleed verification this phase. Screenshot of the real `EntityDetailPage` (via the same `usePageStore.openEntityDetail` scratch-story methodology used every round since the thirteenth) confirms the pane border now exists AND the chart still bleeds correctly inside it — visually matching every other card on the page.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no token/CSS value change — pure JSX wrapper re-add). `test:visual`: confirmed via grep, same finding as every round since the thirteenth — no committed baseline exercises `AssetOverviewTab`'s real composition, so no regeneration required.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a visual-consistency fix restoring a wrapper removed for an unrelated reason two rounds ago, not new architecture.

- **Discover page — three top strips responsive reflow; DiscoverAssetsSection's table min-width investigated and confirmed already correct (2026-08-18).** Architect: "discover page should be responsive, 3 panes notable movers/trending/newly added should stack, first one into new row, then all 3 stacked" + "discover assets should continue resizing there shouldn't be min width for the pane."
  **`EntityDiscoveryStrips`**: was a flat inline `display:grid; gridTemplateColumns: repeat(3,1fr)`, no responsive behavior at all. New `EntityDiscoveryStrips.module.css` — `container-type: inline-size` on a SEPARATE outer `.container` wrapping `.grid` (not on `.grid` itself — reapplying the exact self-reference finding from this same day's `AssetOverviewTab` work: a container query targeting the element that establishes `container-type` doesn't match in this app's actual rendering, confirmed live then, not re-tested from scratch this round since the mechanism is identical). Two tiers, exact ordering requested (not a generic auto-fit reflow, which would produce an unordered split): ≤640px → 2 columns with `.grid > :first-child { grid-column: 1 / -1 }` (Notable movers alone in row 1, Trending+Newly Added share row 2); ≤420px → full 1-column stack, spanning reset to `auto`. 640px derived from the strips' own row content (logo+name+detail+sparkline+value) getting visibly cramped in three even columns before that width, confirmed live; 420px reused directly from `paneFloors.ts`'s own `PANE_FLOOR` rather than a fourth invented number, since that's already this app's own understood "maximally cramped" threshold.
  **`DiscoverAssetsSection`/`EntityAssetTable`**: investigated live (forced container widths via direct DOM resize in a scratch story, not assumed) — Astryx's own `Table` component ALREADY computes an aggregate `tableMinWidth` from its columns' individual `minWidth`s (`DEFAULT_MIN_COLUMN_WIDTH: 120` applied to any column without an explicit one, confirmed by reading `@astryxdesign/core`'s own `columnUtils.d.ts`) and applies it as the rendered `<table>` element's own `min-width` — separate from the existing outer wrapper's `minWidth: 0; overflow: auto`. Live-measured: wrapper shrunk to 700px, `<table>` correctly held at its own computed `min-width: 900px`, wrapper's `scrollWidth > clientWidth` confirmed true (genuinely scrollable, not silently clipped or crushed). This is already exactly the "keep resizing the pane, real floor on the table itself, no artificial page-level min-width" behavior requested — no code change was needed here; `EntityAssetGrid` (the alternate card-grid view) checked too and found to have no equivalent column-crushing risk at all (Astryx `Grid`/`GridSpan` cards wrap by their own content, not fixed columns).
  **Verification, measured via direct DOM resize in a scratch story (not the Storybook viewport, which doesn't reliably force sub-story-default widths) — same forced-resize technique as this same day's earlier container-query verification**: strips grid confirmed 3-col at 734px (above threshold), 2-col with first-child full-span at 600px, full 1-col stack at 380px — all three tiers hit exactly as specified. Table's own min-width/scroll behavior confirmed via `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth` comparison, not just a visual glance.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no new token — 640px/420px are container-query raw values, same documented exception as `AssetOverviewTab`'s own 860px earlier this day; `--space-16` gap reused, nothing new). `test:visual`: confirmed via grep — no committed baseline exercises `EntitiesPage`/`EntityDiscoveryStrips`'s real composition, no regeneration required.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a responsive-layout addition to one page's own top strips, plus confirmation (not a fix) that an already-correct table behavior needed no change.

- **Discover follow-up: three real bugs found and fixed — a genuine PageShell-wide CSS Grid sizing bug, a scrollable category filter (new), and the view toggle moved into the section title row (2026-08-18).** Architect reported the just-shipped strip reflow wasn't visible live, plus two new asks: "make tabs responsive (scrollable)... if astryx not have scrollable tabs then let's try scrollOverflow" and "grid list view should be in same line/row as title... similarly like artifacts pane has options there [close and expand]."
  **Root cause of "can't see reflow" — NOT a bug in the reflow CSS itself, a pre-existing bug one level up the ancestor chain, found by live measurement rather than re-guessing the threshold**: `EntityDiscoveryStrips`' own new `.container` measured WIDER than its direct parent `PageShell.module.css`'s `.paneBody` (734px vs. 418px) — a child genuinely exceeding its parent's box. Traced to `.paneBody` itself: `display: grid` with NO `grid-template-columns` declared, so its one implicit auto column sized to the MAX-CONTENT width of its widest child (CSS Grid's default `min-width: auto` on items — the Grid equivalent of flexbox's well-documented `min-width: 0` trap, and this app already has a written precedent for the flexbox version on `.contentColumn`, just never hit the Grid version until now). Every `PageShell`-based page in the app was silently handing its content a phantom width wider than the pane could ever be, which is also why no `@container` breakpoint had ever fired correctly on THIS page before today, independent of any threshold value chosen. Fixed with `grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr)` on `.paneBody` — forces the track to genuinely respect its container's real available space; a wide child (Discover Assets' own table, `min-width: 900px`) now correctly overflows into ITS OWN horizontal scroll instead of inflating the shared track for every sibling section.
  **Blast radius taken seriously, not glossed over**: this is a shell-wide fix (every page under `PageShell` — Discover, Watchlist, Portfolio, Entity Detail, Data Sources, Holdings). No visual baseline anywhere in `tests/visual/*.spec.ts` actually exercises `PageShell` at all (confirmed via grep — `shell.spec.ts`'s own two-utterance sequence stays on Home/investigation surfaces throughout, never navigates to a `PageShell` page), a real pre-existing coverage gap, not something this round introduced. Live-spot-checked two DIFFERENT `PageShell` pages beyond Discover (`EntityDetailPage` via the real `usePageStore.openEntityDetail` flow, `WatchlistPage`) to confirm no regression before trusting the fix — both rendered correctly, full width, no shrinkage/overflow artifacts. Full suite re-run as a sanity check anyway (235/235, zero change since nothing committed exercises the changed code path).
  **Scrollable category filter — Astryx's own `useScrollOverflow` hook, not hand-rolled**: confirmed via reading its installed `.d.ts` directly (`overflowStart`/`overflowEnd`/`hasOverflow` + a `scrollRef`) rather than trusting the (JS-rendered, unfetchable) docs site — its own source comment states it's "used by Carousel for fade-edge and button state," exactly this job. `OverflowList` (also installed) was considered and rejected: it COLLAPSES items into a "+N more" dropdown, changing what's reachable without scrolling, not the scroll-with-fade-edge pattern asked for. New `DiscoverAssetsSection.module.css`: `.wrapper` (non-scrolling, hosts the fade overlays) wraps `.scrollRow` (the actual `overflow-x: auto` element) as SEPARATE elements — a second real bug found and fixed before landing: the first attempt put the fade overlays as `position: absolute` children of the SCROLLING element itself, so they scrolled away WITH the content instead of staying pinned to the visible edge (measured live: `.fadeStart`'s own `left` position went to -142px, fully off-screen, once scrolled) — same "one element can't serve two conflicting layout roles" lesson as the container-query self-reference bug earlier this same day, recognized and applied without re-deriving it from scratch.
