A THIRD bug on top of that: `.wrapper` itself needed `min-width: 0` — without it, the wrapper refused to shrink below the ToggleButtonGroup's own full unscrolled content width, defeating `.scrollRow`'s `overflow-x: auto` entirely (confirmed live: `clientWidth` stuck equal to `scrollWidth` until this was added). Fade mechanic reuses `PaneTitleBar.module.css`'s own vertical-fade pattern (opaque through content, transparent over the last ~16px) rotated to the horizontal axis, `--surface-1` matching `PageSection`'s own Card background so it blends into its actual pane.
  **View toggle in the title row — a real `PageSection` API gap, not a hack**: `PageSection`'s existing `source` prop is rendered wrapped in `<Text type="supporting">`, correct for its every current caller (a plain citation string) but wrong for an interactive `ToggleButtonGroup` — wrapping a real component in `Text` would have been a type/semantic mismatch. Added a new, separate `actions` prop (raw `ReactNode`, unwrapped) rather than repurposing `source` — additive, every existing `PageSection` caller (Watchlist, Data Sources, the three Discover strips) unaffected. `DiscoverAssetsSection` now passes its view-mode toggle via `actions`, landing in the same title row as "Discover Assets," matching `ArtifactStack`'s own close/expand button placement precedent named directly in the request.
  **Verification, every claim measured live, not eyeballed**: `.paneBody`'s own resolved `grid-template-columns` before/after (734px singular track → correctly tracking real width, e.g. 386px/446px depending on artifact-pane state); strip reflow re-confirmed with the SAME real page + real artifact-open flow that first surfaced the bug (`usePageStore.openEntityDetail`-driven, not a synthetic mimic) — first strip full-span, other two side-by-side below it, screenshot matching the originally-requested layout exactly; category scroller's `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth`/`overflowStart`/`overflowEnd` all confirmed responding correctly to a real `scrollBy()` call, fade position confirmed on-screen (not off-screen) post-fix; title-row placement confirmed via screenshot showing the grid/table icons beside "Discover Assets," matching the artifact pane's own icon-row convention.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no new token — `minmax(0, 1fr)` and `min-width: 0` are structural CSS, not values; `--surface-1` reused). `test:visual`: confirmed via grep that no baseline anywhere exercises any of the three changed surfaces (`PageShell`, `PageSection`, `DiscoverAssetsSection`/`EntityDiscoveryStrips`) — full suite re-run as an extra sanity check given the `PageShell` change's blast radius, 235/235, zero baseline drift.
  No new legislation. `node-vocabulary.md` NOT amended — this round fixed layout/responsiveness bugs and added one small, additive API surface (`PageSection`'s `actions` prop), not new registry architecture. Flagged for a future session: `tests/visual/*.spec.ts` has no coverage of `PageShell`-based pages at all (Discover, Watchlist, Portfolio, Entity Detail, Data Sources, Holdings) — every verification this round and in prior `PageShell`-touching rounds this phase relied on live scratch-story spot-checks rather than a committed regression gate; worth a real work order to close that gap rather than continuing to re-verify by hand each time.

- **Discover strips: reflow grid REPLACED with Astryx Carousel (2026-08-18, same day as the reflow work above).** Architect: "does astryx have carousel? so we could use it for these 3 panes?" — confirmed real (installed, `@astryxdesign/core/Carousel`), and confirmed via AskUserQuestion this is a genuine swap, not an addition: Carousel is horizontal-scroll-only (one row at every width, panned via its own built-in edge-fade + prev/next buttons), fundamentally different from the container-query reflow (3-col → 2-col+1 → 1-col stack) built two rounds earlier THIS SAME DAY — the two behaviors are mutually exclusive, so the reflow work is superseded, not layered.
  **Before recommending, verified Carousel is actually the right tool, not just an available one**: read its own `.d.ts`/`.js` source directly (children mapped into a scroll row, `useScrollOverflow` — the SAME hook already reused for the category filter scroller two rounds ago — drives its edge-fade, `Layer`-rendered prev/next buttons escape any parent overflow clipping). `useScrollOverflow`'s own doc comment ("used by Carousel for fade-edge and button state") is what originally surfaced Carousel's existence as a related primitive, closing the loop from that earlier discovery.
  **Implementation**: `EntityDiscoveryStrips.tsx` — the `.container`/`.grid` reflow wrapper and its two `@container` breakpoints removed; the three `PageSection` strip cards now render as `Carousel`'s own children, each wrapped in a new `.stripItem` (fixed `width: 340px; flex-shrink: 0`) since Carousel's own scroll-row children have no width of their own and would otherwise collapse toward their content's min-content size. `EntityDiscoveryStrips.module.css` reduced to just this one rule — the reflow-era CSS (container-type, the two breakpoints, the first-child span override) deleted outright, not left dead.
  **Verification, live, both states**: wide viewport — all three strips visible in one row, no scroll buttons (nothing overflows, confirmed correct per Carousel's own conditional rendering). Narrowed to 800px — "Newly Added" correctly scrolled out of view, right-chevron button appeared and was enabled, clicking it advanced the scroll and correctly enabled the left-chevron in turn. Both screenshotted and the button's own `disabled` state read directly via DOM, not inferred from appearance.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no new token — 340px is a component-sizing constant, not a design value; same posture as other raw geometry constants this session, e.g. the rail width/card height precedents). `test:visual`: no committed baseline exercises this component (confirmed via grep, same finding as every `EntityDiscoveryStrips`-touching round today) — no regeneration required.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a component swap within an existing page section, not new architecture. Net effect for the day: the container-query reflow work (two rounds prior) is now dead code removed in the same session it shipped, a genuine but fully-intentional reversal driven by a direct, explicit architect choice between two working alternatives — logged here so it reads as a deliberate supersession, not silent churn.

- **Discover strips: hybrid grid+Carousel — grid stretches to full width by default, Carousel only once genuinely out of room (2026-08-18, same day, third revision of this same UI).** Architect, immediately after the Carousel-only swap: "should always stretch across these panes to full width of container, only when they reached their min width so cant shrink more then reflow into carousel" — a correction, not a further pivot: the grid's OWN "stretch to fill" behavior was wanted back, with Carousel demoted to an overflow escape hatch rather than the default.
  **Implementation**: both a CSS grid (`.grid`, `repeat(3, minmax(340px, 1fr))`) and a `Carousel` (children wrapped in fixed-340px `.stripItem`) render into the DOM simultaneously; a `@container` query at 1052px (the exact `3 × 340px + 2 × 16px gap` sum, not eyeballed) toggles which one is `display: none` vs. visible. `stripPanels` (the three `PageSection` cards) authored once as a local array and referenced by both branches' `.map()` calls so the two never drift content out of sync.
  **A real grid-track trap caught before landing, same class as two bugs found earlier this same day**: a first draft used a bare `repeat(3, 1fr)` plus `min-width: 340px` on each grid CHILD — confirmed this does NOT create a floor on the TRACK itself (a `1fr` track still shrinks past a child's own `min-width` unless the track's own definition carries a `minmax()` floor), the grid-track version of the exact "min-width:auto default" trap already hit on `.paneBody` and `DiscoverAssetsSection`'s `.wrapper` earlier today. Fixed by moving the floor onto the track definition itself: `minmax(340px, 1fr)`.
  **Verification, live, both sides of the exact threshold**: `getComputedStyle` confirmed `.grid` active (three equal ~460px columns, stretching to fill a 1414px container) above threshold, `.carouselWrap` active (chevron button present, correct fade) below it — transition measured precisely between container width 1058px (grid) and 1028px (Carousel), straddling the 1052px threshold as designed. Screenshotted both states. Checked for a double-render side effect from mounting both branches simultaneously (necessary so Carousel's own scroll state survives a threshold crossing without remounting): confirmed live 18 `.astryx-item` DOM nodes exist (3 strips × 3 rows × 2 branches) but the hidden branch never paints or visibly animates — a live screenshot at initial mount showed no doubling or flash, so the DOM-doubling tradeoff is real but has no user-visible cost.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no new token — `minmax(340px, 1fr)`/`1052px` are structural/derived CSS values, same raw-value-exception posture as this session's other container-query thresholds). `test:visual`: no committed baseline exercises this component (confirmed via grep, unchanged finding across every round touching this file today) — no regeneration required.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a further revision of the same page-section responsive mechanism, not new architecture. Three real UI states shipped for this one section across three consecutive rounds today (container-query grid-only → Carousel-only → grid+Carousel hybrid) — each a genuine, direct-feedback-driven correction, not drift; logged individually so the history reads as deliberate iteration.

- **Discover strips: corrected the grid's own minimum width (340px → 440px, real content clipping) + added Carousel snap (2026-08-18, same day, fourth revision).** Architect, with a screenshot showing "40.3"/"+0.07" instead of "40.33"/"+0.07%" in the Trending strip: "should not allow content to cut off, should snap also."
  **The 340px value from the immediately-prior round was verified WRONG, not just imprecise — a real methodology gap in that round's own check**: its overflow test compared each `.astryx-item`'s own `scrollWidth` vs `clientWidth`, which stayed equal at every width tested (confirmed re-run: still 0 overflow by that same metric at 1400px, despite the screenshot showing visible clipping). Root cause found by measuring the RIGHT comparison instead — item's own `getBoundingClientRect().right` vs. its parent Card's `right` — which showed `Item` genuinely extending ~14-86px past its own Card boundary at narrower widths, silently clipped by the Card's `overflow: hidden`, one level up from where the previous check was looking. Astryx's own `Item` component doesn't propagate `min-width: 0` through its internal startContent/label/endContent flex row (confirmed by reading its compiled output — only atomic StyleX classes, no stable selector to reach in from outside, consistent with this codebase's own rule against anchoring to unstable hashed classnames), so the real fix is a wider floor, not a CSS override into Item's internals.
  **Corrected value, measured not estimated**: binary-searched the real zero-clipping point via the item-vs-card-right comparison across all three strips' actual data (Trending's own "40.33"/"+0.07%" turned out to be the widest real content) — overflow hit exactly 0px at a 440px per-strip minimum (zero-crossing measured at ~427px, 440px keeps a small safety margin). `.grid`'s track changed from `minmax(340px, 1fr)` to `minmax(440px, 1fr)`, `.stripItem` from 340px to 440px, the `@container` threshold recomputed from `3×340+2×16=1052` to `3×440+2×16=1352`. Re-verified live at the new exact boundary (zero overflow at 1352px container width) and well above it (zero overflow at 1400px, grid mode confirmed active).
  **`hasSnap` added to `Carousel`** — Astryx's own documented prop (`hasSnap?: boolean`, "each direct child snaps to the start edge"), confirmed live via `getComputedStyle(scroller).scrollSnapType === 'x mandatory'` after enabling it, not just added and assumed working.
  **Verification, live, re-confirmed at three widths (1352px exact threshold, 1400px well above it, plus the original clipping repro width) via the CORRECTED item-vs-card measurement this time**: 0px worst-case overflow at every width checked; screenshot at 1600px shows every value fully legible ("40.33", "+0.07%", "28.29", "-0.03%", "55.07", "-0.01%", all previously clipped digits now complete).
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no new token — 440px/1352px are corrected structural values, same posture as their 340px/1052px predecessors). `test:visual`: no committed baseline exercises this component (unchanged finding, confirmed via grep again) — no regeneration required.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change. Process note for future rounds: an overflow check against an element's OWN `scrollWidth`/`clientWidth` only catches self-scrolling overflow — it misses content clipped by an ANCESTOR's `overflow: hidden`, which needs the element's own bounding rect compared against that ancestor's, not against itself. This gap produced a real, screenshot-confirmed bug that shipped and was caught by the architect, not by verification — worth remembering as a standing caveat the next time an overflow/clipping claim needs proving.

- **NEW PRIMITIVE — `theme/bleed.module.css`: the reusable "cancel my pane's padding" contract, replacing TrendChart's hardcoded one-off (2026-08-19).** Architect: the Entity Detail chart still wasn't reaching the pane edge "probably [due to] parent divs below," and asked for a fix that is "flexible reusable for other items that'd go full bleed — best practice for that approach that if pane has default padding what content does to do full bleed?" A design question, answered as a primitive rather than another local patch.
  **Root cause, measured live before designing anything (full ancestor chain probe, not inspection-by-reading)**: TWO independent blockers, only one of which any prior round had considered. (1) `.chartBleed`'s `margin-inline: calc(-1 * var(--space-16))` hardcoded a guess about its ancestor's padding — it happened to match `.paneBody`'s value, but had already silently canceled NOTHING in an earlier revision when the true ancestor was an Astryx `Card` (whose `--container-padding-inline-start` is Card-scoped and simply absent on a plain div). (2) The real reason it looked broken even when the margin was right: `glow.module.css`'s own `.clipped` (`overflow: hidden`) sat on an ancestor BETWEEN the chart and the pane edge and clipped the bleed straight back — measured exactly: the chart correctly reached 58→823 while `.clipped` cut it to 74→807, precisely the 16px per side that presented as "full bleed doesn't work." A negative margin is necessary but never sufficient; no prior round had checked the clipping half.
  **The primitive (`theme/bleed.module.css`), a two-sided contract**: (a) a PANE publishes its own inset as `--pane-inset` — `Panel.tsx` and `PageSection.tsx` on their Card content wrapper, `PageShell.module.css` on `.paneBody`, each adjacent to the padding it mirrors so the two can't drift; (b) a CHILD applies `.inline`/`.blockEnd`/`.blockStart`, which cancel `--pane-inset` generically and never name a spacing token themselves. A pane changing its own padding now carries every bleeding child along automatically. `var(--pane-inset, 0px)` fallback means a child rendered with no pane around it (e.g. `nodes-trendchart--happy`'s bare story) simply doesn't bleed rather than jumping by a wrong amount — confirmed live, that baseline passed unchanged. `.passthrough` is the documented opt-out for an intermediate wrapper that must not clip its bleeding descendants.
  **The clipping half, fixed structurally not by disabling containment**: `TrendChart` now bleeds its WHOLE root (bleed's `.inline`+`.blockEnd`) and re-insets only its header (`.headerInset`, `padding-inline: var(--pane-inset)`) — the inverse of the old `.chartBleed`. This makes the clipping box and the pane-edge box the SAME box, so nothing sits between the chart and the edge, and glow's `.clipped` containment survives intact rather than being traded away. Verified both halves simultaneously: chart SVG 58→823 against the Card's 57→824 border box (1px each side = the border itself), header content starting at exactly 74 (the Card's true content edge), and the glow root's bottom sitting 1px INSIDE the card bottom (`-1`, still contained, `overflow: hidden` still active).
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no new token — `--pane-inset` is a structural custom property, not a design value; every consumer still resolves to the existing `--space-16`). `test:visual`: full suite 235/235, zero drift — `nodes-trendchart--happy` deliberately unaffected (bare story, no pane, fallback path).
  Reusable beyond this consumer by construction, per the ask: any component that wants to reach its pane's edge now applies `bleed.module.css`'s classes and gets it, in a `Panel`, a `PageSection`, or a `PageShell` page body alike, with no knowledge of which one it's in. `glow.module.css` remains the model this follows (presentation-only className hook, composable variants, no registry-node or Panel-prop surface).

- **Entity Detail Overview: three layout moves — Notable price movement to the right pane, Peers paired with Stories & Analysis on the left, and a general "matched pair stretches to equal height" primitive (2026-08-19).** Architect: "Notable price moment should to side pane (right); Peers should move to left pane 2 column together with stories & analysis; general rule if layout panes in 2 cols both should have same height so smaller should stretch to taller."
  **Scope check before touching layout, via AskUserQuestion**: does "equal height" apply to the page's OUTER left/right split too? Confirmed no — that split was deliberately built independent-height on 2026-08-15 specifically because stacks of many cards with different total heights would otherwise leave a large empty gap under the shorter column (a documented, load-bearing decision this round could easily have silently reversed). Scoped the new rule to the new LOCAL pair only.
  **`AssetOverviewTab.tsx`**: `PriceMovementTimeline`'s `PanelWithAction` moved from the left column (was index 5) into the right column (now index 3, after "Why is this moving?"). `RelatedEntitiesStrip`'s `<Panel title="Peers">` moved out of its own standalone slot in the right column into a new `.pairRow` alongside `StoriesAndAnalysisCard` in the left column, sharing one `AnimatedListItem` (index 4) so both animate as a single staggered unit rather than two.
  **`AssetOverviewTab.module.css`'s new `.pairRow`**: a 2-column grid, `align-items: stretch` by Grid's own default (the outer `.layout` explicitly opts OUT of this via `align-items: start`, which is exactly why it needed a NEW class rather than reusing `.layout`). A real two-level gap found and closed: Grid's stretch only fills the DIRECT child's box — `.pairRow`'s direct children are `AnimatedListItem`'s own plain block divs, not the `Panel`/`Card` underneath them, so a block div doesn't automatically pass extra height down to its own child. Fixed with `.pairRow > * { display: grid; height: 100% }`, stretching the animated-item wrapper first, then letting grid's stretch cascade to the `Card` inside it — the same per-level mechanic applied twice, not a single rule.
  **Verification, measured then screenshotted**: `getBoundingClientRect()` on all three moved/paired cards confirmed simultaneously — "Stories & Analysis" and "Peers" share identical `top` and IDENTICAL height (314px each, in a live fixture where Peers legitimately has less content: one related entity vs. Stories & Analysis's three headlines), and "Notable price movement" now sits at the right column's own x-position, below "Why is this moving?". A full-viewport screenshot (story wrapper heightened, no scroll needed) shows the shorter Peers card visibly stretched to match its taller neighbor with no dangling gap.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no new token — the pair uses existing `--space-16` gap only). `test:visual`: confirmed via grep, unchanged finding — no committed baseline exercises `AssetOverviewTab`'s real composition. Full suite run anyway as a sanity check given this touched `AnimatedListItem`/`Panel` reuse patterns shared broadly — 235/235, zero drift.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a layout rearrangement plus one small, locally-scoped CSS addition, not new architecture.

- **Glow moved from content components to their card wrappers — a general "cards should easily have the glow system" capability, not a one-off fix (2026-08-19).** Architect: "notable price movements the bottom gradient should be applied to pane (card) rather than component of timeline itself, cards should easily also have gradient glow system as we have it defined."
  **Root problem, same shape as the bleed fix earlier this same day**: `PriceMovementTimeline.tsx` and `EarningsHistoryChart.tsx` each carried their own `glow` prop, wrapping only their OWN inner content in `glow.module.css`'s classes — nested INSIDE `PanelWithAction`'s `Card`. The glow sat against the content's own padded edge, not the card's true edge, reading as "the glow isn't reaching the pane," the same visual symptom the bleed bug produced for a different root cause.
  **Fix, moved the capability up to the card primitives themselves**: `Panel.tsx` (registry node) and `PanelWithAction.tsx` (shell-level) both gained `glow`/`glowColor` props, applying `glow.module.css`'s classes directly to their own `Card`. Kept OUTSIDE `PanelPropsSchema` (the Zod registry contract) — same posture as `children`, which was already outside it: neither is scene-JSON serializable. `PriceMovementTimeline`'s and `EarningsHistoryChart`'s own `glow` prop REMOVED entirely (component AND Zod schema) — confirmed via grep that neither is used by any scene fixture today (both are shell-only consumers via `AssetOverviewTab.tsx`), so no scene-compatibility risk. `time-series`'s own `glow` prop deliberately left untouched — it has zero live TradableAsset consumers today (`TrendChart` is a separate component with its own data-driven `--delta-up`/`--delta-down` glow, a legitimate exception since it isn't wrapped by `Panel`/`PanelWithAction` at all) — noted in node-vocabulary.md as a "revisit the same way if it ever gets a real consumer," not silently left inconsistent.
  **Verification, measured then screenshotted, at both real consumers**: `getComputedStyle` on the "Notable price movement" card confirmed `--glow-strength` resolves to `.20` (`--tint-subtle`) and `overflow: hidden` (`.clipped`) directly on the `.astryx-card` element itself, not a nested child. Screenshots of both cards (Notable price movement, Earnings) show the glow reaching the card's own true bottom edge, including behind the title/"See all" row — genuinely pane-level now, not content-scoped.

- **Discover strips: a real data bug (every crypto/equity delta silently 0, so Zenith Protocol could never rank as a mover) plus a real interaction bug (rows with no `intent` weren't clickable at all, so South Bow Corp had no path to its own page) — both fixed (2026-08-19).** Architect: "ensure zenith is notable movers pane and that south bow links to page, and all should link to details page not to trigger investigation."
  **Root cause, found by reading `assetDiscovery.ts`'s own `getMarketAssets()` before touching anything**: `deltaRecent` for EVERY entity was computed from `getYieldPoints()`, which only ever reads a `${id}-yield-volatility-90d` dataset — confirmed via direct inspection of `datasets.json` that only 3 of the 13 market entities (`nordbond-2029`, `aldergate-estates`, `helios-yield-fund` — the bond/real-estate/credit-fund set) actually have that key. Every other entity, including Zenith Protocol AND South Bow Corp, silently fell through to `deltaRecent: 0` — not a display bug, a genuine upstream data gap that made `rankMovers()`'s `Math.abs(delta)` sort meaningless for 10 of 13 entities (comparing mostly zeros, ties broken by array order, not by any real signal). Confirmed those 10 entities DO have a real series, just under a different key (`${id}-price-volume-90d`) that `getMarketAssets()` never looked at.
  **Fix**: `getMarketAssets()` now tries `getYieldPoints()` first (bond-style, `deltaRecent` as a raw pp difference, unchanged for the 3 entities that use it), and falls back to a new `getPricePoints()` reading `*-price-volume-90d` for everything else — `deltaRecent` computed as a genuine point-over-point PERCENT change there (matching the interface's own long-standing "24h price %" comment for crypto/stocks, distinct from the bond path's raw pp unit, both already read identically downstream by `EntityAssetTableCells.tsx`'s own delta cell). `vantara-metals` (commodities) has neither dataset and is confirmed to genuinely have no real series — stays honest `deltaRecent: 0`/no sparkline, this codebase's own established "never fabricate" rule, not a residual gap this fix should have papered over.
  **Verified against real numbers before trusting the fix, not just re-running the app and eyeballing it**: computed the actual top-3 by `|delta|` directly from `datasets.json` (Zenith Protocol +41.03%, Valiant Pharma -20.37%, Solent Stablecoin +1.81% — South Bow Corp's own +0.17% ranks 4th, genuinely not a top-3 mover with real data) BEFORE touching the app, so the live check below was confirming a known-correct answer, not discovering one. Live: "Notable movers" strip's own rows read exactly `Zenith Protocol...+41.03%`, `Valiant Pharma...-20.37%`, `Solent Stablecoin...+1.81%`, in that order — matching the hand-computed ranking precisely.
  **Second bug, `EntityDiscoveryStrips.tsx`'s own click wiring**: every row's `onClick` was gated on `row.intent` existing (`{...(intent ? { onClick: () => onInvestigate(intent) } : {})}`) — a row with no intent had no `onClick` prop at all, not even a fallback, which is the actual reason South Bow Corp (and any entity without an authored investigation intent) had no click path from these three strips. Fixed by replacing the whole `onInvestigate(intent)` path with an unconditional `onOpenDetail(row.id)` — every row browses to its own entity-detail page now, `DiscoveryRow.intent` removed from the type entirely since nothing reads it post-fix (confirmed via grep before removing, and confirmed `EntitiesPage.tsx`'s separate "Other tracked entities" list — a genuinely different component with its own explicit Investigate button — is untouched and still correct as its own distinct pattern). `EntitiesPage.tsx`'s now-dead `intentById` map (built but never read once the three row-builders stopped spreading `intent` into their rows) removed too, per CLAUDE.md's own no-dead-code rule.
  **Verified live, not assumed from the diff**: clicked Zenith Protocol's row — landed on `#entity-detail` showing Zenith's own price/chart, confirmed `hasTrail: false` (no investigation trail mounted, a real DOM check, not an inference from the URL alone). Clicked South Bow Corp's row (appearing twice across strips, both instances now share the same fixed component) — same result, its own detail page, its own real price ($40.33).
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42 (no test coverage existed for `assetDiscovery.ts` before or after — confirmed via find, not a regression this round introduced), `lint:tokens` clean (no token change — pure data/logic fix). `test:visual`: confirmed via grep no baseline exercises `EntitiesPage`/`EntityDiscoveryStrips`. Full suite hit one `shell.spec.ts` flake on the first run (a Home-flow test, nowhere near anything this round touched) — re-ran in isolation and it passed cleanly, matching this session's own established contention-flake pattern; two full subsequent runs both passed 235/235.
  No new legislation, no node-vocabulary.md change — a data-source bugfix plus an interaction-law correction (every strip row browses, per the page's own long-standing "browse first" law already governing the grid/table below it), not new architecture.

Also should have config for no border. And actually in related use item comp that is used in notable movers — each item has also sparkline and price and change... if not enough room (width) then sparkline should be hidden as responsive behaviour."

Removed the internal label entirely; confirmed live via DOM query that "Related" no longer appears anywhere inside the card.

Peers is the first real consumer (`<Panel title="Peers" bordered={false}>`).
  **Shared row extraction, `DiscoveryItemRow.tsx` (new)**: the `Item`-based row (AssetLogo + name/detail + sparkline/value/delta) that Notable Movers/Trending/Newly Added already used was inline inside `EntityDiscoveryStrips.tsx`'s own `DiscoveryStripList`, not its own component — extracted out so `RelatedEntitiesStrip.tsx` (Peers) could become its second real consumer, per the explicit ask. `EntityDiscoveryStrips.tsx` now imports and uses it instead of authoring the `Item` inline; `DiscoveryRow` (the shared row-data type) moved into the new file and re-exported from the old one so nothing else needed updating.

An entity outside `MARKET_ENTITY_IDS` (South Bow Corp's own related "Halberg Materials AG," confirmed not in that list) still renders correctly with an honest "—" instead of a fabricated value or a dropped row — this codebase's own "never fabricate" principle, not a new rule invented for this round.

The `bordered` prop itself (Panel.tsx/PageSection.tsx, `panelFlatNoBorder` across all 5 theme files) stays in place as a real, general capability — this round only reverts its one application, not the capability. Verified live: card's own computed `border` reads `1px solid` again, class list back to `panelFlat`.

- **Chat pane text reveal redesigned — chunked blur/opacity/stagger reveal replacing the single clip-path wipe, no animation library (2026-08-21).** Architect supplied a GSAP-based reference (chunk text into 2-4 word groups, blur+opacity+translateY fade per chunk, randomized-feeling stagger) and asked for "something modular reusable, simple and effective... no breaking into words." CLAUDE.md's dependency list is closed and explicitly bans any animation library ("motion via CSS transitions/keyframes driven by motion tokens and mount order") — GSAP was never an option; reimplemented the reference's own visual language on this project's existing motion architecture instead.
  **Where "the chat pane... when AI agent is writing" actually lives**: confirmed by reading `Transcript.tsx` that the transcript's "agent activity" side renders `ThinkingTrail`/`StepRow` (reasoning-step labels), not a separate prose response — `StepRow.tsx`'s own active-label text is the only place text "prints" live today, via a clip-path wipe (`useTextReveal.ts` + `trail.css`'s `.merlin-text-reveal`, one whole-block mask reveal, not chunked). That's the real target.
  **New primitive, matching the codebase's own established stagger pattern exactly**: `chunkText.ts` (new) splits a string into 2-4 word groups, deterministic (same input → same boundaries every render, cycling chunk size 2→3→4 rather than randomizing, so a re-render mid-animation can't reshuffle visible chunks). `TextChunkReveal.tsx` (new) renders each chunk as its own `<span>` with an inline `--chunk-index` custom property — the SAME mechanism `AnimatedListItem.tsx`/`.module.css` already establishes for its own entrance stagger (`--item-index` driving `calc()` inside `animation-delay`), reused rather than reinvented. `trail.css` gained `.merlin-chunk`/`@keyframes merlin-chunk-in` (opacity 0→1, `blur(var(--blur-8))`→0, `translateY(var(--space-4))`→0, `--duration-320`/`--ease-decel`) — every value maps to an EXISTING token (confirmed before use, not assumed): the reference's own 8px blur and 4px translateY both happened to already match `--blur-8`/`--space-4` exactly.
  **Per-chunk stagger interval is DERIVED, not a new token**: `durationMs / chunk-count`, clamped 60–160ms — keeps total reveal time pinned to the step's own real `ThinkingStep.durationMs` (the SAME "reuse real fixture pacing, never invent a duration" rule the prior clip-path wipe already followed, carried forward rather than dropped), regardless of how many chunks a given label happens to produce.
  **Dead code removed in the same change, not left behind**: `useTextReveal.ts` and `trail.css`'s own `@property --reveal`/`.merlin-text-reveal` rules were StepRow's only real consumer — confirmed via grep before deleting both outright (CLAUDE.md's no-dead-code rule), plus two dangling comment-only references in `LandingState.tsx` that cited the deleted hook as a design precedent, corrected to stand on their own.
  **Verified live, not assumed from the diff**: DOM inspection of a real `StepRow` confirmed 5 chunks from an 18-word label, each preserving whitespace correctly on reconstruction, with staggered `animation-delay` values (`0s, 0.16s, 0.32s, 0.48s, 0.64s` — 160ms apart, the derived-stagger cap kicking in for this label's chunk count) and settling to full opacity/zero blur after the animation completes (no stuck partial-reveal state). `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` confirmed showing all chunks at full opacity immediately. A mid-animation screenshot shows the intended visual exactly: earlier chunks fully settled, the active chunk mid-blur-fade, later chunks still hidden.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no new token — every motion/blur/spacing value reuses an existing primitive). `test:visual`: no baseline exists for `StepRow`/`ThinkingTrail` directly; `shell.spec.ts` (the one spec that renders the trail) waits for full trail completion before screenshotting, so its captures are always the settled state, unaffected by the active-chunk animation — confirmed by running it directly (5/5, zero drift) rather than assumed. Full suite run twice — 235/235 both times.
  No new legislation, node-vocabulary.md unchanged (the reveal mechanism itself was never named there — checked via grep before deciding no amendment was needed). A modular, reusable primitive per the order's own framing: `TextChunkReveal`/`chunkText` take plain `text`/`durationMs` props, no StepRow-specific coupling, available to any future consumer that wants the same chunked reveal.

- **Text chunk reveal: stagger tightened (2026-08-21, same-day follow-up).** Architect: "less stagger between the reveals." `TextChunkReveal.tsx`'s clamp halved (60–160ms → 30–80ms) — same derived-from-durationMs shape, just a tighter cascade. Verified live: a 5-chunk label's delays went from `0/160/320/480/640ms` to `0/80/160/240/320ms`. Gate: `tsc`/`vitest`/`lint:tokens` clean, no visual baseline affected (same finding as the prior round — `shell.spec.ts` only captures the trail's settled state).

- **Text chunk reveal: random chunk sizes, skewed small (2026-08-21, same-day follow-up).** Architect: "vary the number of words randomly and in short... keep them close to word-for-word, and sometimes up to three words max." Reverses the deterministic 2→3→4 cycle from earlier this same day — genuine randomness this time, not a fixed pattern.
  **`chunkText.ts` rewritten**: chunk size now drawn from a weighted roll (50% chance of 1 word, 35% chance of 2, 15% chance of 3) instead of cycling fixed sizes. Randomness is SEEDED off the text string itself (a small hand-rolled hash + linear congruential generator — no new dependency, same posture as `assetDiscoveryMock.ts`'s own seeded-sine generator elsewhere in this app), not `Math.random()` — the order's own "keep them" plus StepRow's real re-render risk (isActive can flip mid-animation) meant chunk boundaries needed to stay STABLE per label even though the generation itself is genuinely random-looking, not a fixed cycle. Confirmed live via a full page reload: the same label produces byte-identical chunk boundaries every time (`STABLE: true`).
  **Distribution verified against 5 different real sentences (37 total chunks) before trusting the weighting, not assumed from the roll thresholds alone**: 1-word chunks 19/37 (~51%), 2-word 15/37 (~41%), 3-word 3/37 (~8%) — matches "close to word-for-word, sometimes up to three" as an actual measured outcome, not just a probability on paper.
  **Gate**: `tsc`/`vitest`/`lint:tokens` clean. `test:visual`: no baseline affected (same finding both prior rounds touching this file — `shell.spec.ts` only captures the trail's settled state, never the active-chunk animation).

- **Phase 20 WO-1 — Mobile shell chrome: hamburger drawer nav + sticky logo bar, overriding Phase 18's closed "<768px permanently out of scope" decision (2026-08-21).** Direct order: "we need to make the platform fully responsive, including mobile. I know we have an order that prevents it, so this order now overrides it." A real law reversal, not a code tweak — CLAUDE.md amended FIRST (new Phase 20 entry, citing and superseding Phase 18 item 1's own closed decision, following this document's own established "mark superseded, don't silently rewrite" practice, same as Phase 17's own note), before any component code, per the repo's own conflict-resolution order (this file → tokens-spec.md → STATE.md → ask). Sequencing confirmed via AskUserQuestion: shell chrome (this round) first since everything else mounts inside it; New Investigation page responsiveness and chat/artifact mobile modals deferred to follow-up rounds, not attempted here.
  **Astryx-first, checked before building anything custom**: found `MobileNav`/`MobileNavToggle`/`TopNav`/`NavIcon`/`NavMenu` already installed and unused in this codebase — read every one of their `.d.ts` files and `AppShell.js`'s own source before writing code. Confirmed `AppShell` already auto-generates a mobile drawer from whatever `sideNav` content it's given, via its own internal `useMediaQuery`-driven breakpoint (`md` = 768px, matching the value this app's own removed `DESKTOP_MIN_WIDTH` already used) — `Sidebar.tsx`'s existing `SideNavSection`/`SideNavItem` tree is reused VERBATIM as the drawer's own content through `AppShell`'s own context wiring (`SideNavRenderContext`), no second nav tree authored, no duplication.
  **`Frame.tsx`**: the hard `viewportWidth < 768` early-return (`<DesktopOnlyNotice/>`) removed entirely — `DesktopOnlyNotice.tsx` and `useViewportWidth.ts` deleted outright as genuinely dead code (confirmed via grep, zero other consumers, per CLAUDE.md's no-dead-code rule). A `TopNav` (`heading={<BrandMark isCollapsed />}` — the SAME collapsed-state logo mark `Sidebar.tsx` already uses, not a second asset) is now passed as `AppShell`'s own `topNav` slot — but ONLY when `useMediaQuery('(max-width: 768px)')` is true, not unconditionally.
  **A real desktop regression caught live before shipping, not assumed safe**: the first attempt passed `topNav` unconditionally, assuming (wrongly) that `AppShell` only renders it in "mobile-bar mode" below breakpoint the same way it suppresses the desktop sideNav rail. Screenshotted the result at 1280px and found a genuine phantom "M" bar sitting above the existing sidebar's own "Merlin" heading — confirmed by re-reading `AppShell.js` line-by-line that `topNavContent` renders `topNav` at EVERY width, only switching TopNav's own internal render mode below breakpoint; there is no equivalent to `showSideNavInline`'s suppression for a caller-supplied `topNav`. Fixed by gating the prop's inclusion in JSX directly on `isMobile`, confirmed live afterward: desktop screenshot at 1280px is now byte-identical to before this round (no phantom bar), `nav[aria-label="Mobile navigation"]` and the hamburger both confirmed absent from the DOM entirely at desktop width.
  **A second real bug caught the same way**: an explicit `<MobileNavToggle/>` was originally placed as `TopNav`'s own `startContent`, assuming a hamburger had to be authored manually. Live DOM inspection found TWO `[aria-label="Open navigation"]` buttons — `TopNav` auto-injects its OWN toggle in mobile-bar mode once it detects `sideNav` content via `AppShell`'s context, so the explicit one was a genuine duplicate (one real/laid-out, one inert/zero-size). Removed the explicit `MobileNavToggle` entirely — confirmed live afterward: exactly one real toggle in the DOM.
  **Verified live, full round trip, not assumed from the diff**: mobile screenshot (390×844) shows the requested "regular sticky with a hamburger and an M logo as the minimized navigation" exactly — logo left, hamburger right. Clicking the hamburger opens the native `<dialog>` drawer (screenshotted) showing the IDENTICAL nav items/order/icons as the desktop sidebar (New investigation, Discover, Watchlist, Holdings, Market Pulse, Portfolio, Risk, Data Sources, Recent, profile/notifications footer) — confirmed via DOM text extraction, not just a visual glance. Clicked "Discover" inside the drawer: drawer closed automatically AND the app genuinely navigated to the real Discover page (confirmed via `document.body.innerText` showing real content — "Notable movers," "Zenith Protocol," etc. — not a stub).
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no new token — reuses existing components/tokens throughout). `test:visual`: `shell.spec.ts` (the one spec exercising `Frame`/`shell-frame--default`, confirmed running at Playwright's default 1280×720 desktop viewport, well above the 768px mobile gate) re-run directly — 5/5, zero drift — before trusting the change didn't regress the one committed baseline that touches this file. Full suite run twice — 235/235 both times.

Discover and Entity Detail confirmed already responsive under Phase 18's own earlier tier-2 work — not touched, not re-verified beyond their normal desktop-width regression coverage this round.

- **Phase 20 WO-2/WO-3 — New Investigation page pane-floor removal + collapse re-enable, and chat/artifact mobile Dialogs (2026-08-21, same day as WO-1's shell chrome).** Continuing the mobile responsiveness order — this round covers the two remaining items flagged as follow-up work at the end of the shell-chrome round.
  **WO-2 — mobile pane floor + re-enabled collapse**: confirmed via AskUserQuestion before touching CSS — a real ~280px mobile floor (not zero, not the desktop 420/320px values), and auto-collapse of the least-recently-active pane (re-enabling the existing, currently-dormant `usePaneFitCollapse` mechanism) rather than horizontal scroll, when three panes still don't fit even at the mobile floor. New `MOBILE_PANE_FLOOR = 280` (paneFloors.ts) + a `@media (max-width: 768px)` block in `Frame.module.css` overriding `.contentColumn`/`.transcriptPane`/`.artifactPane`'s `min-width` down to that one shared value — same 768px number `Frame.tsx`'s own mobile-breakpoint check already uses, not a second independently-chosen threshold. `usePaneVisibility.ts`'s `usePaneFitCollapse(rowRef, collapseCandidates)` call restored verbatim from git history (the exact wiring disabled 2026-08-09) — its ORIGINAL disable reason (three different per-pane floors fighting each other) no longer applies now that every pane shares one floor below 768px, confirmed as the correct root-cause read before re-enabling rather than assumed safe. `.transcriptPane`'s own `max-width: 400px` left untouched — confirmed it can never actually bind on a real phone viewport (400 > any phone width), so it isn't a competing constraint needing reconciliation.
  **Verified live, not assumed from the diff**: submitted a real query at 390px width — `.contentColumn` correctly shrank to exactly the new 280px floor; the artifact pane, unable to fit alongside it, was auto-collapsed by the re-enabled mechanism into `CollapsedPaneChip`'s own existing 48px "labeled return point" (confirmed via DOM measurement, not a screenshot guess). Clicked "Restore": the pane genuinely reopened for one render tick (`[280, 280, 48]`, 3 children), then `usePaneFitCollapse`'s own `ResizeObserver`-driven `checkFit()` correctly detected the row still overflows at that width and re-collapsed it within ~1 second — the intended "the mechanism won't hold open something that genuinely doesn't fit, even on explicit user request" behavior, confirmed as real re-triggering, not a stuck state. `shell.spec.ts` (desktop-width, the one baseline exercising `Frame`/`usePaneVisibility`) re-run directly and confirmed zero drift both before and after re-enabling the collapse mechanism — three panes at their much larger DESKTOP floors never trip `isOverflowing` in a normal browser viewport, so the re-enabled observer is provably inert there, not just assumed to be.
  **WO-3 — chat/artifact mobile Dialogs**: Astryx's own `Dialog` (`variant="fullscreen"`) — the exact primitive named in the order, not a hand-rolled overlay, per CLAUDE.md's Astryx-first rule. On mobile, `ArtifactStackMount`/`TranscriptPaneMount` render inside a fullscreen `Dialog` + `DialogHeader` (title + close button) instead of inline in the flex row; on desktop, byte-identical to before (same components, same `show` prop, just placed in the row directly). `onOpenChange(false)` reuses the EXACT close actions the existing desktop pane controls already call — `closeStack()` (ArtifactStackControl's own action) and `toggleChatPane()` (ChatPaneControl's own action) — not a second close pathway invented for mobile. `collapsedPane`/`usePaneFitCollapse` never apply to the mobile modals: both mounts are excluded from `.row` entirely on mobile (confirmed by construction — the desktop-only render branch is gated on `!isMobile`), so they can never contribute to the row's own overflow measurement.
  **File-budget discipline**: `Frame.tsx` crossed 200 lines once the modal JSX landed (305 lines) — split into `MobilePaneModals.tsx` (the two Dialogs), `MobileTopNav.tsx` (the WO-1 top-bar composition, previously inline), and `WorkbenchRow.tsx` (the flex row + its mobile-modal sibling, extracted together since they're logically one "workbench body" unit) — `Frame.tsx` itself down to 189 lines, every new file under budget too (108/61/36 lines).
  **Verified live, full round trip on a real place page**: submitted a query — artifact modal opened as a genuine fullscreen `<dialog open>` with real scene content (confidence gauge, reasoning, recommendation, ranked candidates), confirmed via both DOM query and screenshot; clicked its close button — dialog count dropped to 0, confirmed via direct query, not inferred. Navigated to Discover via the WO-1 hamburger drawer, clicked the existing `ChatPaneControl` toggle — chat modal opened with the real `ChatComposer` inside, correctly responsive at mobile width with no extra work needed (confirmed the task's own framing: "the composer should just follow the mobile patterns" — Astryx's own composer already does, once the artificial pane-width constraint is gone).
  **Flagged, not silently glossed over**: the artifact modal's OWN internal scene content (side-by-side Reasoning/Recommendation cards, long titles) still shows some horizontal crowding at 390px in the screenshot evidence — `ArtifactStack`'s internal layout hasn't itself been adapted for mobile-narrow rendering; the modal WIRING (open/close/content-mounts-correctly) is confirmed working, but the scene content's own responsive behavior inside that modal is a separate, real follow-on item, not silently claimed as done here.
  **Gate**: `tsc` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (no new token — `MOBILE_PANE_FLOOR` is a structural layout constant, same posture as `PANE_FLOOR`/`CHAT_PANE_FLOOR` beside it). `test:visual`: `shell.spec.ts` re-run directly at multiple points during this round (before/after collapse re-enable, before/after modal extraction) — 5/5 every time. Full suite hit one single-test flake on ops-dark under heavy parallel load; re-ran in isolation and it passed cleanly (same established contention-flake pattern this session has hit repeatedly, not a real regression); two full subsequent runs both passed 235/235.
  **Remaining scope, restated honestly**: `ArtifactStack`'s own internal scene-content responsiveness at mobile widths (the side-by-side card crowding noted above) is not yet addressed — a real next item, not silently folded into "mobile modals done."

- **External architecture/styling audit + Tier 1–2 remediation (2026-08-28).** Read-only audit of `app/src/` against CLAUDE.md §2/§4/§7, followed by fixes to the unambiguous-defect subset only. Full report written to `audit/merlin-architecture-audit.md`.

  **Audit result, in brief**: the token cascade and the engine boundary are both clean — zero raw color values, theme parity holds across all 5 theme files (47 tokens), and §7's Astryx indirection rule is perfectly observed (zero components read a `--color-*`/`--spacing-N` cascade variable directly, so the "new theme = zero component changes" guarantee genuinely holds). Dependency direction, registry purity, component purity and dead-code checks all came back clean. Drift is concentrated in two places: rule 4's inline-style ban (127 occurrences, all token-sourced so no theme breaks — a structural violation, not a visual one) and the `min-width:0` overflow bug class (~20 unswept `.module.css` files).

  **Fixed this session (Tier 1 — real defects)**:
  - `SceneSummary.module.css` — `.confidenceMetricsRow` is the identical `1fr 1fr` shape as `.summaryRecommendationRow` four lines below it, which *was* container-query-guarded; the twin was missed when that one was fixed. Added the matching `@container` reflow + `min-width:0` on both column children. (`.summaryRecommendationRow`'s own children are `Panel`s, which already carry `min-width:0` internally — left alone deliberately.)
  - `KeyIssuesCard.module.css` — was guarding an artifact-pane grid with `@media (max-width:768px)`, i.e. a *viewport* query on a node whose width is set by the resizable pane. This is precisely what SceneSummary's own head comment documents as wrong. Converted to `@container (max-width:560px)`, added `min-width:0` to `.viewColumn`, and added `flex-wrap` to `.sourceRow` (matching AiRationaleRail's own identical chip-row mitigation).
  - `AnalystConsensus.module.css` — `repeat(4, 1fr)` of currency figures with no guard at all; added `container-type` on root, a 2-column `@container` reflow, `min-width:0` on `.targetCell`, and `flex-wrap`+`gap`+`min-width:0` on `.headerRow`/`.legendRow`.
  - `EntityAssetTable.tsx/.module.css` — was solving this same bug *inline* (`{width,minWidth,overflow,maxWidth}`), off the documented pattern. Moved to `.root` in its CSS module and added the `:global(.astryx-table-scroll-wrapper)` two-part override that `DataTable`/`Comparison` both document as required — plus `min-width:0` on the intermediate `.animatedTable`, which sits between the wrapper and the table and had no guard.
  - `engine/assetDiscoveryMock.ts` — removed the codebase's only `any` (rule 11). It was the standard `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` workaround: build a mutable object, then conditionally assign optional fields. Rebuilt as a single typed expression with conditional spreads, so omission-vs-`undefined` is preserved without suppression.

  **Fixed this session (Tier 2 — guards & budget)**:
  - `package.json` `lint:tokens` gained a second grep arm for `oklch(`/`oklab(`/`lab(`/`lch(`, scoped `--exclude-dir=theme`. The original regex only knew `#hex`/`rgb()`/`hsl()`, so it was blind to the very format the theme files use — a component author could have dropped a raw `oklch()` in and passed lint. **Verified by planting a violation and confirming a non-zero exit**, then confirming theme files still pass; not just observed passing.
  - `engine/mockAssetBuilder.ts` (new, 74 lines) — the `any` fix pushed `assetDiscoveryMock.ts` from 207 to 212 lines, worsening an existing budget violation, so the per-seed construction was split out per rule 12. `assetDiscoveryMock.ts` is now **154 lines, back under budget**. Behavior equivalence verified with a temporary test (no `undefined`-valued optional keys; bonds keep bond fields and correctly omit `price`), then removed.

  **Deliberately NOT done, flagged for a scoped work order**: the 127 inline `style` props. Fixing them means editing **59 component files and creating 44 new CSS modules** — squarely the kind of cross-cutting refactor rule 9 forbids outside its own phase, and it would invalidate all 248 visual baselines at once. Architect asked and confirmed the deferral. Note ~23 of the 127 are genuinely dynamic (`SceneRenderer.tsx:59`'s `--reveal-index` is load-bearing for the assembly effect itself) and need a ratified custom-property-bridge carve-out rather than deletion; two carry an independent second breach (raw magic numbers `maxWidth: 320` in `InvestigationsPage.tsx:116`, `width: 300` in `PaneFitCollapseHarness.tsx:90`).

  **Corrections to the audit's own first pass, on closer reading**: `AssetPriceHeader.module.css` was ranked MEDIUM risk but all three of its flex rows already carry `flex-wrap: wrap` — not a defect, left untouched. `TrendChart`/`EarningsHistoryChart` were flagged as unconfirmed Recharts inference; both are single-column grids whose header rows already wrap, so `ResponsiveContainer` shrinks correctly — also left untouched. Reported rather than quietly dropped, since the audit is the artifact of record.

  **Still open (pre-existing, untouched — reported not fixed, per "report, don't do")**: file budget. `EntitiesPage.tsx` **346** (STATE.md logged it at 304→301 as a known violation; it has since grown +45 lines unlogged), `TrendChart.tsx` 338, `chartCraft.tsx` 236 (STATE.md:1252 reasoned it was "well under budget" when code was moved in — no longer true), `tradableAsset.ts` 229, `Sidebar.tsx` 213 (last logged 136), plus `PageShell.module.css` 256 and `Frame.module.css` 219. Separately, all 5 theme files are over 200 lines (`theme.default.css` 438); rule 12 exempts only fixture JSON, so a token map is arguably a category the rule never contemplated — **wants an explicit architect ruling to exempt them** rather than standing as five permanent silent violations.

  **Gate**: `tsc -b app/tsconfig.json` clean, `tsc --noEmit -p mcp-server/tsconfig.json` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean (with the new arm), `test:visual` **235/235**. Baselines deliberately NOT regenerated — every CSS change takes effect only below its container-query threshold, so unchanged default-width baselines are the correct evidence that nothing regressed. One ops-dark `shell.spec.ts` flake under parallel load appeared mid-session; passed in isolation and on a full re-run — the same contention flake the previous entry documents, not a regression.

- **Inline-style extraction, safe subset only (2026-08-28, same session, follow-on).** Continued from the audit remediation above, taking only the low-risk slice of the deferred rule-4 work: files that ALREADY had a `.module.css` and whose inline styles were entirely static. No new-module-per-file sweep (that stays deferred — it's 44 new files).

  **Cleared six files completely** — `InvestigationsPage.tsx` (3, plus a new module; also the `maxWidth: 320` magic number, now a literal in a stylesheet, matching `ChatViewport.module.css`'s own precedent for a layout dimension), `Frame.tsx` (3), `ArtifactStack.tsx` (3), `DashboardSummaryHeader.tsx` (2), `EntityDiscoveryStrips.tsx` (1), `DiscoveryItemRow.tsx` (1). **127 → 113 inline styles.**

  **A real mistake, caught and reverted before it shipped.** The first pass "improved while extracting" — added `min-height:0`, `flex-wrap:wrap`, and `min-width:0` to several of the new classes where the inline originals had none. That is not an extraction, it's an unrequested behavior change riding inside a mechanical refactor, and `flex-wrap` on a `space-between` row genuinely alters layout. Reverted every added property so each new class is byte-equivalent to the inline object it replaced; also reverted a gratuitous `layout`→`styles` import rename in `Frame.tsx` (that stylesheet is shared — `ArtifactStackMount`/`TranscriptPaneMount`/`WorkbenchRow` all import it as `layout`). Same discipline applied retroactively to the earlier `AnalystConsensus` `.headerRow`/`.legendRow` fix, which had also gained `flex-wrap`+`gap` beyond what the overflow fix needed — trimmed to `min-width:0` alone. `KeyIssuesCard`'s `.sourceRow` `flex-wrap` was KEPT: that one is a genuine overflow fix (chips escaping their column), not incidental.

  **Verified NOT violations, left alone**: `PaneFitCollapseHarness.tsx`'s `width: 300` reads as a magic number but is load-bearing Storybook-only gate scaffolding — its own comment explains the value is deliberately below the 416px fit threshold to force a collapse. Tokenizing it would obscure its purpose. `ArtifactStackMount.tsx:60` was miscounted as static in the audit's first tally: its four animation properties are `isExiting` ternaries spanning lines, so it's genuinely dynamic and needs the class-toggle pattern, not extraction — deferred with the rest.

  **Also confirmed**: Astryx `Blockquote` and `Item` both accept and forward `className` (checked their `.d.ts`, and `Blockquote` sets no `font-family` of its own, so `.voiceFace` applies without a specificity fight) — verified before swapping, not assumed.

  **Gate**: `tsc -b app/tsconfig.json` clean, `vitest` 42/42, `lint:tokens` clean, `test:visual` **235/235, exit 0**. Baselines again NOT regenerated — pure extractions must be render-identical, so unchanged baselines are the proof. Two intermediate full runs failed (1 test, then 8) under parallel load and both passed cleanly on re-run with no code change between; the 8-failure run's diff image showed a whole-layout horizontal offset with doubled content, i.e. the trail animation caught at a different frame, not a styling delta. Third consecutive contention flake this session — `shell.spec.ts` and the renderer fixtures are the ones that trip. Worth treating as a known property of this suite under load rather than re-diagnosing each time.

- **Phase 21 WO-1 — inline scene rendering, architect legislation + order (2026-08-30).** New CLAUDE.md §3 law: a resolved query's output mounts either ARTIFACT (unchanged) or INLINE (new — renders directly in the turn's own transcript entry via `SceneRenderer`, never touching the artifact store). Same Scene contract, same registry, same renderer — `SceneRenderer.tsx` was already fully decoupled from its mount point and needed zero changes. Built exactly ONE scenario per the order's own instruction, to prove the mechanism before generalizing: "what are the hottest crypto this week" → inline text line + a 2-card grid of real crypto entities (Zenith Protocol, Solent Stablecoin — the only two universe entities actually tagged `crypto`; declined to pad the grid with `assetDiscoveryMock.ts`'s fictional filler, which has no real entity-detail data behind it and would need either a silent dead click or invented fixture data outside the app's own established "never fabricate" discipline — confirmed via AskUserQuestion).

  **Placement decision, ratified via AskUserQuestion**: `mount?: 'inline' | 'artifact'` lives on the scene's `manifest.json` entry (default `'artifact'`, every scene authored before this phase unaffected), not on the Scene/Zod contract — same place `module` already lives as per-scene metadata outside the Scene shape. Consequence, stated explicitly in both CLAUDE.md and here rather than silently assumed: `liveChannel.ts`'s MCP `push_scene` path has no manifest entry to read `mount` from, so a pushed scene always promotes to the artifact stack regardless of what an inline-shaped scene it might be — giving `push_scene` its own inline path is real, unscoped future work if ordered.

  **Card reuse, ratified via AskUserQuestion**: `EntityAssetGrid.tsx` (the Discover page's own card grid) is a shell component — engine imports (`MarketAsset`) and callback props (`onWatch`/`onOpenDetail`) that structurally cannot become a registry node without breaking rule 3 (no engine imports) and rule 7 (no callbacks) as written. Extracted the pure presentational card into `nodes/AssetCardVisual.tsx` (plain, engine-agnostic row shape — `formattedValue`/`deltaPercent`, no category enum, no click handler of its own), consumed by BOTH `EntityAssetGrid.tsx` (still callback-driven, unchanged behavior) and the new registry node `asset-card-grid` (`nodes/AssetCardGrid.tsx`, data-attribute click only, three-artifact rule 14 satisfied: component + `contracts/props/asset-card-grid.ts` + `AssetCardGrid.stories.tsx`). One card system, two consumers, zero rule violations — the order's own case-study point made structurally true, not just visually similar.

  **A real dependency-direction violation found and fixed mid-build, not shipped**: the first draft of `AssetCardVisual.tsx` imported `AssetTrendGlyph`/`AssetIdentity`/`TrendDelta` from `components/shell/` — legal for the shell's own `EntityAssetGrid.tsx` to do, but backwards for a file that must also be importable by a registry node under `components/nodes/` (rule 3: `contracts ← registry ← renderer ← engine ← app`, registry may never import from the app/shell layer). All four files (`AssetTrendGlyph`, `AssetIdentity`, `TrendDelta`, `AssetLogo` — confirmed via grep that none has an engine import or store access, so the move was clean) relocated from `shell/` to `nodes/`; every external importer (`DiscoveryItemRow.tsx`, both `EntityAssetTable*Columns.tsx`, `EntityAssetTableCells.tsx`, `EntityPaneTitle.tsx`) updated to the new path. Confirmed via precise import-line grep (not a substring match, which returned false positives from stale comments) that no consumer was missed.

  **New `entity-cards` DataSet kind** (`contracts/data.ts`), not an extension of `table`: table's cells are typed per-COLUMN, but every row here needs the identical fixed field set (id/name/type/value/deltaPercent) — forcing that through table's column/row shape would mean a hardcoded column layout invented per scene, exactly the coupling DataSet kinds exist to avoid. `value` arrives pre-formatted by the scene author (`"$4.95"` vs `"5.8%"`), matching `AssetCardVisual`'s own `formattedValue` prop — no currency/percent opinion baked into the node.

  **Turn shape change, with a real regression caught before shipping**: `Turn` gains `inlineScene?: HydratedScene`, `artifactRef` becomes optional, `settleTurn` accepts either (mutually exclusive, mirroring the scene's own mount decision). Grepping every `artifactRef` consumer (10 files) found two — `RecentSection.tsx`, `ThreadRow.tsx` — that read "no `artifactRef` yet" as "still thinking": an inline-settled turn has no `artifactRef` but is just as done as an artifact-mounted one, so left unfixed these would have shown a permanently-stuck "Thinking…" status dot for every inline result in Sidebar's Recent list and Investigations' thread rows. Fixed both to treat `artifactRef || inlineScene` as "settled." `sessionStore.ts`'s own `addTurn` interrupt-check had the identical bug (an in-flight turn was detected by `!existing.artifactRef` alone) — fixed the same way, in the one place that logic lives.

  **Entity-click delegation, deliberately routed differently from Canvas.tsx's own**: `Transcript.tsx` gained its own `data-entity-id` delegated listener (same `resolveClickedEntityId` primitive `Canvas.tsx`/`DashboardPage.tsx` already share) — routed to `openEntityDetail`, matching `DashboardPage.tsx`'s "browse first" semantics, NOT `Canvas.tsx`'s `submitQuery`-based investigation routing. Correct per the order's own three-way split: an inline, browse-shaped result is "go look at this entity," never "investigate this as a new query" — those are different registers by design, not an oversight that happened to pick the wrong one.

  **Fixture**: `scenes/hottest-crypto-week.scene.json` (module `discover`, `mount: "inline"`, 7 intents, 5 thinking steps in the plan→search→retrieve→synthesize→verify arc per `merlin-scene-authoring`) — real prices/deltas pulled from the actual `*-price-volume-90d` datasets (`$4.95`/`+41.03%` Zenith, `$1.02`/`+1.81%` Solent), not invented. One authoring mistake caught by `tsc`/schema validation before landing: an invented `voice` prop on `text-block` that doesn't exist in `TextBlockPropsSchema` — removed.

  **Live-verified, full gate, not assumed from the diff**: submitted "what are the hottest crypto this week" on Home's landing composer via a real Playwright session against Storybook's `shell-frame--default` story — trail streamed (5 steps, correct labels/sources), text line + 2-card grid rendered directly in the transcript with **zero artifact card/pane created** (confirmed by DOM absence, not just a visual glance), "Hottest Crypto This Week" appeared correctly in Sidebar Recent with an "Answered" status dot (confirming the `RecentSection.tsx` fix). Clicked the Zenith Protocol card: navigated to its real Entity Detail page (`ZNTH` symbol confirmed in DOM) while the original transcript text remained present in the DOM simultaneously (two-pane law holds — `page === 'home'`'s content column never hides the transcript on an artifact/entity-detail navigation, confirmed structurally in `Frame.tsx`/`WorkbenchRow.tsx` before relying on it, not assumed). Typed a genuine bucket-2 follow-up ("earnings last 5 years") from the open Entity Detail page afterward — no crash, no console error, resolver honestly returns no match (bucket 2's own scene fixture was not authored this WO, per its own explicit scope: build ONE scenario). `asset-card-grid` node spot-checked in Storybook across default/ops-dark/glass — identical rendering, no theme-specific breakage.

  **Bucket 2 (single-entity facet inline, e.g. "earnings last 5 years" while an entity is open) — flagged as a fast WO-2, not built**: the INFRASTRUCTURE is fully reusable as-is (`mount: "inline"` manifest field, `Turn.inlineScene`, `Transcript.tsx`'s render branch + click delegation, `presentScene.ts`'s branch) — nothing there is scoped to WO-1's specific scenario. What bucket 2 needs beyond that is real, additional authoring work, not "trivial": a new single-node scene fixture scoped to "whatever entity is currently open" (the resolver has no concept of "currently open entity" today — `pageStore.selectedEntityId` would need to inform intent matching or scene selection, which is new plumbing, not just a new fixture), reusing an existing node (`EarningsHistoryChart` was named in the order as the likely fit). Per the order's own escape hatch, this is reported rather than built unscoped.

  **Gate**: `tsc` clean (both `app/tsconfig.json` and the root), `lint:tokens` clean, `vitest` **45/45** (42 existing + 3 new — the scene-corpus contract tests pick up the new fixture automatically), `test:visual` **235/235** at `--workers=4` (the documented 8-worker `shell.spec.ts` contention flake from prior sessions avoided by capping workers, not re-litigated). New `AssetCardGrid` node has no `test:visual` baseline yet (a new node's first Storybook pass — no prior baseline to regress against); live-verified instead per the gate condition's own wording ("cards match Discover's exact visual treatment," confirmed pixel-identical card chrome across both consumers). 3 themes confirmed both via the live query-flow screenshot (default) and the node's own Storybook story (default/ops-dark/glass).
